\displaystyle=(-1)^{r\operatorname{par}(b)}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b)h_{r}^{(n)}\otimes 1=(-1)^{r\operatorname{par}(b)}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b)\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}e_{s}^{(\ell)}\otimes 1

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r\operatorname{par}(b)}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b)\operatorname{sh}_{n}\Big((-1)^{r}e_{r}^{(n^{\prime})}\Big)\otimes 1=0.

The second equality here is just the observation that

e^{(\ell)}_{s}\otimes 1
is zero in
OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}\mathbb{F}
for
s>0
.
The penultimate equality
follows from
lemma

6.10
(1).

Now consider
\bar{s}^{(n)}_{\lambda}\in\overline{OH}^{\ell}_{n}
.
If
{\operatorname{ht}}(\lambda)>n
, we already know this is zero by
theorem

6.3
.
We have by
eq.

6.1
that
\bar{s}^{(n)}_{\lambda}=\bar{h}^{(n)}_{\lambda}+(
a linear combination of
\bar{h}^{(n)}_{\mu}

for
\mu>\lambda
).
We deduce that
\bar{s}^{(n)}_{\lambda}=0
if
\lambda_{1}>n^{\prime}

since
\bar{h}^{(n)}_{\lambda}
and all of the
\bar{h}^{(n)}_{\mu}
appearing in this expansion are zero by the defining relations of
\overline{OH}^{\ell}_{n}
.
This shows that
\overline{OH}^{\ell}_{n}
is spanned by the elements

\bar{s}^{(n)}_{\lambda}\>\big(\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{n\times n^{\prime}}}\big)
.
To see that these elements are linearly independent, hence, a basis
for
\overline{OH}^{\ell}_{n}
, we act on the vector
1\otimes 1\in OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}\mathbb{F}
to obtain
the vectors

s^{(n)}_{\lambda}\otimes 1\>(\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{n\times n^{\prime}}})

which constitute a basis for

OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}\mathbb{F}
by
theorem

6.12
(1).
This argument also shows that the map (1) is an isomorphism.

(2)
Similarly,
to construct the bottom map, we must make

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
into a graded right

\overline{OH}_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
-supermodule
so that
(1\otimes b)\cdot\bar{a}=(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(a)}1\otimes b\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a)

for
b\in OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
and
a\in OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}}
.
To do this,
one first applies
*
to
theorem

6.12
to deduce that
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
is
spanned by vectors of the form
1\otimes a
for
a\in OS\!ym_{n}
.
This plus
lemma

6.10
(2)
are then used
establish the well-definedness of the action.
The fact that the bottom map
is an isomorphism could be deduced using
theorem

6.12
(2) like in the previous paragraph,
but it also follows
once we have checked the commutativity of the diagram using that the other three maps (1), (3) and (4) are all isomorphisms.

(3)
To obtain the map (3), we start with
the isomorphism
OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})},a\mapsto a^{*}
where
*
here is the restriction of the
superalgebra anti-involution
*:O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{\ell}\rightarrow O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{\ell}
.
Since we have that
(ab)^{*}=(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(b)}b^{*}a^{*}
for any

b\in OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
and
a\in OS\!ym_{\ell}

with
a^{*}\in OS\!ym_{\ell}
again,
this induces the desired isomorphism
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}\mathbb{F}
.

(4)
By definition,
\overline{OH}^{\ell}_{n}
is the quotient of
OS\!ym

by the two-sided ideal generated by

\{e_{r}\>|\>r>n\}\cup\{h_{r}\>|\>r>n^{\prime}\}

and
\overline{OH}^{\ell}_{n^{\prime}}
is the quotient of
OS\!ym

by the two-sided ideal generated by

\{h_{r}\>|\>r>n\}\cup\{e_{r}\>|\>r>n^{\prime}\}
.
The involution
\psi\circ\operatorname{p}^{n}

interchanges these two ideals
so it factors through the quotients to induce an isomorphism
\overline{\psi}_{n}^{\ell}:\overline{OH}^{\ell}_{n}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\overline{OH}^{\ell}_{n^{\prime}}
. This gives the graded superalgebra isomorphism (4).

To complete the proof, it just remains to show that the diagram commutes.
Consider
\bar{h}_{r_{1}}^{(n)}\cdots\bar{h}_{r_{k}}^{(n)}\in\overline{OH}^{\ell}_{n}

for
r_{1},\dots,r_{k}>0
and
k\geq 0
.
The map (1) takes it to

h_{r_{1}}^{(n)}\cdots h_{r_{k}}^{(n)}\otimes 1
.
As in the opening paragraph of the proof, we have that

fh_{r}^{(n)}\otimes 1=(-1)^{r}f\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(e_{r}^{(n^{\prime})}\big)\otimes 1=(-1)^{r+\operatorname{par}(f)r}\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(e_{r}^{(n^{\prime})}\big)f\otimes 1

for any
r
and
f\in OS\!ym_{n}
.
By induction, it follows that

h_{r_{1}}^{(n)}\cdots h_{r_{k}}^{(n)}\otimes 1=(-1)^{r_{1}+\cdots+r_{k}+\sum_{i<j}r_{i}r_{j}}\operatorname{sh}_{n}\Big(e^{(n^{\prime})}_{r_{k}}\cdots e^{(n^{\prime})}_{r_{1}}\Big)\otimes 1=(-1)^{r_{1}+\cdots+r_{k}}\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(e^{(n^{\prime})}_{r_{1}}\cdots e^{(n^{\prime})}_{r_{k}}\big)^{*}\otimes 1.

This is the same as the image of
\bar{h}_{r_{1}}^{(n)}\cdots\bar{h}_{r_{k}}^{(n)}
going around the other three sides of the square.
∎

Corollary 7.3
.

For
\ell=n+n^{\prime}
,

\overline{OH}_{n}^{\ell}
is of graded superdimension

q^{nn^{\prime}}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{\ell}{n}_{q,\pi}
.

Proof.

This follows from the basis described in
theorem

7.1
plus
corollary

3.2
.
∎

Corollary 7.4
.

In
OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}\mathbb{F}
,
we have that

\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(\sigma_{\mu}^{(n^{\prime})}\big)\otimes 1=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\mu)}s_{\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}\otimes 1

for every
\mu\in{\Lambda^{\!+}}
.

Proof.

Note that
(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\mu)}s_{\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}\otimes 1
is the image of

(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\mu)}\bar{s}_{\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}
under the top map in the commuting square
eq.

7.2
. Now we compute the image of
(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\mu)}\bar{s}_{\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}
around the other three edges of this square.
Using that

\overline{NE}(\mu)=|\mu|+dN(\mu)+dE(\mu)+NE(\mu)
,
it maps first to

(-1)^{dN(\mu)+dE(\mu)+n|\mu|}\bar{s}_{\mu}^{(n^{\prime})}

thanks to
eq.

6.4
, then to

(-1)^{dN(\mu)+dE(\mu)}1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(s_{\mu}^{(n^{\prime})}\big)
,
then to

\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(\sigma_{\mu}^{(n^{\prime})}\big)\otimes 1

thanks to
eq.

6.8
.
∎

Corollary 7.5
.

For
\ell=n+n^{\prime}
,
there is a unique (up to scalars)
trace map

\overline{\tr}:\overline{OH}_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow\mathbb{F}
making

\overline{OH}^{\ell}_{n}
into a graded Frobenius superalgebra over
\mathbb{F}
of degree

2nn^{\prime}
and parity
nn^{\prime}\pmod{2}
.
Moreover, normalizing
\overline{\tr}
so that

\overline{\tr}\big(\bar{s}_{({n^{\prime}}^{n})}^{(n)}\big)=1

and recalling the definition of
\Tr
from
theorem

6.12
,
we have that

\Tr(a)\otimes 1=1\otimes\overline{\tr}(\bar{a})

in
OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}\mathbb{F}

for any
a\in OS\!ym_{n}
.

Proof.

If it exists, the trace map is unique up to a non-zero scalar; cf. the discussion after
eq.

2.10
.
Now we
define

\overline{\tr}:\overline{OH}_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow\mathbb{F}
so that

\Tr(a)\otimes 1=1\otimes\overline{\tr}(\bar{a})
and check that is a trace
map sending
\bar{s}_{({n^{\prime}}^{n})}^{(n)}
to
1
. The latter statement follows because
we know in
eq.

6.29
that
\operatorname{sgn}(\varnothing)=1
.
To show that
\overline{\tr}
is a trace map,
we need to show that there exist elements

b_{1},\dots,b_{m},b_{1}^{\vee},\dots,b_{m}^{\vee}\in\overline{OH}_{n}^{\ell}

satisfying
eqs.

2.9
and
2.10
.
We take
b^{\vee}_{1},\dots,b^{\vee}_{m}

and
b_{1},\dots,b_{m}
to be the elements

\bar{s}_{\lambda}^{(n)}
and

(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\mu)}\operatorname{sgn}(\mu)\bar{s}_{\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}

for
\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{n\times n^{\prime}}}
and
\mu\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{n^{\prime}\times n}}
,
respectively,
enumerated
so that

b^{\vee}_{r}=\bar{s}_{\lambda}^{(n)}

and
b_{r}=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\mu)}\operatorname{sgn}(\mu)\bar{s}_{\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}

if and only if
\mu^{\mathtt{t}}_{i}=n^{\prime}-\lambda_{n+1-i}
for each
i=1,\dots,n
.
The properties
eq.

2.9
obviously hold,
and
eq.

2.10
follows from
corollary

7.4
and
eq.

6.29
.
∎

Corollary 7.6
.

Let
\lambda,\mu\in{\Lambda^{\!+}}
be related as in the first case of
eq.

6.29
.
Then
\operatorname{sgn}(\mu)=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\mu)}LR_{\lambda,\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}^{\nu}

where
\nu:=({n^{\prime}}^{n})
and

LR_{\lambda,\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}^{\nu}
denotes the odd Littlewood-Richardson coefficient,
that is, the coefficient of
s_{\nu}
when

s_{\lambda}s_{\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}
is expanded in terms of odd Schur functions.

Proof.

By the previous two corollaries and the definition
eq.

6.29
,
we have that

\operatorname{sgn}(\mu)=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\mu)}\overline{\tr}\Big(\bar{s}_{\lambda}^{(n)}\bar{s}^{(n)}_{\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}\Big)=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\mu)}LR_{\lambda,\mu^{\mathtt{t}}}^{\nu}.

∎

Some very special odd Littlewood-Richardson coefficients arise in the odd analog of the
Pieri formula

proved in
[
EKL
, (2.72)]
:

(7.3)

s_{\lambda}h_{r}=\sum_{\mu}(-1)^{NE(\lambda)+NE(\mu)+S(\lambda,\mu)}s_{\mu}.

The sum here is over all partitions
\mu
whose Young diagram is obtained by adding one box to the bottom of
r

different columns of the Young diagram of
\lambda
, and

S(\lambda,\mu):=\sum_{1\leq j\leq r}\sum_{k=i_{j}+1}^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{k}^{\mathtt{t}}

assuming these columns are indexed by
i_{1}<\cdots<i_{r}
.
The ghastly signs appearing in
eq.

7.3
and in the next lemma fortunately play no significant role.

Lemma 7.7
.

The inclusion
OS\!ym_{n}\hookrightarrow OS\!ym_{(n-1,1)}

maps

s^{(n)}_{\mu}\mapsto\sum_{r,\lambda}(-1)^{NE(\lambda)+dE(\lambda)+NE(\mu)+dE(\mu)+S(\lambda,\mu)+\binom{r}{2}}s^{(n-1)}_{\lambda}x_{n}^{r}

where the sum is over all
r\geq 0

and partitions
\lambda
whose Young diagram is obtained by removing one box from the bottom of

r
different columns of the Young diagram of
\mu
,
including all boxes from its
n
th row since

s^{(n-1)}_{\lambda}=0
if
\lambda_{n}>0
.

Proof.

From
eq.

6.13
, it follows that
the coefficient of
s^{(n-1)}_{\lambda}x_{n}^{r}

when
s^{(n)}_{\mu}
is expanded in terms of the Schur basis
for
OS\!ym_{(n-1,1)}

is equal to the
s_{\lambda}\otimes h_{r}
-coefficient of

\Delta^{+}(s_{\mu})
.
Using
corollaries

6.2
and
6.6
, this is

(-1)^{dE(\lambda)+\binom{r}{2}}(s_{\lambda}\otimes h_{r},\Delta^{+}(s_{\mu}))^{+}\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}(-1)^{dE(\lambda)+\binom{r}{2}}(s_{\lambda}h_{r},s_{\mu})^{+}.

Now use
eq.

7.3
plus
corollary

6.2
once again.
∎

Remark 7.8
.

A general formula for odd Littlewood-Richardson coefficients
is derived in
[
E
, Th. 4.8]
, showing that they can be computed
by counting the same set of
semi-standard skew tableaux that appear in the ordinary Littlewood-Richardson rule, but counting each one with a sign
\pm
.
A useful consequence of this is that if an ordinary even Littlewood-Richardson coefficient is zero then so is the corresponding odd Littlewood-Richardson coefficient. This, together with the odd Pieri rule, is all that we actually use below.

8.
Equivariant odd Grassmannian cohomology algebras

Recall from
corollary

4.13
that

R_{\ell}
denotes
the largest supercommutative quotient of
OS\!ym_{\ell}
.
We are now going to work over this as our base ring.
We use the notation
\dot{c}
to denote the image of
c\in OS\!ym_{\ell}

in
R_{\ell}
.
Note that
OS\!ym_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}
is a graded
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra
with structure map
\eta:R_{\ell}\rightarrow OS\!ym_{n}\otimes R_{\ell},\dot{c}\mapsto 1\otimes\dot{c}
.

Definition 8.1
.

For
\ell=n+n^{\prime}
, the

equivariant odd Grassmannian cohomology algebra

is
the graded
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra

(8.1)

\displaystyle OH_{n}^{\ell}

\displaystyle:=OS\!ym_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}\>\Bigg/\>\Bigg\langle\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}\>\Bigg|\>r>n^{\prime}\Bigg\rangle.

For
a\in OS\!ym_{n}

and
c\in OS\!ym_{\ell}
, we denote the canonical image
of
a\otimes\dot{c}\in OS\!ym_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}

in the quotient
OH_{n}^{\ell}
by
a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}
.

As the name suggests, this is
an odd analog of the
GL_{\ell}(\mathbb{C})
-equivariant cohomology algebra
of the Grassmannian of
n
-dimensional subspaces of
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}
.
Given any graded
supercommutative
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra

A
, one can specialize to obtain
the graded
A
-superalgebra
OH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{R_{\ell}}A
.
In particular, the ordinary
odd Grassmannian cohomology algebra
\overline{OH}_{n}^{\ell}
from
eq.

7.1
is naturally identified with the specialization
OH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{R_{\ell}}\mathbb{F}
.

Example 8.2
.

We have that

OH_{1}^{2}\cong O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{2}\,\big/\,\langle x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2},x_{1}^{3}+x_{1}^{2}x_{2}\rangle

via the isomorphism
h_{r}^{(1)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\mapsto x_{1}^{r}
,

1{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{e}_{1}^{(2)}\mapsto x_{1}+x_{2}
,

1{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{e}_{2}^{(2)}\mapsto x_{1}x_{2}
.
A linear basis
is given by the elements
\{x_{1}^{r},x_{2},x_{1}x_{2}\>|\>r\geq 0\}
.

Lemma 8.3
.

For
\ell=n+n^{\prime}
, there is a surjective graded superalgebra homomorphism

\alpha_{n}^{\ell}:OH_{n}^{\ell}\twoheadrightarrow R_{n^{\prime}}
taking

a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1
to zero for
a\in OS\!ym_{n}
of positive degree
and
1{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}^{(\ell)}
to
\dot{c}^{(n^{\prime})}
for
c\in OS\!ym
.

Proof.

This is clear from the nature of the defining relations
eq.

8.1
.
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

The two-sided ideal

\Big\langle\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}\>\Big|\>r>n^{\prime}\Big\rangle

of
OS\!ym_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}
contains
the elements
\sum_{s=0}^{r}h_{r-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}

for all
r>n^{\prime}+1
.
Also the two-sided ideal

\Big\langle\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{(r-n^{\prime})s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}\>\Big|\>r>n^{\prime}\Big\rangle

contains
the elements
\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{(r-n^{\prime})s+s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}

for all
r>n^{\prime}+1
.
Hence, these two ideals are equal.

Proof.

By
section

4
, we have that
h_{1}h_{r}=h_{r}h_{1}

if
r
is odd and
h_{1}h_{r}=-h_{r}h_{1}+2h_{r+1}
if
r
is even.
Suppose that
r>n^{\prime}+1
.
The ideal contains

a:=\sum_{s=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{s}h_{r-1-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}
.
Hence, it contains

(-1)^{r}(h_{1}\otimes 1)a-a(h_{1}\otimes 1)=\sum_{s=0}^{r}(1-(-1)^{r-s})h_{r-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}.

Adding this to
\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{r-s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}
, which is also in the ideal, gives the claimed elements
for the first assertion of the lemma.
The second assertion is proved similarly.
To deduce that these ideals are
the same, the facts established so far show that both are generated by the lowest degree generator

\sum_{s=0}^{n^{\prime}+1}(-1)^{s}h_{n^{\prime}+1-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}

together with the higher degree generators

\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}

and

\sum_{s=0}^{r}h_{r-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}

for all
r>n^{\prime}+1
.
∎

For any
\alpha\in\Lambda(k,\ell)
,

OS\!ym_{\alpha}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}

is a graded left
OS\!ym_{\alpha}
-supermodule, and it is a graded

R_{\ell}
-supermodule for the left action that is induced by the natural right action. Thus,
for
a\in OS\!ym_{\alpha}
and

c\in OS\!ym_{\ell}
, we have that

(8.2)

ac\otimes 1=a\otimes\dot{c}=(a\otimes 1)\cdot\dot{c}=(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(c)}\dot{c}\cdot(a\otimes 1).

However this is in general
not
equal to
ca\otimes 1
.
Note also that
OS\!ym_{\alpha}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}

is not itself a graded superalgebra in any apparent way.
Indeed,
R_{\ell}
is the quotient of
OS\!ym_{\ell}
by
the two-sided ideal
I_{\ell}
generated by
\big(o^{(\ell)}\big)^{2}

and
\big[o^{(\ell)},e_{2}^{(\ell)}\big]
,
so

OS\!ym_{\alpha}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}=OS\!ym_{\alpha}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}OS\!ym_{\ell}/I_{\ell}\simeq OS\!ym_{\alpha}/OS\!ym_{\alpha}I_{\ell}.

However, in general,

OS\!ym_{\alpha}I_{\ell}
is merely a left ideal, not
a two-sided ideal of
OS\!ym_{\alpha}
.
Similar remarks apply to
R_{\ell}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}OS\!ym_{\alpha}
,
which is a graded right
OS\!ym_{\alpha}
-supermodule, and a graded

R_{\ell}
-supermodule for the right action that
is induced by the natural left action.

Theorem 8.5
.

For
\ell=n+n^{\prime}
,

OH_{n}^{\ell}
is free as a graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule with basis
given by the odd Schur polynomials

s_{\lambda}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1
for

\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{n\times n^{\prime}}}
.
Moreover,
there is a commuting diagram

(8.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH^{\ell}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH^{\ell}_{n^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\ell}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}\mapsto a\otimes\dot{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{n}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}\mapsto(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)(n+\operatorname{par}(c))}\dot{c}\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{c}\otimes a\mapsto(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(c)}a^{*}\otimes\dot{c}}

of isomorphisms in which

(1)

the top map is an even degree 0 isomorphism
of graded
(OS\!ym_{n},R_{\ell})
-superbimodules;

(2)

the bottom map is an even degree 0
isomorphism
of graded
(R_{\ell},OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}})
-superbimodules
for the right action of
OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}}
on
R_{\ell}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
defined by restricting
the natural right action of
OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
along

\operatorname{sh}_{n}\circ\operatorname{p}^{n}:OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}}\rightarrow OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
;

(3)

the right hand map is an even degree 0
graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule isomorphism;

(4)

the left hand map
\psi_{n}^{\ell}

is a graded
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra isomorphism
such that

(8.4)

\displaystyle\psi_{n}^{\ell}\big(a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}a_{i}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}_{i}

for
a\in OS\!ym_{n}

such that

a=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(a_{i})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a_{i})^{*}c_{i}

for

a_{i}\in OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}},c_{i}\in OS\!ym_{\ell}
.

Proof.

(1)
To construct the top map,
we must show that
OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OSym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}
can be
made into a graded left
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule so that

a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}\cdot(b\otimes 1)=(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(c)\operatorname{par}(b)}ab\otimes\dot{c}

for
a\in OS\!ym_{n},b\in OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
and
c\in OS\!ym_{\ell}
.
To see this is well defined, we know by

theorem

6.12
(2) that
OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}

is generated as a
R_{\ell}
-supermodule
by vectors of the form
\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b)\otimes 1

for
b\in OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}}
, so it suffices to check that

\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}

acts as zero on
\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b)\otimes 1
for
b\in OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}}

and
r>n^{\prime}
.
This follows by

lemma

6.10
(1):

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}\cdot(\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b)\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s+s\operatorname{par}(b)}h_{r-s}^{(n)}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b){\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r\operatorname{par}(b)}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b)\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}e_{s}^{(\ell)}\otimes 1=0.

Thus, we have defined the top map.

Next, we show that the elements
\big\{s^{(n)}_{\lambda}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\>\big|\>\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{n\times n^{\prime}}}\big\}

generate
OH^{\ell}_{n}
as a graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule.
This is not quite as easy as before since it is no longer the case that

s^{(n)}_{\lambda}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1=0
in
OH^{\ell}_{n}
when
\lambda_{1}>n
.
Instead, one shows by induction on
|\lambda|

that any
s^{(n)}_{\lambda}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1
for

\lambda
with
\lambda_{1}>n
can be written as an
R_{\ell}
-linear
combination of other
s^{(n)}_{\mu}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1
for

\mu
with
\mu_{1}\leq n
and
|\mu|<|\lambda|
.

Now the proof of the first part of the theorem can be completed.
The spanning set for
OH_{n}^{\ell}

just constructed
is also linearly independent
because it becomes the basis for
OSym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}
arising from
theorem

6.12
(1) when we act on the vector
1\otimes 1
.
This also shows that the top map (1) is an isomorphism.

(2)
We need to make

R_{\ell}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}

into a graded right
OH_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
-supermodule so that

(1\otimes b)\cdot a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}=(-1)^{(\operatorname{par}(a)+\operatorname{par}(b))\operatorname{par}(c)+n\operatorname{par}(a)}\dot{c}\otimes b\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a)

for
a\in OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}}
,

b\in OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
and

c\in OS\!ym_{\ell}
.
To check that this action is well defined,
we know from
lemma

8.4
that the ideal defining

OH_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
is generated by the elements

\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{(r-n)s}h_{r-s}^{(n^{\prime})}\otimes\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}

for
r>n
.
It suffices to check that the image of each such element
in
OH_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
acts as zero on

1\otimes b
for
b\in OS\!ym_{n}
.
This follows by

lemma

6.10
(2):

\displaystyle(1\otimes b)\cdot\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{(r-n)s}h_{r-s}^{(n^{\prime})}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{rn}\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s+s\operatorname{par}(b)}\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}\otimes b\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(h_{r-s}^{(n^{\prime})}\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r(\operatorname{par}(b)+n)}1\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}e_{s}^{(\ell)}\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(h_{r-s}^{(n^{\prime})}\big)b=0.

Applying
*
to the basis from
theorem

6.12
(2), we deduce that
R_{\ell}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
is a free graded

R_{\ell}
-supermodule with basis
\Big\{1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n}(s^{(n^{\prime})}_{\mu})\>\Big|\>\mu\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{n^{\prime}\times n}}\Big\}
. Also
the elements
\big\{s^{(n^{\prime})}_{\mu}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\>\big|\>\mu\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{n^{\prime}\times n}}\big\}
span
OH_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
as in the previous paragraph. Acting on
1\otimes 1

shows finally that these elements form a basis
for
OH_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
and that the bottom map is an isomorphism.

(3)
To construct the right hand map, we
start with the map

R_{\ell}\otimes OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\rightarrow OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell},\qquad\dot{c}\otimes a\mapsto(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(c)}a^{*}\otimes\dot{c}

for
c\in OS\!ym_{\ell},a\in OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
.
Using that
R_{\ell}
is supercommutative,
this is easily checked to be a graded

R_{\ell}
-supermodule homomorphism.
Also this map is balanced. To see this, we need to show that the images of

\dot{c}_{1}\dot{c}_{2}\otimes a
and
\dot{c}_{1}\otimes c_{2}a
are the same
for
a\in OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
and
c_{1},c_{2}\in OS\!ym_{\ell}
.
Note that the superalgebra anti-involution
*
of
OS\!ym_{\ell}

descends to a superalgebra anti-involution
*
of
R_{\ell}
.
Since
R_{\ell}
is supercommutative and each of its generators

\dot{e}_{r}^{(\ell)}\>(r\geq 1)
is fixed by
*
, this is induced
anti-involution is equal to the identity.
So
\dot{c}_{2}=\dot{c}_{2}^{*}
, and
the image of
\dot{c}_{1}\dot{c}_{2}\otimes a
is

\displaystyle(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)(\operatorname{par}(c_{1})+\operatorname{par}(c_{2}))}a^{*}\otimes\dot{c}_{1}\dot{c}_{2}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(c_{1})+\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(c_{2})+\operatorname{par}(c_{1})\operatorname{par}(c_{2})}a^{*}\otimes\dot{c}_{2}^{*}\dot{c}_{1}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(c_{1})+\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(c_{2})+\operatorname{par}(c_{1})\operatorname{par}(c_{2})}a^{*}c_{2}^{*}\otimes\dot{c}_{1}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(\operatorname{par}(a)+\operatorname{par}(c_{2}))\operatorname{par}(c_{1})}(c_{2}a)^{*}\otimes\dot{c}_{1}

which is equal to the image of

\dot{c}_{1}\otimes c_{2}a
.
So this map induces the required
graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule isomorphism.

(4)
We define
\psi_{n}^{\ell}
to be the unique
graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule isomorphism making the diagram commute.
If
a=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(a_{i})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a_{i})^{*}c_{i}
for

a_{i}\in OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}},c_{i}\in OS\!ym_{\ell}

then the image of
a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1
under the top map is
equal to
\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(a_{i})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a_{i})^{*}\otimes\dot{c}_{i}
.
It follows that
\psi_{n}^{\ell}(a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1)=\sum_{i=1}^{p}a_{i}\otimes\dot{c}_{i}

because the latter expression also maps to

\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(a_{i})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a_{i})^{*}\otimes\dot{c}_{i}

when the bottom map followed by the right hand map is applied.

We still need to show that
\psi_{n}^{\ell}
is actually a graded

R_{\ell}
-superalgebra isomorphism.
For this, we take
a,b\in OS\!ym_{n}

such that
a=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(a_{i})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a_{i})^{*}c_{i}

and
b=\sum_{j=1}^{q}(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(b_{j})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b_{j})^{*}d_{j}

for
a_{i},b_{j}\in OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}}
and
c_{i},d_{j}\in OS\!ym_{\ell}
.
We have that

\displaystyle ab

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{q}(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(b_{j})}a\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b_{j})^{*}d_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}(-1)^{(\operatorname{par}(a)+n)\operatorname{par}(b_{j})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b_{j})^{*}ad_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{j=1}^{q}(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(a_{i})+(\operatorname{par}(a_{i})+\operatorname{par}(c_{i})+n)\operatorname{par}(b_{j})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b_{j})^{*}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a_{i})^{*}c_{i}d_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{j=1}^{q}(-1)^{n(\operatorname{par}(a_{i})+\operatorname{par}(b_{j}))+\operatorname{par}(c_{i})\operatorname{par}(b_{j})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a_{i}b_{j})^{*}c_{i}d_{j}.

So

\psi_{n}^{\ell}(ab)=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{j=1}^{q}(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(c_{i})\operatorname{par}(b_{j})}a_{i}b_{j}\otimes\dot{c}_{i}\dot{d}_{j}=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{p}a_{i}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}_{i}\Big)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{q}b_{j}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{d}_{j}\Big)=\psi_{n}^{\ell}(a)\psi_{n}^{\ell}(b).

∎

Corollary 8.6
.

For
\ell=n+n^{\prime}
,

OH_{n}^{\ell}
is
a free graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule
of graded rank

q^{nn^{\prime}}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{\ell}{n}_{q,\pi}
.

Proof.

This follows by the basis theorem that is the first assertion of

theorem

8.5
plus
corollary

3.2
.
∎

Corollary 8.7
.

For
\ell=n+n^{\prime}
, there is a unique (up to scalars)
trace map

\tr:OH_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow R_{\ell}
making

OH^{\ell}_{n}
into a graded Frobenius superalgebra over

R_{\ell}
of degree

2nn^{\prime}
and parity
nn^{\prime}\pmod{2}
.
Moreover, normalizing
\tr
so that

\tr\big(s_{({n^{\prime}}^{n})}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\big)=1

and recalling the definition of
\Tr
from
theorem

6.12
,
we have that

\Tr(a)\otimes 1=1\otimes\tr(a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1)

in
OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}

for any
a\in OS\!ym_{n}
.

Proof.

The uniqueness follows from the general principles discussed after

eq.

2.10
since
\big(OH_{n}^{\ell}\big)_{0,\bar{0}}=\mathbb{F}
.
For existence, define
\tr:OH_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow R_{\ell}

to be the unique graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule homomorphism
such that

\Tr(a)\otimes 1=1\otimes\tr(a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1)

for
a\in OS\!ym_{n}
.
Noting that
\binom{n}{2}+\binom{n^{\prime}}{2}-\binom{\ell}{2}=-nn^{\prime}
,
the definition of
\Tr
implies that
this is a homogeneous linear map
of degree
-2nn^{\prime}
and parity
nn^{\prime}\pmod{2}
such that

\tr\big(s_{({n^{\prime}}^{n})}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\big)=1
.
It remains to check
eqs.

2.9
and
2.10
.
We take
b^{\vee}_{1},\dots,b_{m}^{\vee},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\in OH_{n}^{\ell}

to be the elements

s_{\lambda}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

and

\big(\psi_{n}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}\Big((-1)^{dN(\mu)+dE(\mu)+n|\mu|}\operatorname{sgn}(\mu)s_{\mu}^{(n^{\prime})}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\Big)

for
\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{n\times n^{\prime}}}
and
\mu\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{n^{\prime}\times n}}
,
respectively,
enumerated in such a way that

b^{\vee}_{r}=s_{\lambda}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

and
\psi_{n}^{\ell}(b_{r})=(-1)^{dN(\mu)+dE(\mu)+n|\mu|}\operatorname{sgn}(\mu)s_{\mu}^{(n^{\prime})}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

if and only if
\mu^{\mathtt{t}}_{i}=n^{\prime}-\lambda_{n+1-i}
for each
i=1,\dots,n
.
By
eq.

6.8
and the commutativity of the diagram
eq.

8.3
,
the top horizontal map in the diagram takes

b^{\vee}_{r}
to
s_{\lambda}^{(n)}\otimes 1

and
b_{r}
to

(-1)^{dN(\mu)+dE(\mu)}\operatorname{sgn}(\mu)\operatorname{sh}_{n}\Big(s_{\mu}^{(n^{\prime})}\Big)^{*}\otimes 1=\operatorname{sgn}(\mu)\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(\sigma_{\mu}^{(n^{\prime})}\big)\otimes 1
.
Then
eq.

2.10
follows using
eq.

6.29
.
∎

The next lemma investigates the
graded
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra isomorphism
\psi_{n}^{\ell}:OH_{n}^{\ell}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}OH_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
constructed in
theorem

8.5
(4).

Lemma 8.8
.

For
\ell=n+n^{\prime}
, the isomorphism

\psi_{n}^{\ell}:OH_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow OH_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}

maps

(8.5)

\displaystyle h^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{(n+1)(r-s)}e_{r-s}^{(n^{\prime})}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{h}_{s}^{(\ell)},

\displaystyle e^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{(n+r)(r-s)}h_{r-s}^{(n^{\prime})}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}

for
r\geq 0
.
The inverse isomorphism

\big(\psi_{n}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}:OH_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}\rightarrow OH_{n}^{\ell}

maps

(8.6)

\displaystyle h^{(n^{\prime})}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{(n+r)(r-s)+ns}e_{r-s}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{h}_{s}^{(\ell)},

\displaystyle e^{(n^{\prime})}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{(n+1)(r-s)+ns}h_{r-s}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}

for
r\geq 0
.

Proof.

We first observe that

h^{(n)}(t)=\operatorname{sh}_{n}\Big(e^{(n^{\prime})}\big(t\big)\Big)h^{(\ell)}(t)
and

\varepsilon^{(n)}(t)=\operatorname{sh}_{n}\Big(\eta^{(n^{\prime})}\big(t\big)\Big)\varepsilon^{(\ell)}(t)

in
OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime})}
.
This follows from
eq.

4.57

using the identities

h^{(\ell)}(t)=\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(h^{(n^{\prime})}(t)\big)h^{(n)}(t)

and

\varepsilon^{(\ell)}(t)=\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(\varepsilon^{(n^{\prime})}(t)\big)\varepsilon^{(n)}(t)
.
Hence, using
eq.

8.4
, we deduce that
\psi_{n}^{\ell}
maps

\displaystyle h^{(n)}(t){\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{nn^{\prime}}e^{(n^{\prime})}\big((-1)^{n}t\big)^{*}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{h}^{(\ell)}(t),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{(n)}(t){\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{nn^{\prime}}\eta^{(n^{\prime})}\big((-1)^{n}t\big)^{*}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{\varepsilon}^{(\ell)}(t).

The first formula in
eq.

8.5
follows from the first of these identities
on equating
t^{-n-r}
-coefficients,
remembering that
e^{(n^{\prime})}_{r}
is fixed by
*
.
Similarly, equating
t^{n-r}
-coefficients in the second identity
gives that

\psi_{n}^{\ell}\Big(\varepsilon^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\Big)=\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{(n+1)(r-s)}\big(\eta_{r-s}^{(n^{\prime})}\big)^{*}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{\varepsilon}_{s}^{(\ell)}.

Replacing
\varepsilon^{(n)}_{r}
with
(-1)^{\binom{r}{2}}e^{(n)}_{r}
,

\big(\eta^{(n^{\prime})}_{r-s}\big)^{*}

with
(-1)^{\binom{r-s}{2}}h^{(n^{\prime})}_{r-s}

and
\dot{\varepsilon}_{s}^{(\ell)}
with
(-1)^{\binom{s}{2}}\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}

using
eqs.

4.48
and
4.49

gives the second formula in
eq.

8.5
.
The formulae in
eq.

8.6
are easily deduced from
eq.

8.5
together
with the infinite Grassmannian relation in
R_{\ell}
.
∎

The following composition defines a graded
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra automorphism:

(8.7)

\delta_{n}^{\ell}:=\psi_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}\circ\psi_{n}^{\ell}:OH_{n}^{\ell}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}OH_{n}^{\ell}.

Using
lemma

8.8
, this can be described explicitly on generators, as follows.

Corollary 8.9
.

The automorphism
\delta_{n}^{\ell}

maps

(8.8)

\displaystyle h^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{\ell r}h^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1+(-1)^{\ell(r-1)}\big(1+(-1)^{n+r}\big)h_{r-1}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{o}^{(\ell)},

(8.9)

\displaystyle e^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{\ell r}e^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1+(-1)^{(\ell+1)(r-1)}\big(1+(-1)^{n+r}\big)e_{r-1}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{o}^{(\ell)},

(8.10)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{\ell r}\varepsilon^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1+(-1)^{\ell(r-1)}\big(1+(-1)^{n+r}\big)\varepsilon_{r-1}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{o}^{(\ell)},

(8.11)

\displaystyle\eta^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{\ell r}\eta^{(n)}_{r}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1+(-1)^{(\ell+1)(r-1)}\big(1+(-1)^{n+r}\big)\eta_{r-1}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}

for any
r\geq 1
.
In particular,
\delta_{n}^{\ell}\big(o^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\big)=(-1)^{\ell}o^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1+\big(1-(-1)^{n}\big)1{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}
.

Proof.

We first prove
eq.

8.8
.
Using
eq.

8.5
, we compute

(\psi_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}\circ\psi_{n}^{\ell})\big(h_{r}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\big)
:
it equals

\sum_{p=0}^{r}\sum_{q=0}^{r-p}(-1)^{(n+1)(r-p)+(n^{\prime}+r-p)(r-p-q)}h_{r-p-q}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{e}_{q}^{(\ell)}\dot{h}_{p}^{(\ell)}=\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{\ell r+(n^{\prime}+r)s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\bigg(\sum_{t=0}^{s}(-1)^{(n+r-s+1)t}\dot{e}_{s-t}^{(\ell)}\dot{h}_{t}^{(\ell)}\bigg).

Now we claim that the expression in the brackets here is equal to

\delta_{s,0}
if
n+r-s+1
is odd,
and it is equal to
\delta_{s,0}+2\delta_{s,1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}

is
n+r-s+1
is even.
The formula
eq.

8.8
is easily deduced using this claim.
To prove the claim, it follows immediately for odd
n+r-s+1
by the
infinite
Grassmannian relation. When
n+r-s+1
is even, it follows from the relation

(8.12)

\sum_{t=0}^{s}\dot{e}_{s-t}\dot{h}_{t}=\delta_{s,0}+2\delta_{s,1}\dot{o}

in
R
, which is easily checked in the cases
s=0
and
s=1

and follows for
s\geq 2
using
eqs.

4.67
and
4.68
.

The proof of
eq.

8.9
is a similar calculation.

Then
eq.

8.10
follows easily
since
\varepsilon_{r}^{(n)}=(-1)^{\binom{r}{2}}e_{r}^{(n)}
and

\varepsilon_{r-1}^{(n)}=(-1)^{\binom{r}{2}+r+1}e_{r-1}^{(n)}
.

Finally, to deduce
eq.

8.11
, we first write
eq.

8.10
in terms
of generating functions:

(8.13)

\delta_{n}^{\ell}\big(\varepsilon^{(n)}(t){\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\big)=(-1)^{\ell n}\varepsilon^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big){\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1-(-1)^{\ell n}t^{-1}\Big[\varepsilon^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big)-\varepsilon^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+1}t\big)\Big]{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}.

This can be checked by equating coefficients of
t^{n-r}
on both
sides.
Then we formally invert both sides of
eq.

8.13
using
eq.

4.57
to
obtain the identity

(8.14)

\delta_{n}^{\ell}\big(\eta^{(n)}(t){\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\big)=(-1)^{\ell n}\eta^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big){\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1+(-1)^{(\ell-1)n}t^{-1}\Big[\eta^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+1}t\big)-\eta^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big)\Big]{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}.

To see that the right hand of this is indeed the inverse of the right
hand side of
eq.

8.13
, one just has to multiply together
using
\big(\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\big)^{2}=0

then simplify the result to see that it equals 1, which is straightforward.
Finally, we equate coefficients of
t^{-n-r}
on both sides of

eq.

8.14
to obtain
eq.

8.11
.
∎

9.
Deformed odd cyclotomic nil-Hecke algebras

The
odd cyclotomic nil-Hecke algebra

\overline{ONH}_{n}^{\ell}
is the quotient of
ONH_{n}
by the two-sided ideal generated by the element
x_{1}^{\ell}
.
This algebra was introduced originally in
[
EKL
, Sec. 5]
.
In particular, in
[
EKL
, Prop. 5.2]
, it is shown that

\overline{ONH}_{n}^{\ell}
is zero unless
0\leq n\leq\ell
, in which case it
is isomorphic to the graded matrix superalgebra

M_{q^{n/2}[n]^{!}_{q,\pi}}\big(\overline{OH}_{n}^{\ell}\big)
,
notation as explained after
remark

5.6
.

Definition 9.1
.

The
deformed odd cyclotomic nil-Hecke algebra
is the quotient algebra

(9.1)

ONH_{n}^{\ell}:=ONH_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}\>\Bigg/\>\left\langle\sum_{r=0}^{\ell}(-1)^{r}x_{1}^{\ell-r}\otimes\dot{e}_{r}^{(\ell)}\right\rangle

for
n>0
.
We also let
ONH_{0}^{\ell}:=ONH_{0}\otimes R_{\ell}\cong R_{\ell}

so that
ONH_{n}^{\ell}
makes sense for all
n\geq 0
.
We denote the image of
a\otimes\dot{c}
in
ONH_{n}^{\ell}

by
a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}
.

The graded
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra
ONH_{n}^{\ell}
is
the odd analog of the algebras
defined for
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
in
[
R1
, Sec. 5.2.1]

and for other Cartan types in
[
R2
, Sec. 4.4.1]
;
our definition
eq.

9.1
looks more like the latter formulation.

Theorem 9.2
.

The deformed odd cyclotomic nil-Hecke algebra
ONH^{\ell}_{n}
is zero unless

0\leq n\leq\ell
. Assuming
0\leq n\leq\ell
,
the natural left action of
ONH_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}
on
O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n}}OH_{n}^{\ell}

factors through the quotient
ONH_{n}^{\ell}

to make
O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n}}OH_{n}^{\ell}
into a
graded
(ONH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell})
-superbimodule.
The associated representation

\rho:ONH_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow\End_{{\text{-}}OH_{n}^{\ell}}\left(O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n}}OH_{n}^{\ell}\right)

is an isomorphism of graded superalgebras.
Moreover,

O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n}}OH_{n}^{\ell}

is free as a graded right
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule
with basis
\big\{x_{n}^{\kappa_{n}}\cdots x_{1}^{\kappa_{1}}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\>\big|\>\kappa\in K_{n}\}
where

K_{n}:=\big\{\kappa=(\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\>\big|\>0\leq\kappa_{i}\leq n-i\text{ for }i=1,\dots,n\big\}.

theorem

9.2
shows that

ONH_{n}^{\ell}
is isomorphic to the graded matrix superalgebra
M_{q^{\binom{n}{2}}[n]^{!}_{\!q,\pi}}\Big(OH_{n}^{\ell}\Big)
.
We will prove the theorem later in the section,
ultimately deducing it from
theorem

5.4
which showed that

ONH_{n}
is isomorphic to

M_{q^{\binom{n}{2}}[n]^{!}_{\!q,\pi}}\Big(OS\!ym_{n}\Big)
.
First, we state some corollaries.
The first is the analog of
corollary

5.10
for
ONH_{n}^{\ell}
.

Corollary 9.3
.

The element
(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n}:=(\omega\xi)_{n}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1

is a primitive idempotent in
ONH_{n}^{\ell}
.
Moreover, the maps
\imath
and
\jmath
from
lemma

5.9

induce an isomorphism

OH_{n}^{\ell}\cong(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n}ONH_{n}^{\ell}(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n}
,
of graded superalgebras and an isomorphism

O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n}}OH_{n}^{\ell}\simeq ONH_{n}^{\ell}(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n}
of graded
(ONH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell})
-superbimodules. Making these identifications, the idempotent truncation functor

(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n}-:ONH^{\ell}_{n}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}\rightarrow OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}

is an equivalence of graded
(Q,\Pi)
-supercategories.

Corollary 9.4
.

For
0\leq n\leq\ell
,

ONH_{n}^{\ell}
is a free graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule of graded rank

q^{n(\ell-n)}[\ell]^{!}_{q,\pi}\overline{[n]^{!}}_{\!q,\pi}\big/[\ell-n]^{!}_{q,\pi}.

Proof.

This follows using the final part of the theorem and
corollary

8.6
.
∎

Corollary 9.5
.

For
0\leq n\leq\ell
, the monomials

\big\{x^{\kappa}\tau_{w}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\>\big|\>w\in S\!_{n},\kappa\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\text{ with }0\leq\kappa_{i}\leq\ell-i\text{ for }i=1,\dots,n\big\}

form a basis for
ONH_{n}^{\ell}
as a free
R_{\ell}
-supermodule.

Proof.

The free graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule
with basis given by elements of the same degrees and parities as these monomials
is graded rank

q^{n(\ell-n)}[\ell]^{!}_{q,\pi}\overline{[n]}^{!}_{q,\pi}/[\ell-n]^{!}_{q,\pi}
, which is the same as the graded rank
of
ONH_{n}^{\ell}
according to
corollary

9.4
.
Therefore it suffices to show that the monomials

\big\{x^{\kappa}\tau_{w}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\>\big|\>w\in S\!_{n},\kappa\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\text{ with }0\leq\kappa_{i}\leq\ell-i\text{ for }i=1,\dots,n\big\}

are linearly independent over
R_{\ell}
.

We first prove this linear independence in the special case that
n=\ell
,
in which case we have simply that
OH_{\ell}^{\ell}=R_{\ell}
.
Suppose we have a linear relation

\sum_{w,\kappa}x^{\kappa}\tau_{w}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}_{w,\kappa}=0

in
ONH_{\ell}^{\ell}

for
\dot{c}_{w,\kappa}\in OS\!ym_{\ell}
that are not all zero,
summing over
w\in S_{\ell},\kappa\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}

with
0\leq\kappa_{i}\leq\ell-i
for all
i=1,\dots,\ell
.
Pick
w
of minimal length such that
\dot{c}_{w,\kappa}\neq 0
for some
\kappa
.
Then we act on the vector

p_{w}^{(\ell)}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1\in O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{\ell}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}
to deduce as in the proof of
theorem

5.2

that
\sum_{\kappa}(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(c_{w,\kappa})\ell(w)}x^{\kappa}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}_{w,\kappa}=0
.
The elements
x^{\kappa}{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1
for

\kappa\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}

with
0\leq\kappa_{i}\leq\ell-i
for all
i=1,\dots,\ell

are linearly independent over
R_{\ell}
thanks to
remark

5.6
.
So this implies that
\dot{c}_{w,\kappa}=0
for all
\kappa
, which is a contradiction.

Now we treat the general case.
The inclusion
ONH_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}\hookrightarrow ONH_{\ell}\otimes R_{\ell}

induces an
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra homomorphism

\imath:ONH^{\ell}_{n}\rightarrow ONH^{\ell}_{\ell}
.
The monomials in
ONH^{\ell}_{n}
which we are trying to show are linearly independent map to a subset of the monomials shown to be linearly independent in the previous paragraph. This completes the proof (and also shows that

\imath
is injective).
∎

Corollary 9.6
.

For
0\leq n<\ell
,
the graded superalgebra homomorphism
ONH_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}

induced by the natural embedding

ONH_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}\hookrightarrow ONH_{n+1}\otimes R_{\ell}
is injective.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the basis theorem in the previous corollary.
∎

Corollary 9.7
.

The graded superalgebra

ONH_{n}^{\ell}
is a graded Frobenius superalgebra over
R_{\ell}
of degree

2n(\ell-n)
and parity
n(\ell-n)\pmod{2}
.

Proof.

This follows from
theorem

9.2
and
corollary

8.7
.
∎

Remark 9.8
.

Since

\overline{ONH}_{n}^{\ell}\cong ONH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{R_{\ell}}\mathbb{F}

and
\overline{OH}_{n}^{\ell}\cong OH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{R_{\ell}}\mathbb{F}
,

corollary

9.4
implies that
\overline{ONH}_{n}^{\ell}

is isomorphic to the graded matrix superalgebra
M_{q^{\binom{n}{2}}[n]^{!}_{q,\pi}}\Big(\overline{OH}_{n}^{\ell}\Big)
,
recovering
[
EKL
, Prop. 5.2]
.
Also
corollary

9.5
implies that
\overline{ONH}_{n}^{\ell}

has basis
given by the canonical images of the monomials

\big\{x^{\kappa}\tau_{w}\>\big|\>w\in S\!_{n},\kappa\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\text{ with }0\leq\kappa_{i}\leq\ell-i\text{ for }i=1,\dots,n\big\},

recovering
[
HS
, Th. 4.10]
. The proof that these monomials span given
in
[
HS
]
gives an explicit algorithm to “straighten” arbitrary monomials into this form.

In the remainder of the section, we prove
theorem

9.2
.
The approach is based on the proof of
[
EKL
, Prop. 5.2]
(the result which we are generalizing).
First we record some preliminary lemmas.

Lemma 9.9
.

Suppose that
n\geq 1
.
Let
y
be a non-zero homogeneous element of
\operatorname{sh}_{1}(O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n-1})

and consider the (free) right
OS\!ym_{n}
-submodule of
O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n}
with
basis

v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
defined from

v_{i}:=yx_{1}^{i-1}
.
The matrix of the endomorphism of this subspace defined by the left action of
(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(y)}x_{1}
is
equal to the (non-commutative) companion matrix

(9.2)

\begin{pmatrix}0&0&\cdots&0&(-1)^{n-1}e^{(n)}_{n}\\
1&0&\cdots&0&(-1)^{n-2}e^{(n)}_{n-1}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&1&0&-e^{(n)}_{2}\\
0&\cdots&0&1&e^{(n)}_{1}\end{pmatrix}

of the polynomial
(t-x_{1})\cdots(t-x_{n})\in O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n}[t]
.

Proof.

We have that
(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(y)}x_{1}yx_{1}^{i-1}=yx_{1}^{i}
.
This gives all but the last column of the matrix already.
For the last column, use
lemma

6.10
(1), taking
n
and
n^{\prime}

there to be
1
and
n-1
in the current setup,
noting that
h^{(1)}_{r-s}=x_{1}^{r-s}
.
∎

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
C
be the
n\times n
companion matrix from
eq.

9.2
.
For any
1\leq i,j\leq n
and
k\geq 0
, the

(i,j)
-entry of
C^{k}
is equal to

(9.3)

c_{i,j;k}:=\sum_{t=0}^{\min(k+j-i,n-i)}(-1)^{t}e_{t}^{(n)}h_{k+j-i-t}^{(n)},

which is zero if
k<i-j
and
1
if
k=i-j
.

Proof.

This goes by induction on
k=0,1,\dots
. When
k=0
, we have that

c_{i,j;k}=\sum_{t=0}^{j-i}(-1)^{t}e_{t}^{(n)}h_{j-i-t}^{(n)}

which is zero if
i>j
as it is the empty sum,
and it is
\delta_{i,j}
if
i\leq j
by the infinite Grassmannian relation.
This checks the induction base. Then we take
k\geq 0
and consider
the
(i,j)
-entry of

C^{k+1}=CC^{k}
.
Since
C
has at most two non-zero entries in its
i
th row,
namely, its
(i,i-1)
-entry
1
if
i>1
and
its
(i,n)
-entry
(-1)^{n-i}e_{n+1-i}^{(n)}
,
we get by induction that
the
(i,j)
-entry of
C^{k+1}
is equal to

c_{i-1,j;k}+(-1)^{n-i}e_{n+1-i}^{(n)}c_{n,j;k}
where the first term should be omitted in the case that
i=1
.
This is equal to

\sum_{t=0}^{\min(k+1+j-i,n+1-i)}(-1)^{t}e_{t}^{(n)}h_{k+1+j-i-t}^{(n)}+(-1)^{n-i}e_{n+1-i}^{(n)}h_{k+j-n}^{(n)},

interpreting
h_{k+j-n}^{(n)}
as zero if
k<n-j
and
noting in the case that
i=1
that the first term here is zero by the infinite Grassmannian relation (so there
is no need to omit it).
To complete the proof, we need to show that

\sum_{t=0}^{\min(k+1+j-i,n-i)}(-1)^{t}e_{t}^{(n)}h_{k+1+j-i-t}^{(n)}=\sum_{t=0}^{\min(k+1+j-i,n+1-i)}(-1)^{t}e_{t}^{(n)}h_{k+1+j-i-t}^{(n)}+(-1)^{n-i}e_{n+1-i}^{(n)}h_{k+j-n}^{(n)}.

If
k+1+j-i\leq n-i
both sums are over
0\leq t\leq k+1+j-i
and the second term on the right hand side is zero by convention since
k+j-n<0
, so the equality is true.
If
k+1+j-i>n-i
then the sum on the right hand side has one extra term
when
t=n+1-i
compared to the sum on the left hand side. But this extra term
is
(-1)^{n+1-i}e_{n+1-i}^{(n)}h_{k+j-n}^{(n)}
which cancels with the final term on the right hand side.
∎

Proof of
theorem

9.2
.

Let
A:=ONH_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}
,

B:=OS\!ym_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}
and

V:=O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}
,
which is a graded
(A,B)
-superbimodule.
By
remark

5.6
,
V
is free as a graded right
B
-supermodule
with basis
\big\{x_{n}^{\kappa_{n}}\cdots x_{1}^{\kappa_{1}}\otimes 1\>\big|\>\kappa\in K_{n}\big\}
.
Also, by
theorem

5.4
,
A\cong\End_{{\text{-}}B}(V)
so that

A
can be identified with the graded matrix superalgebra
consisting of matrices
(a_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}})_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\in K_{n}}
,
for
a_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}\in B
, this matrix representing the endomorphism

x^{\kappa^{\prime}}\otimes 1\mapsto\sum_{\kappa\in K_{n}}(x^{\kappa}\otimes 1)a_{\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}}
.
In this situation, Morita theory implies that
there are bijections between the sets of graded superideals of
A
,
graded sub-superbimodules of
V

and graded superideals of
B

so that
I\leftrightarrow IV=VJ\leftrightarrow J
.
For
I\unlhd A
corresponding to
J\unlhd B
in this way,
a set of generators for
J
is given by the matrix entries of a set of generators of
I
, and
we have that
A/I\cong\End_{{\text{-}}B/J}(V/IV)
.
Thus, to prove the theorem, we
start from the two-sided ideal
I
of
A
from
eq.

9.1
,
which may be described equivalently as the two-sided ideal generated
by the elements

\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}x_{1}^{r-s}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}\>(r\geq\ell)
.
We must show that the two-sided ideal
J
of
B
generated
by the matrix entries of the generators of
I
is equal to

B
if
n>\ell
and it is equal to the two-sided ideal
of
B
from
eq.

8.1
if
n\leq\ell
.

Consider the matrix associated to the generator

\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}x_{1}^{r-s}\otimes\dot{e}^{(\ell)}_{s}
of
I

for
r\geq\ell
.

lemma

9.9

implies that it is a block diagonal matrix with
n\times n
blocks
parametrized by all
\kappa\in K_{n}
with
\kappa_{1}=0
,
the
(i,j)
-entry of this block being the
x_{n}^{\kappa_{n}}\cdots x_{2}^{\kappa_{2}}x_{1}^{i-1}\otimes 1
-coefficient
of
\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}x_{1}^{r-s}\otimes\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}\cdot(x_{n}^{\kappa_{n}}\cdots x_{2}^{\kappa_{2}}x_{1}^{j-1}\otimes 1)
for
1\leq i,j\leq n
.
Denote this matrix entry by
f_{i,j;r}^{(\kappa)}
.
Using
lemmas

9.9
and
9.10
,
we have that

(9.4)

f_{i,j;r}^{(\kappa)}=(-1)^{r|\kappa|}\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{sj}\sum_{t=0}^{\min(r+j-i-s,n-i)}(-1)^{t}e_{t}^{(n)}h_{r+j-i-s-t}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}.

Apart from the leading sign which is irrelevant for the problem in hand,
this does not depend on
\kappa
, so we may as well assume
from now on that
\kappa=\mathbf{0}=(0,\dots,0)
.
If
n>\ell
, we take
r=\ell,j=1
and
i=\ell+1
, in which case
the summations collapse and we deduce that
f_{\ell+1,1;\ell}^{(\mathbf{0})}=1\in J
.
So
J=B
as required in this case.

Now assume that
n\leq\ell
and set
n^{\prime}:=\ell-n
.
It remains to show that
the elements

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle:=\left\{f_{i,j;r}^{(\mathbf{0})}\>\Big|\>1\leq i,j\leq n,r\geq\ell\right\},

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle:=\left\{\sum_{s=0}^{r}(-1)^{s}h_{r-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}\>\Bigg|\>r>n^{\prime}\right\}

generate the same two-sided ideal of
B
.
We switch the summations in
eq.

9.4
to deduce that

f_{i,j;r}^{(\mathbf{0})}=\sum_{t=0}^{\min(r+j-i,n-i)}(-1)^{t}e_{t}^{(n)}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{\min(r,r+j-i-t)}(-1)^{sj}h_{r+j-i-t-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}\right).

We have that
r+j-i\geq\ell+1-i>n-i
so the first summation is over

0\leq t\leq n-i
.
Since
\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}=0
for
s>\ell

we can change the second summation so that it
is over
0\leq s\leq r+j-i-t
.
Taking
i=n
and
j=1
gives us the elements

\sum_{s=0}^{r-n+1}(-1)^{s}h_{r-n+1-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}

for all
r\geq\ell
. Since we have all
r
so that
r-n+1>n^{\prime}
,
these already give us all of the elements of
G
, demonstrating one containment.
It remains to show that all
f_{i,j;r}^{(\mathbf{0})}

for
1\leq i,j\leq n
and
r\geq\ell
also lie in
\langle G\rangle
.
In fact, given that
t\leq n-i
, we have that

\sum_{s=0}^{r+j-i-t}(-1)^{sj}h_{r+j-i-t-s}^{(n)}\otimes\dot{e}_{s}^{(\ell)}\in\langle G\rangle

because

r+j-i-t\geq n^{\prime}+j
,
the sign is
(-1)^{s}
if
j\geq 1
is odd or
1
if
j\geq 2
is
even,
and we know all of these elements lie in
\langle G\rangle
by
lemma

8.4
.
∎

10.
The 2-category

\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}

of odd Grassmannian bimodules

Throughout the section,
\ell
is fixed.
We will work always over the ground ring
R_{\ell}
,
but note that everything in this section
also makes sense
on base change from
R_{\ell}
to any graded
supercommutative
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra, including the most important case when the ground ring is the field
\mathbb{F}
.
For
0\leq n\leq\ell
, we denote the element of
OH_{n}^{\ell}

formerly denoted
by
a{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}1
simply by
\bar{a}\>\>(a\in OS\!ym_{n})
, and identify

R_{\ell}
with a subalgebra of
OH_{n}^{\ell}
via the embedding

R_{\ell}\hookrightarrow Z(OH_{n}^{\ell}),\dot{c}\mapsto 1{\,\bar{\otimes}\,}\dot{c}\>(c\in OS\!ym_{\ell})
.

Suppose that
n,d,n^{\prime}\geq 0
with
n+d+n^{\prime}=\ell
and

\alpha\in\Lambda(k,d)
.
The cases
\alpha=(d)
and
\alpha=(1^{d})

will be particularly important.
Let

(10.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}

\displaystyle:=OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell},

\displaystyle U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}

\displaystyle:=R_{\ell}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}OS\!ym_{(n^{\prime},\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n)}.

These are graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodules, with the action of
R_{\ell}
on

\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}
coming from the natural right action
and the action of
R_{\ell}

on
U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}
coming from the natural left action.
We refer to
\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}
and
U^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}
as
odd Grassmannian bimodules
.
According to the following lemma, they are graded superbimodules over equivariant odd Grassmannian cohomology algebras.

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
\ell=n+d+n^{\prime}
and
\alpha\in\Lambda(k,d)
be fixed as above.

(1)

There is a unique way to make
\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}
into a
graded
\big(OH^{\ell}_{n},OH_{n+d}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule
so that the left action of
\bar{a}\>\>(a\in OS\!ym_{n})

is defined by
\bar{a}(b\otimes 1):=ab\otimes 1

for
b\in OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n^{\prime})}
,
and the right action of

\bar{a}\>\>(a\in OS\!ym_{n+d})

is defined by
(b\otimes 1)\bar{a}:=ba\otimes 1

for
b\in OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k})}
.
Moreover:

(a)

\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}

has basis
\Big\{{\gamma}_{n+d}\big(p_{w}^{(n+d)}\big)\otimes 1\>\Big|\>w\in[S\!_{n+d}/S\!_{(\alpha_{k},\dots,\alpha_{1},n)}]_{\min}\Big\}

as a right
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
-supermodule;

(b)

\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}
has basis

\Big\{\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(p_{w}^{(n^{\prime}+d)}\big)\otimes 1\>\Big|\>w\in[S\!_{n^{\prime}+d}/S\!_{(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n^{\prime})}]_{\min}\Big\}

as a left
OH^{\ell}_{n}
-supermodule.

(2)

There is a unique way to make
U^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}
into a graded

\big(OH_{n+d}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule
so that the right action of
\bar{a}\>\>(a\in OS\!ym_{n})

is defined by
(1\otimes b)\bar{a}:=(-1)^{(n^{\prime}+d)\operatorname{par}(a)}1\otimes b\operatorname{sh}_{n^{\prime}+d}(a)

for
b\in OS\!ym_{(n^{\prime},\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n)}
,
and the left action of
\bar{a}\>\>(a\in OS\!ym_{n+d})
is defined by

\bar{a}(1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n^{\prime}}(b)):=(-1)^{n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(a)}1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n^{\prime}}(ab)

for
b\in OS\!ym_{(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n)}
.
Moreover:

(a)

U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}

has basis

\Big\{1\otimes{\gamma}_{n^{\prime}+d}\big(p_{w}^{(n^{\prime}+d)}\big)^{*}\>\Big|\>w\in[S\!_{n^{\prime}+d}/S\!_{(\alpha_{k},\dots,\alpha_{1},n^{\prime})}]_{\min}\Big\}

as a right
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule;

(b)

U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}

has basis

\Big\{1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n^{\prime}}\big(p_{w}^{(n+d)}\big)^{*}\>\Big|\>w\in[S\!_{n+d}/S\!_{(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n)}]_{\min}\Big\}

as a left
OH^{\ell}_{n+d}
-supermodule.

Proof.

(1)
By
theorem

6.8
,
OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n^{\prime})}

is generated by the elements of
OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k})}

as a right
OS\!ym_{\ell}
-supermodule.
Hence,
\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}
is generated as an
R_{\ell}
-supermodule
by elements of the form
b\otimes 1

for
b\in OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k})}
.
In view of this, provided that it is well defined, there is a unique
way to make
\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}
into a graded right
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
-supermodule
such that
(b\otimes 1)\bar{a}=ba\otimes 1

for all
b\in OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k})}
and
a\in OS\!ym_{n+d}
.
It is also clear that the
right action of
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
defined in this way
and the left action of
OH_{n}^{\ell}
from the statement of the lemma commute with each other, again assuming that both actions are well defined.

To see that the right action is well defined,
we have that

\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}\simeq OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{(n+d,n^{\prime})}}OS\!ym_{(n+d,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}.

By
corollary

6.9
(2), we deduce that

\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}=\bigoplus_{w\in\big[S\!_{n+d}/S\!_{(\alpha_{k},\dots,\alpha_{1},n)}\big]_{\min}}{\gamma}_{n+d}\big(p_{w}^{(n+d)}\big)\otimes\big(OS\!ym_{(n+d,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}\big)

with each summand being a
copy of
OS\!ym_{(n+d,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}

shifted in degree and parity. Hence,
each of these subspaces is isomorphic to

OH_{n+d}^{\ell}

via the isomorphism from
theorem

8.5
(1).
The right action of
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}

we are defining
is just the natural right action of
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}

on itself transported through these isomorphisms.
So it is well defined.
We have also proved (1a).

For the left action,
we have that

\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}\simeq OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime}+d)}}OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime}+d)}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}.

By
corollary

6.9
(1), we deduce that

\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}=\bigoplus_{w\in\big[S\!_{n^{\prime}+d}/S\!_{(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n^{\prime})}\big]_{\min}}\operatorname{sh}_{n}\Big(p_{w}^{(n^{\prime}+d)}\Big)\otimes\big(OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime}+d)}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}\big)

with each summand being
a graded left
OS\!ym_{n}\otimes R_{\ell}
-submodule isomorphic to
OS\!ym_{(n,n^{\prime}+d)}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}

(shifted in parity and degree).
It remains to apply
theorem

8.5
(1) to see that the left action of

OH_{n}^{\ell}
is well defined. This also establishes (1b).

(2)
This is similar to the proof of (1), using
the isomorphism from
theorem

8.5
(2) in place of the one from
theorem

8.5
(1), and the left supermodule analogs of

theorems

6.8
and
6.9
obtained by
applying
{\gamma}_{\ell}\circ*
to those assertions.
∎

More often than not, we will work with a degree- and parity-shifted version of
\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}

and, very occasionally, of
U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}
.
These have been chosen to ensure that the adjunctions in

theorems

11.3
and
11.5
below are even of degree 0, and
to eliminate any additional shifts in the definition of the singular
Rouqiuer complex in the next section.
Recall that

n\#d
denotes
n+(n+1)+\cdots+(n+d-1)
.
For
\ell=n+d+n^{\prime}

and
\alpha\in\Lambda(k,d)
as before, we define

(10.2)

\displaystyle V_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}

\displaystyle:=(\Pi Q^{-2})^{n\#d}\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell},

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}

\displaystyle:=(\Pi Q^{-2})^{n^{\prime}\#d}U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell},

recalling
eq.

2.5
.
We also refer to these as odd Grassmannian bimodules.
Of course, the bases for
\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}
and

U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}
from
lemma

10.1
are also bases for

V_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}
and
\widetilde{U}_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}
.

We proceed to develop the properties of odd Grassmannian
bimodules in a systematic way.
Take
\ell=n+d+n^{\prime}
and
\alpha\in\Lambda(k,d)
.
There
are even degree 0 isomorphisms of graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodules

(10.3)

\displaystyle*:\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}U^{\ell}_{\alpha;n^{\prime}}\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle*:V^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\widetilde{U}^{\ell}_{\alpha;n^{\prime}},

\displaystyle a\otimes\dot{c}

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(c)}\dot{c}\otimes a^{*},

(10.4)

\displaystyle*:U^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n^{\prime};\alpha}\,\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle*:\widetilde{U}^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}V^{\ell}_{n^{\prime};\alpha},

\displaystyle\dot{c}\otimes a

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(c)}a^{*}\otimes\dot{c}.

The first pair of these with the roles of
n
and
n^{\prime}
switched
are two-sided inverses of the second pair.

Lemma 10.2
.

Continue with
\ell=n+d+n^{\prime}
and
\alpha\in\Lambda(k,d)
.

(1)

The isomorphisms from
eq.

10.3

satisfy

\left(\bar{a}_{1}v\bar{a}_{2}\right)^{*}=\psi_{n}^{\ell}(\bar{a}_{1})v^{*}\psi_{n+d}^{\ell}(\bar{a}_{2})

for
a_{1}\in OS\!ym_{n}
,
a_{2}\in OS\!ym_{n+d}
and
v\in\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}
or
v\in V^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}
.

(2)

The isomorphisms from
eq.

10.4

satisfy

\left(\bar{a}_{1}u\bar{a}_{2}\right)^{*}=\big(\psi_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}(\bar{a}_{1})u^{*}\big(\psi_{n^{\prime}+d}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}(\bar{a}_{2})

for
a_{1}\in OS\!ym_{n}
,
a_{2}\in OS\!ym_{n+d}
and
u\in U^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}
or

u\in\widetilde{U}^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}
.

Proof.

(1)
It suffices to prove this for
v\in\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}
, then the
identity for
v
viewed instead as a vector in
V_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}

follows as the same parity shifts are used to define

V_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}

from
\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}

as
\widetilde{U}_{\alpha;n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
from
U_{\alpha;n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
.
We first consider right actions. Take
a\in OS\!ym_{n+d}
.
By
eq.

8.4
,
we have that

\psi_{n+d}^{\ell}(\bar{a})=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bar{a}_{i}\dot{c}_{i}

where
a=\sum_{1=1}^{p}(-1)^{(n+d)\operatorname{par}(a_{i})}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i})^{*}c_{i}
.
We saw in the proof of
lemma

10.1
(1) that
\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}
is spanned as an
R_{\ell}
-supermodule
by vectors of the form
b\otimes 1
for

b\in OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k})}
.
So we may assume that
v=b\otimes 1
for such a
b
.
We have that

\displaystyle(v\bar{a})^{*}

\displaystyle=(ba\otimes 1)^{*}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{(n+d)\operatorname{par}(a_{i})}b\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i})^{*}c_{i}\otimes 1\right)^{*}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{(n+d+\operatorname{par}(b))\operatorname{par}(a_{i})}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i})^{*}b\otimes\dot{c}_{i}\right)^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{(n+d)\operatorname{par}(a_{i})+(\operatorname{par}(b)+\operatorname{par}(a_{i}))\operatorname{par}(c_{i})}\dot{c}_{i}\otimes b^{*}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i})=(1\otimes b^{*})\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bar{a}_{i}\dot{c}_{i}=v^{*}\psi^{\ell}_{n+d}(\bar{a}).

For left actions,
take
a\in OS\!ym_{n}
with

a=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(a_{i})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a_{i})^{*}c_{i}
,
so that
\psi^{\ell}_{n}(\bar{a})=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bar{a}_{i}\dot{c}_{i}
.
We may assume that
v=\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b)\otimes 1

for
b\in OS\!ym_{(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},n^{\prime})}
.
Then we have that

\displaystyle(\bar{a}v)^{*}

\displaystyle=(a\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b)\otimes 1)^{*}=(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(b)}(\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b)a\otimes 1)^{*}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{(\operatorname{par}(a_{i})+\operatorname{par}(c_{i}))\operatorname{par}(b)+n\operatorname{par}(a_{i})}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(ba_{i}^{*})\otimes\dot{c}_{i}\right)^{*}=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a_{i})(n+\operatorname{par}(c_{i}))}\dot{c}_{i}\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n}(a_{i}b^{*})

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}a_{i}\dot{c}_{i}\right)\cdot(1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n}(b^{*}))=\psi_{n}^{\ell}(\bar{a})v^{*}.

(2)
By (1), we have that
\Big[\Big(\big(\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}(\bar{a}_{1}))\Big)u^{*}\Big(\big(\phi_{n^{\prime}+d}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}(\bar{a}_{2})\Big)\Big]^{*}=\bar{a}_{1}u\bar{a}_{2}
for
a_{1}\in OS\!ym_{n},a_{2}\in OS\!ym_{n+d}
and

u\in U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}
or
u\in\widetilde{U}_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}
. Now apply
*
to both sides.
∎

Assuming still that

\ell=n+d+n^{\prime}
and
\alpha\in\Lambda(k,d)
, we next introduce a
convenient shorthand for special
elements of odd Grassmannian bimodules.
Recall that
\alpha^{\mathtt{r}}
denotes the reversed composition

(\alpha_{k},\dots,\alpha_{1})
,
and note that the involution
{\gamma}_{d}:OS\!ym_{d}\rightarrow OS\!ym_{d}

interchanges the subalgebras

OS\!ym_{\alpha}
and
OS\!ym_{\alpha^{\mathtt{r}}}
.
For
f\in OS\!ym_{\alpha}

and
g\in OS\!ym_{\alpha^{\mathtt{r}}}
, we let

(10.5)

\displaystyle{\tilde{v}}_{n;\alpha}(f)

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{sh}_{n}(f)\otimes 1\in\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha},

\displaystyle v_{n;\alpha}(f)

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{sh}_{n}(f)\otimes 1\in V^{\ell}_{n;\alpha},

(10.6)

\displaystyle u_{\alpha;n}(g)

\displaystyle:=(-1)^{n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(g)}1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n^{\prime}}\big({\gamma}_{d}(g)\big)\in U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell},

\displaystyle{\tilde{u}}_{\alpha;n}(g)

\displaystyle:=(-1)^{n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(g)}1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n^{\prime}}\big({\gamma}_{d}(g)\big)\in\widetilde{U}_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}.

The vectors
{\tilde{v}}_{n;\alpha}(f)
and
u_{\alpha;n}(g)
are
of the same degrees and parities as
f
and
g
, respectively.
The vector
v_{n;\alpha}(f)
is equal to
{\tilde{v}}_{n;\alpha}(f)
but
it is being viewed as an element of a different superbimodule—the
left action of
OH_{n}^{\ell}
is different due to the parity shift in
eq.

10.2
.
Also,
v_{n;\alpha}(f)
is of degree
\operatorname{deg}(f)-2(n\#d)

and parity
\operatorname{par}(f)+n\#d\pmod{2}
.
Similarly,

{\tilde{u}}_{\alpha;n}(g)
is equal to
u_{\alpha;n}(g)
but with
a different left action of
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
, and

{\tilde{u}}_{\alpha;n}(g)
is of degree
\operatorname{deg}(g)-2(n^{\prime}\#d)
and parity

\operatorname{par}(g)+n^{\prime}\#d\pmod{2}
.
The isomorphisms
*
from
eqs.

10.3
and
10.4
satisfy

(10.7)

\displaystyle{\tilde{v}}_{n;\alpha}(f)^{*}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(f)}u_{\alpha;n^{\prime}}\big({\gamma}_{d}(f)^{*}\big),

\displaystyle v_{n;\alpha}(f)^{*}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n\operatorname{par}(f)}{\tilde{u}}_{\alpha;n^{\prime}}\big({\gamma}_{d}(f)^{*}\big),

(10.8)

\displaystyle u_{\alpha;n}(g)^{*}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(g)}{\tilde{v}}_{n^{\prime};\alpha}\big({\gamma}_{d}(g)^{*}\big),

\displaystyle{\tilde{u}}_{\alpha;n}(g)^{*}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(g)}v_{n^{\prime};\alpha}\big({\gamma}_{d}(g)^{*}\big).

We point out also that the basis vectors in both of the bases constructed in
lemma

10.1
(1)
are
of the form
{\tilde{v}}_{n}(f)
for
f\in OS\!ym_{\alpha}
.
So the vectors
{\tilde{v}}_{n}(f)
(resp.,
v_{n}(f)
) for all
f\in OS\!ym_{\alpha}

generate
\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}
(resp.,
V_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}
) either as a left

OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule or as a right
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
-supermodule.
Similarly, the bases vectors in
lemma

10.1
(2) are of the form

u_{n}(g)

for
g\in OS\!ym_{\alpha^{\operatorname{rev}}}
.
So the vectors
u_{n}(g)
(resp.,
{\tilde{u}}_{n}(g)
) for all
g\in OS\!ym_{\alpha^{\operatorname{rev}}}

generate

U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}

(resp.,
\widetilde{U}_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}
) either as a left

OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
-supermodule or as a right
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule.

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
\ell=n+d+d^{\prime}+n^{\prime}
,
\alpha\in\Lambda(k,d)

and
\alpha^{\prime}\in\Lambda(k^{\prime},d^{\prime})
.

(1)

There are unique isomorphisms
of graded
\big(OH^{\ell}_{n},OH^{\ell}_{n+d+d^{\prime}}\big)
-superbimodules

(10.9)

\displaystyle{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}:\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha\sqcup\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}\otimes_{OH^{\ell}_{n+d}}\widetilde{V}_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}^{\ell},

\displaystyle{\tilde{v}}_{n;\alpha\sqcup\alpha^{\prime}}\big(f\operatorname{sh}_{d}(f^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto{\tilde{v}}_{n;\alpha}(f)\otimes{\tilde{v}}_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}(f^{\prime})

and

(10.10)

\displaystyle c_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}:V^{\ell}_{n;\alpha\sqcup\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}V^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}\otimes_{OH^{\ell}_{n+d}}V_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}^{\ell},

\displaystyle v_{n;\alpha\sqcup\alpha^{\prime}}\big(f\operatorname{sh}_{d}(f^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{((n+d)\#d^{\prime})\operatorname{par}(f)}v_{n;\alpha}(f)\otimes v_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}(f^{\prime})

for
f\in OS\!ym_{\alpha},f^{\prime}\in OS\!ym_{\alpha^{\prime}}
.

(2)

There are unique isomorphisms
of
graded
\big(OH^{\ell}_{n+d+d^{\prime}},OH^{\ell}_{n}\big)
-superbimodules

(10.11)

\displaystyle b_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}:U^{\ell}_{\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha;n}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}U^{\ell}_{\alpha^{\prime};n+d}\otimes_{OH^{\ell}_{n+d}}U^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}

\displaystyle u_{\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha;n}\big(\operatorname{sh}_{d}(f^{\prime})f\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{d^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f)}u_{\alpha^{\prime};n+d}(f^{\prime})\otimes u_{\alpha;n}(f)

and

(10.12)

\displaystyle{\tilde{b}}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}:\widetilde{U}^{\ell}_{\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha;n}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\widetilde{U}^{\ell}_{\alpha^{\prime};n+d}\otimes_{OH^{\ell}_{n+d}}\widetilde{U}^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}

\displaystyle{\tilde{u}}_{\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha;n}\big(\operatorname{sh}_{d}(f^{\prime})f\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{d^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f)+((n^{\prime}+d^{\prime})\#d)\operatorname{par}(f^{\prime})}{\tilde{u}}_{\alpha^{\prime};n+d}(f^{\prime})\otimes{\tilde{u}}_{\alpha;n}(f)

for

f\in OS\!ym_{\alpha^{\mathtt{r}}},f^{\prime}\in OS\!ym_{(\alpha^{\prime})^{\mathtt{r}}}
.

Proof.

In each case, the uniqueness is clear since the vectors specified are
superbimodule generators.

(1)
Let
\gamma:=(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k},\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},n^{\prime})
.
Consider the surjective
\mathbb{F}
-linear map

OS\!ym_{\gamma}\rightarrow\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}},\qquad b_{1}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})\mapsto(b_{1}\otimes 1)\otimes(\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})\otimes 1)

for

b_{1}\in OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k})},b_{2}\in OS\!ym_{(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},n^{\prime})}
.
It is easy to check that it is a right
OS\!ym_{\ell}
-supermodule homomorphism,
so it induces a surjective
R_{\ell}
-supermodule homomorphism

{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}:OS\!ym_{\gamma}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}\rightarrow\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH^{\ell}_{n+d}}\widetilde{V}_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}^{\ell}
.
This is the map
eq.

10.9
.

The domain and range of
{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
are free graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodules, so
to see that
{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
is an isomorphism it suffices to check that they have the same graded ranks.
By
lemma

10.1
(1a) and
eq.

3.8
,

\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}
is a free graded right
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
-supermodule
of graded rank

q^{N(\alpha)+nd}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{n+d}{(n,\alpha_1,\dots,\alpha_k)}_{q,\pi}
.
By
lemma

10.1
(1b) and
eq.

3.8
,

\widetilde{V}_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}^{\ell}
is a free graded left
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
-supermodule
of graded rank

q^{N(\alpha^{\prime})+n^{\prime}d^{\prime}}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{n'+d'}{(\alpha_1',\dots,\alpha'_{k'},n')}_{q,\pi}
.
By
corollary

8.6
,
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
is a free graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule
of graded rank
q^{(n+d)(n^{\prime}+d^{\prime})}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{\ell}{n+d}_{q,\pi}
.
Multiplying these together and using the identity

N(\gamma)=N(\alpha)+N(\alpha^{\prime})+nd+n^{\prime}d^{\prime}+(n+d)(n^{\prime}+d^{\prime})

gives that

\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}
is a free graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule
of graded rank

q^{N(\gamma)}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{\ell}{\gamma}
.
This is also the graded rank of

\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha\sqcup\alpha^{\prime}}
as
a free graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule, as follows from
theorem

6.8
and
eq.

6.23
.

We still need to show that
{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}

is a graded
\big(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n+d+d^{\prime}}^{\ell}\big)
-supermodule homomorphism.
We just go through the details for the right action whose definition is slightly more
complicated than the left action. We restrict to
considering just to vectors
b_{1}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})\otimes 1\in\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha\sqcup\alpha^{\prime}}^{\ell}

for
b_{1}\in OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k})}
and
b_{2}\in OS\!ym_{(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})}
.
We can do this because these vectors generate
\widetilde{V}_{n;\alpha\sqcup\alpha^{\prime}}^{\ell}
as an
R_{\ell}
-supermodule.
Then we take
a\in OS\!ym_{n+d+d^{\prime}}
, write it as

a=\sum_{i=1}^{p}a_{i}^{\prime}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i}^{\prime\prime})

for
a_{i}^{\prime}\in OS\!ym_{(n,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{k})}
and
a_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in OS\!ym_{(\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\alpha^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})}
,
and calculate:

\displaystyle{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\left((b_{1}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})\otimes 1)\bar{a}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\left(b_{1}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})a^{\prime}_{i}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i}^{\prime\prime})\otimes 1\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(b_{2})\operatorname{par}(a_{i}^{\prime})}{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\left(b_{1}a_{i}^{\prime}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2}a_{i}^{\prime\prime}))\otimes 1\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(b_{2})\operatorname{par}(a_{i}^{\prime})}(b_{1}a_{i}^{\prime}\otimes 1)\otimes(\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i}^{\prime\prime})\otimes 1),

\displaystyle{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\left(b_{1}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})\otimes 1\right)\bar{a}

\displaystyle=((b_{1}\otimes 1)\otimes(\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})\otimes 1))\bar{a}=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(b_{1}\otimes 1)\otimes(\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})a_{i}^{\prime}\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i}^{\prime\prime})\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(b_{2})\operatorname{par}(a_{i}^{\prime})}(b_{1}\otimes 1)\otimes\bar{a}_{i}^{\prime}(\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i}^{\prime\prime})\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(b_{2})\operatorname{par}(a_{i}^{\prime})}(b_{1}\otimes 1)\bar{a}_{i}^{\prime}\otimes(\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i}^{\prime\prime})\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(b_{2})\operatorname{par}(a_{i}^{\prime})}(b_{1}a_{i}^{\prime}\otimes 1)\otimes(\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(b_{2})\operatorname{sh}_{n+d}(a_{i}^{\prime\prime})\otimes 1).

These are equal so
{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
is a right
OH_{n+d+d^{\prime}}^{\ell}
-supermodule homomorphism.
We have now established the existence of
eq.

10.9
.

To obtain
eq.

10.10
,
we define a superbimodule isomorphism
c_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}

so that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\ell}_{n;\alpha\sqcup\alpha^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}V^{\ell}_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha\sqcup\alpha^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\id}

The vertical maps here arise from identity maps on the underlying
vector spaces, which are graded superbimodule isomorphisms but they are not
even of degree 0.
It remains to compute
c_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\big(v_{n;\alpha\sqcup\alpha^{\prime}}(f\operatorname{sh}_{d}(f^{\prime}))\big)

explicitly by tracing it around the other three sides of the square
to see that it is exactly the map
c_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
from
eq.

10.10
.
The complicated sign arises because the right hand map takes

{\tilde{v}}_{n;\alpha}(f)\otimes{\tilde{v}}_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}(f^{\prime})

to
(-1)^{((n+d)\#d^{\prime})\operatorname{par}(f)}v_{n;\alpha}(f)\otimes v_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}(f^{\prime})

since
\id:\widetilde{V}_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}^{\ell}\rightarrow V_{n+d;\alpha^{\prime}}^{\ell}

is of parity
(n+d)\#d^{\prime}
.

(2)
Writing
*
for the appropriate one of the isomorphisms
from
eqs.

10.4
and
10.3
,
we define
b_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}
to be
the composition
(*\otimes*)\circ{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}\circ*
.
The appropriate diagram is

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha;n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{\alpha^{\prime};n+d}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}U_{\alpha;n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{V}_{n^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{V}_{n^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime}}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n^{\prime}+d^{\prime}}^{\ell}}\widetilde{V}_{n^{\prime}+d^{\prime};\alpha}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*\otimes*}

The resulting isomorphism
b_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}
is a
graded
\big(OH^{\ell}_{n+d+d^{\prime}},OH^{\ell}_{n}\big)
-superbimodule homomorphism
thanks to
lemma

10.2
(1).
It remains to compute
b_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}\big(\operatorname{sh}_{d}(f^{\prime})f\big)

for
f\in OS\!ym_{\alpha^{\mathtt{r}}},f^{\prime}\in OS\!ym_{(\alpha^{\prime})^{\mathtt{r}}}
.
Note that
\operatorname{sh}_{d}(f^{\prime})f=(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(f)\operatorname{par}(f^{\prime})}f\operatorname{sh}_{d}(f^{\prime})\in OS\!ym_{(\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha)^{\mathtt{r}}}
.
Using
eqs.

10.7
and
10.8
, we have that

\displaystyle{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}\left(u_{\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha;n}\big(\operatorname{sh}_{d}(f^{\prime})f\big)^{*}\right)^{*\otimes*}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f)+n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f^{\prime})+\operatorname{par}(f)\operatorname{par}(f^{\prime})}{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}\left({\tilde{v}}_{n^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha}\Big({\gamma}_{d+d^{\prime}}\big(f^{*}\operatorname{sh}_{d}((f^{\prime})^{*})\big)\Big)\right)^{*\otimes*}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f)+n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f^{\prime})}{\tilde{c}}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}\left({\tilde{v}}_{n^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha}\Big({\gamma}_{d^{\prime}}(f^{\prime})^{*}\operatorname{sh}_{d^{\prime}}\big({\gamma}_{d}(f)^{*}\big)\Big)\right)^{*\otimes*}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f)+n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f^{\prime})}\left({\tilde{v}}_{n^{\prime};\alpha^{\prime}}\big({\gamma}_{d^{\prime}}(f^{\prime})^{*}\big)\otimes{\tilde{v}}_{n^{\prime}+d^{\prime};\alpha}\big({\gamma}_{d}(f)^{*}\big)\right)^{*\otimes*}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{d^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f)}u_{\alpha^{\prime};n+d}(f^{\prime})\otimes u_{\alpha;n}(f),

which is the formula for

b_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}\Big(u_{\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\alpha;n}\big(\operatorname{sh}_{d}(f^{\prime})f\big)\Big)

from
eq.

10.11
. This establishes the existence of

b_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}
.
Finally, the existence of
eq.

10.12
can now be deduced
in the same way that
eq.

10.10
was
deduced from
eq.

10.9
in the proof of (1).
∎

The next lemma gives “Schur bases” for
V_{n;(d)}^{\ell}

and
U_{(d);n}^{\ell}
, and for various specializations
in which we are viewing
\mathbb{F}
as a graded supermodule
concentrated in degree 0 and even parity in the unique possible way.
Similar statements hold for
\widetilde{V}_{n;(d)}^{\ell}
and

\widetilde{U}_{(d);n}^{\ell}
, but these will not be needed.

Lemma 10.4
.

Suppose that
\ell=n+d+n^{\prime}
.

(1)

The supermodule
V_{n;(d)}^{\ell}
has basis

\Big\{v_{n;(d)}\big(s_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\>\Big|\>\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{d\times n^{\prime}}}\Big\}
as a free left
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule,
and basis
\Big\{v_{n;(d)}\big(\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\>\Big|\>\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{d\times n}}\Big\}

as a free right
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
-supermodule.
Hence, the vectors

\Big\{v_{n;(d)}\big(\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\otimes 1\>\Big|\>\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{d\times n}}\Big\}
give a linear basis for

V_{n;(d)}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}\mathbb{F}
.
Moreover, for
\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}}
and any
f\in OS\!ym_{d}
,
we have that

(10.13)

v_{n;(d)}\big(f\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\otimes 1=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\lambda)+|\lambda|(\operatorname{par}(f)+n\#d)}\bar{s}^{(n)}_{\lambda^{\mathtt{t}}}v_{n;(d)}(f)\otimes 1

in
V_{n;(d)}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}\mathbb{F}
.
In particular,

v_{n;(d)}\big(\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\otimes 1=0

unless
\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{d\times n}}
.

(2)

The supermodule
U_{(d);n}^{\ell}
has basis

\Big\{u_{(d);n}\big(s_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\>\Big|\>\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{d\times n}}\Big\}

as a free left
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
-supermodule,
and basis

\Big\{u_{(d);n}\big(\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\>\>\Big|\>\>\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{d\times n^{\prime}}}\Big\}

as a free right
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule.
Hence,
the vectors
