\Big\{1\otimes u_{(d);n}\big(s_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\>\Big|\>\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{d\times n}}\Big\}
give a linear basis for

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}U_{(d);n}^{\ell}
.
Moreover, for
\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}}
and any
f\in OS\!ym_{d}
,
we have that

(10.14)

1\otimes u_{(d);n}\big(s_{\lambda}^{(d)}f\big)=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\lambda)+|\lambda|(\operatorname{par}(f)+d)}1\otimes u_{(d);n}(f)\bar{s}^{(n)}_{\lambda^{\mathtt{t}}}

in
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}U_{(d);n}^{\ell}
.
In particular,

1\otimes u_{(d);n}\big(s_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)=0

unless
\lambda\in{\Lambda^{\!+}_{d\times n}}
.

Proof.

(1)
The existence of the two families of
Schur bases for
V_{n;(d)}^{\ell}

follow in the same way as the bases in
lemma

10.1
(1)
were constructed, using
corollary

6.13
in place of
corollary

6.9
.
To prove
eq.

10.13
,
we have in
OS\!ym_{(n,d)}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n+d}}\mathbb{F}

that

\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\otimes 1=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\lambda)}s_{\lambda^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}\otimes 1

thanks to
corollary

7.4
.
Multiplying this identity on the left by
\operatorname{sh}_{n}(f)

for
f\in OS\!ym_{d}

gives that

\operatorname{sh}_{n}\big(f\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\otimes 1=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\lambda)+|\lambda|\operatorname{par}(f)}s_{\lambda^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}\operatorname{sh}_{n}(f)\otimes 1.

This implies
eq.

10.13
.

For the final assertion, it remains to observe that if
\lambda\notin{\Lambda^{\!+}_{d\times n}}

then we either have that
\lambda_{1}>n
, in which case

s_{\lambda^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}=0
by
theorem

6.3
,
or
{\operatorname{ht}}(\lambda)>d
, in which case

\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}=0
by
corollary

6.4
.
Hence,

{\tilde{v}}_{n;(d)}\big(\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}\big)\otimes 1=\pm{\tilde{v}}_{n;(d)}(1)\otimes 1=0

for such
\lambda
.

(2)
The existence of the two families of bases follows
by applying
*
to the bases in (1),
using also
lemma

10.2
(1),
eq.

10.7
and
eq.

6.7
.
To prove
eq.

10.14
(which is
not
what one gets by applying

*
to
eq.

10.13
),
we start from the identity

s_{\lambda}^{(d)}\otimes 1=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\lambda)}\operatorname{sh}_{d}\big(\sigma_{\lambda^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}\big)\otimes 1

in
OS\!ym_{d,n}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n+d}}\mathbb{F}

from
corollary

7.4
.
Applying the isomorphism
*

that is the right hand map of
eq.

7.2

gives

1\otimes\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\lambda)}1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{d}\big(s_{\lambda^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}\big)

in
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n+d}}OS\!ym_{d,n}
.
This we multiply on the right by
{\gamma}_{d}(f)\in OS\!ym_{d}
to obtain

1\otimes\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}{\gamma}_{d}(f)=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\lambda)+\operatorname{par}(f)|\lambda|}1\otimes{\gamma}_{d}(f)\operatorname{sh}_{d}\big(s_{\lambda^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}\big)
.
This implies that

1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n^{\prime}}\big(\sigma_{\lambda}^{(d)}{\gamma}_{d}(f)\big)=(-1)^{\overline{NE}(\lambda)+\operatorname{par}(f)|\lambda|}1\otimes\operatorname{sh}_{n^{\prime}}({\gamma}_{d}(f))\operatorname{sh}_{n^{\prime}+d}\big(s_{\lambda^{\mathtt{t}}}^{(n)}\big)

in
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}U^{\ell}_{(d);n}
.
Using the definition of the right action in
lemma

10.1
(2) and
eq.

10.6
,
this implies
eq.

10.14
.

The final assertion follows as in (1).
∎

From this point onwards, we will
denote
\widetilde{V}_{n;(1)}^{\ell},V_{n;(1)}^{\ell},U_{(1);n}^{\ell}

and
\widetilde{U}_{(1);n}^{\ell}
by

\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell},V_{n}^{\ell},U_{n}^{\ell}
and
\widetilde{U}_{n}^{\ell}
, respectively.
We denote the generators

{\tilde{v}}_{n;(1)}(f),v_{n;(1)}(f),u_{(1);n}(g)

and
{\tilde{u}}_{(1);n}(g)
from
eqs.

10.5
and
10.6

by
{\tilde{v}}_{n}(f),v_{n}(f),u_{n}(g)
and
{\tilde{u}}_{n}(g)
for
f,g\in OS\!ym_{1}
.
It is also convenient
to write simply
x
in place of
x_{1}
when working in rank 1, i.e.,
we identify
OS\!ym_{1}
with the graded polynomial
superalgebra
\mathbb{F}[x]
generated by the variable
x

that is odd of degree 2 so that
x_{1}\in OS\!ym_{1}

is identified with
x\in\mathbb{F}[x]
.
So, for
f(x)\in\mathbb{F}[x]
, we have that

(10.15)

\displaystyle{\tilde{v}}_{n}\big(f(x)\big)

\displaystyle=f(x_{n+1})\otimes 1\in\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n},

\displaystyle v_{n}\big(f(x)\big)

\displaystyle=f(x_{n+1})\otimes 1\in V^{\ell}_{n},

(10.16)

\displaystyle u_{n}\big(f(x)\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f)}1\otimes f(x_{n^{\prime}+1})\in U^{\ell}_{n},

\displaystyle{\tilde{u}}_{n}\big(f(x)\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n^{\prime}\operatorname{par}(f)}1\otimes f(x_{n^{\prime}+1})\in\widetilde{U}^{\ell}_{n}

for
n^{\prime}
defined from
\ell=n+1+n^{\prime}
. For further motivation for the significance of the graded

\big(OH_{n},OH_{n+1}\big)
-superbimodule
V_{n}^{\ell}
and the
graded
\big(OH_{n+1},OH_{n}\big)
-superbimodule
U_{n}^{\ell}
, see
corollaries

11.4
and
11.6
.

Applying a sequence of the isomorphisms from

eqs.

10.10
and
10.11

in any way that makes sense, we obtain even degree 0 isomorphisms

(10.17)

\displaystyle c_{(1)^{d}}:V_{n;(1^{d})}^{\ell}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}V_{n+1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+2}^{\ell}}\cdots\otimes_{OH_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}V_{n+d-1}^{\ell}

\displaystyle v_{n;(1^{d})}\Big(x_{1}^{\kappa_{1}}\cdots x_{d}^{\kappa_{d}}\Big)

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{d}((n+i)\#(d-i))\kappa_{i}}v_{n}\big(x^{\kappa_{1}}\big)\otimes\cdots\otimes v_{n+d-1}\big(x^{\kappa_{d}}\big)

of
\big(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n+d}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodules, and

(10.18)

\displaystyle b_{(1)^{d}}:U_{(1^{d});n}^{\ell}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}U_{n+d-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}\cdots\otimes_{OH_{n+2}^{\ell}}U_{n+1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}

\displaystyle u_{(1^{d});n}\Big(x_{d}^{\kappa_{d}}\cdots x_{1}^{\kappa_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\mapsto(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^{d}(d-i)\kappa_{i}}u_{n+d-1}\big(x^{\kappa_{d}}\big)\otimes\cdots\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{\kappa_{1}}\big)

of
\big(OH_{n+d}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodules.

The parity shifts incorporated into the definitions of the actions in
the next lemma have been
included to ensure that the actions agree with
eqs.

13.32
and
13.33
below.

Lemma 10.5
.

Suppose that
\ell=n+d+n^{\prime}
.

(1)

There is a left action of
ONH_{d}
on
V_{n;(1^{d})}^{\ell}

making it into an
\big(OH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes ONH_{d},OH_{n+d}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule
so that
a\cdot v_{n;(1^{d})}(f)=(-1)^{(n\#(d-1))\operatorname{par}(a)}v_{n;(1^{d})}(a\cdot f)
for
a\in ONH_{d}
and

f\in O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}
.
Moreover, the inclusion
OS\!ym_{d}\hookrightarrow O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}
induces an isomorphism

V_{n;(d)}^{\ell}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}(\omega\xi)_{d}\cdot\widetilde{V}_{n;(1^{d})}^{\ell}

of
\big(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n+d}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodules
taking
v_{n;(d)}(f)\mapsto v_{n;(1^{d})}(f)
for
f\in OS\!ym_{d}
. Hence, we have that

(10.19)

V_{n;(1^{d})}^{\ell}\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in S_{d}}(\Pi Q^{2})^{\ell(w)}V_{n;(d)}^{\ell}

as graded
(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n+d}^{\ell})
-superbimodules.

(2)

There is a right action of
ONH_{d}
on
U_{(1^{d});n}^{\ell}

making it an

\big(OH_{n+d}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes ONH_{d}\big)
-superbimodule
so that
u_{(1^{d});n}(f)\cdot a=(-1)^{(d-1)\operatorname{par}(a)}u_{(1^{d});n}(f\cdot a)
for
a\in ONH_{d}
and
f\in O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}
; the right action of

ONH_{d}
on
O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}
being used here is the one from
eq.

5.31
. Moreover,
the inclusion
OS\!ym_{d}\hookrightarrow O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}
induces
an isomorphism
U_{(d);n}^{\ell}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}U_{(1^{d});n}^{\ell}\cdot(\xi\omega)_{d}
taking
u_{(d);n}(f)\mapsto u_{(1^{d});n}(f)

for
f\in OS\!ym_{n}
. Hence,
we have that

(10.20)

U_{(1^{d});n}^{\ell}\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in S_{d}}(\Pi Q^{2})^{\ell(w)}U_{(d);n}^{\ell}

as graded
(OH_{n+d}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell})
-superbimodules.

Proof.

(1)
Since
\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;(1^{d})}=OS\!ym_{(n,1^{d},n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}

and
OS\!ym_{(n,1^{d},n^{\prime})}=OS\!ym_{n}\otimes O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}\otimes OS\!ym_{n^{\prime}}
,
the left action of
ONH_{d}
on
O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}

from
section

5

twisted by the automorphism
\operatorname{p}^{n+d-1}:ONH_{d}\rightarrow ONH_{d}

induces a left action of
ONH_{d}
on
\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;(1^{d})}

such that
a\cdot v_{n;(1^{d})}(f)=(-1)^{(n+d-1)\operatorname{par}(a)}v_{n;(1^{d})}(a\cdot f)
for
a\in ONH_{d}
and

f\in O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}
.
This supercommutes with the left action of
OH_{n}^{\ell}

and commutes with the right action of
OH_{n+d}^{\ell}
,
so it makes
\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;(1^{d})}
into an

\big(OH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes ONH_{d},OH_{n+d}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule.
We get the action of
ONH_{d}
on
V^{\ell}_{n;(1^{d})}
in the statement
of the lemma on incorporating the
additional sign of
(-1)^{(n\#d)\operatorname{par}(f)}
into the action, which comes from
the parity shift
\Pi^{n\#d}
in the definition
eq.

10.2
of
V_{n;(1^{d})}^{\ell}
.
Since
OS\!ym_{d}=(\omega\xi)_{d}\cdot O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}
,
the inclusion

OS\!ym_{d}\hookrightarrow O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}
induce an isomorphism

V^{\ell}_{n;(d)}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}(\omega\xi)_{d}\cdot V^{\ell}_{n;(1^{d})}
.
The decomposition
eq.

10.19
follows from
theorem

5.4
.

(2)
This is similar, starting from the right action of
ONH_{d}

on
O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}
discussed at the end of
section

5

composed with
\operatorname{p}^{d-1}
.
The last assertions in (2)
follow because
OS\!ym_{d}=O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{d}\cdot(\xi\omega)_{d}
,
and
eq.

10.20
follows from
eq.

5.33
.
∎

In view of
lemmas

10.3
and
10.5
, all of the odd Grassmannian bimodules

\widetilde{V}^{\ell}_{n;\alpha}
,

V^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}
,
U^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}
and
\widetilde{U}^{\ell}_{\alpha;n}

are isomorphic to 1-morphisms in the 2-supercategory
\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}

introduced in the next important definition.

Definition 10.6
.

The category
\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}

of
odd Grassmannian bimodules
is the full additive graded sub-
(Q,\Pi)
-2-supercategory
of the (weak) graded
(Q,\Pi)
-2-supercategory of
graded superalgebras, graded superbimodules and superbimodule homomorphisms
consisting of objects that are the graded superalgebras
OH_{n}^{\ell}

for
0\leq n\leq\ell
plus a distinguished object that is a trivial (zero)
graded superalgebra,
and
1
-morphisms that are generated by the odd Grassmannian bimodules
V_{n}^{\ell}\in\Hom_{\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}}(OH_{n+1}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell})

and
U_{n}^{\ell}\in\Hom_{\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}}(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n+1}^{\ell})
for
0\leq n<\ell
.

Remark 10.7
.

Although not a strict 2-supercategory, we often work with

\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}
as though it was in fact strict. More formally, when we do this, we
are replacing it with its strictification. The latter can be realized as a 2-subcategory of the
strict graded 2-supercategory of graded supercategories, graded superfunctors and graded supernatural transformations
in such a way that the graded
2-superequivalence from
\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}
takes
the graded
superalgebra
OH_{n}^{\ell}

to the graded supercategory
OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}
,
a graded
\big(OH_{m}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule
M

in
\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}

to the graded superfunctor
M\otimes_{OH_{n}}-:OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}\rightarrow OH_{m}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}
,
and a graded superbimodule endomorphism

f:M\rightarrow M^{\prime}

to the graded supernatural transformation

f\otimes\id:M\otimes_{OH_{n}}-\Rightarrow M^{\prime}\otimes_{OH_{n}}-
.

The next lemma gives explicit presentations for the
generating odd Grassmannian bimodules

V^{\ell}_{n}
and
U_{n}^{\ell}
. In formulating the result,
we also incorporate an indeterminate
t
into our notation,
working in the
R_{\ell}(\!(t^{-1})\!)
-supermodules

V^{\ell}_{n}(\!(t^{-1})\!)
and

U^{\ell}_{n}(\!(t^{-1})\!)
,
this being a natural extension of the generating function formalism developed already for odd symmetric functions.

Lemma 10.8
.

Suppose that
\ell=n+1+n^{\prime}
.

(1)

Let

V:=OH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{R_{\ell}}R_{\ell}[x]\otimes_{R_{\ell}}OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
, which is
the free graded
\big(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule
on the graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule
R_{\ell}[x]
.
For
f(x)\in\mathbb{F}[x]\subset R_{\ell}[x]
, we denote
1\otimes f(x)\otimes 1\in V
by
v\big(f(x)\big)
.
Let
T
be the sub-bimodule of
V
generated by either of the following
equivalent relations
2
2

2

We mean the relations obtained by equating coefficients of powers of
t
on both sides.
:

(10.21)

\displaystyle v\big(x^{r}\big)\bar{e}^{(n+1)}(t)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n(r+1)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)v\big((t-x)x^{r}\big),

(10.22)

\displaystyle\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)v\big(x^{r}\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n(r+1)}v\big(((-1)^{n+r}t-x)^{-1}x^{r}\big)\,\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big),

for
r\geq 0
.
There is an isomorphism of graded
\big(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodules

(10.23)

V\>\big/\>T\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}V_{n}^{\ell},\qquad v\big(f(x)\big)+T\mapsto v_{n}\big(f(x)\big).

Moreover:

(a)

V_{n}^{\ell}
is free
as a graded left
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule
with basis

\{v_{n}(x^{r})\>|\>0\leq r\leq n^{\prime}\}
;

(b)

V_{n}^{\ell}
is free
as a graded right
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
-supermodule
with
basis

\{v_{n}(x^{r})\>|\>0\leq r\leq n\}
;

(c)

the vector
v_{n}(1)
generates

V_{n}^{\ell}
as a graded
\big(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule.

(2)

Let

U:=OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\otimes_{R_{\ell}}R_{\ell}[x]\otimes_{R_{\ell}}OH_{n}^{\ell}
, which is
the free graded
\big(OH_{n+1}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule
on the graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule
R_{\ell}[x]
.
For
f\in\mathbb{F}[x]\subseteq R_{\ell}[x]
, we denote
1\otimes f\otimes 1\in U
by
u(f)
.
Let
S
be the sub-bimodule of
U
generated by either of the following
equivalent relations:

(10.24)

\displaystyle\bar{e}^{(n+1)}(t)u\big(x^{r}\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n(r+1)}u\big((t-x)x^{r}\big)\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{r+1}t\big),

(10.25)

\displaystyle u\big(x^{r}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n(r+1)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{r+1}t\big)u\big(((-1)^{r+1}t-x)^{-1}x^{r}\big),

for
r\geq 0
.
There is an isomorphism of graded
\big(OH_{n+1}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodules

(10.26)

U\>\big/\>S\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}U_{n}^{\ell},\qquad u\big(f(x)\big)+S\mapsto u_{n}\big(f(x)\big).

Moreover:

(a)

U_{n}^{\ell}
is free
as a right
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule
with
basis

\{u_{n}(x^{r})\>|\>0\leq r\leq n^{\prime}\}
;

(b)

U_{n}^{\ell}
is free
as a graded left
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
-supermodule
with basis

\{u_{n}(x^{r})\>|\>0\leq r\leq n\}
;

(c)

the vector
u_{n}(1)
generates

U_{n}^{\ell}
as a graded
\big(OH_{n+1}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule.

Proof.

Note to start with that (1a)–(1b) and (2a)–(2b) follow immediately from

lemma

10.4
.

(1)
In this paragraph, we prove the equivalence of the relations
eq.

10.21

and
eq.

10.22
by some formal manipulation with power series.
Replacing
t
by
(-1)^{n+r}t
, the relations
eq.

10.21
are
equivalent to

(10.27)

\displaystyle\bar{e}^{(n)}\big(t\big)v\big(\big((-1)^{n+r}t-x\big)x^{r}\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n(r+1)}v\big(x^{r}\big)\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)

for all
r\geq 0
.
We first show that the relations
eq.

10.27
are equivalent to the relations

(10.28)

\displaystyle\bar{e}^{(n)}(t)v\big(x^{r}\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(n+1)r}v\big(t(t^{2}+x^{2})^{-1}x^{r}\big)\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)-(-1)^{nr}v\big(x(t^{2}+x^{2})^{-1}x^{r}\big)\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r+1}t\big)

for all
r\geq 0
.
To deduce
eq.

10.27
from
eq.

10.28
, we take the left hand
side of
eq.

10.27
, which equals

(-1)^{n+r}\bar{e}^{(n)}(t)v\big(tx^{r}\big)-\bar{e}^{(n)}(t)v\big(x^{r+1}\big)
.
Then we use
eq.

10.28
to commute
\bar{e}^{(n)}(t)
to the right
to obtain

(-1)^{n(r+1)}v\big(t^{2}(t^{2}+x^{2})^{-1}x^{r}\big)\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)+(-1)^{(n+1)(r+1)}v\big(tx(t^{2}+x^{2})^{-1}x^{r}\big)\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r+1}t\big)\\
-(-1)^{(n+1)(r+1)}v\big(tx(t^{2}+x^{2})^{-1}x^{r}\big)\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r+1}t\big)+(-1)^{n(r+1)}v\big(x^{2}(t^{2}+x^{2})^{-1}x^{r}\big)\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big).

After making obvious cancellations, this is equal to the right hand
side of
eq.

10.27
.
The reverse implication, that is, the deduction of
eq.

10.28
from

eq.

10.27
is similar: one starts with the right hand side of

eq.

10.28
then uses
eq.

10.27
to commute the
\bar{e}^{(n+1)}(\pm t)
term to the left.
So the relations
eq.

10.21
and

eq.

10.28
are equivalent. Then we prove that
eq.

10.28
and

eq.

10.22
are equivalent using the identities

(10.29)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{(n)}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbf{i}}^{1-n}e^{(n)}({\mathbf{i}}t)+{\mathbf{i}}^{n}e^{(n)}(-{\mathbf{i}}t)}{1+{\mathbf{i}}},

\displaystyle e^{(n)}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbf{i}}^{1-n}\varepsilon^{(n)}({\mathbf{i}}t)+{\mathbf{i}}^{n}\varepsilon^{(n)}(-{\mathbf{i}}t)}{1+{\mathbf{i}}}

where
{\mathbf{i}}\in\mathbb{F}
denotes a square root of
-1
,
which may be verified by equating coefficients on each side.
To pass from
eq.

10.28
to
eq.

10.22
,
we replace
\varepsilon^{(n)}(t)
in
eq.

10.22
with this
linear combination of

e^{(n)}(\pm{\mathbf{i}}t)
, then use
eq.

10.28

with
t
replaced by
\pm{\mathbf{i}}t
to
commute
e^{(n)}(\pm{\mathbf{i}}t)
to the right. At the end,
a linear factor in the denominator

t^{2}-x^{2}=(t-x)(t+x)
cancels, and after that one converts
back to
\varepsilon^{(n)}(\pm t)
using
eq.

10.29
again.
This calculation is quite lengthy but elementary.
The argument can be reversed to obtain
eq.

10.28
from

eq.

10.22
, hence, the equivalence.

Next,
we first check that the images of the relations
eq.

10.21
and

eq.

10.22
hold
3
3

3

Since
eq.

10.21
and

eq.

10.22
are equivalent,
we really only need to check one of these. We check
both because it is easy and explains how we discovered the
relations in the first place.
for the
actions of
OH_{n}^{\ell}
and
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
on
V_{n}^{\ell}
.
For
eq.

10.21
, it suffices to check that

v_{n}(1)\bar{e}^{(n+1)}(t)=(-1)^{n}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{n}t\big)v_{n}(t-x)
, i.e., the
r=0
case,
for then we can act on the left with
x_{1}^{r}\in ONH_{1}
using

lemma

10.5
to deduce the more general formulae.
This follows because

(10.30)

{\tilde{v}}_{n}(1)\bar{e}^{(n+1)}(t)=\bar{e}^{(n)}(t){\tilde{v}}_{n}(t-x)

in
\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell}[t]=OS\!ym_{(n,1,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}[t]

since, by the definition of the actions and
eq.

4.55
, both sides
are equal to
(t-x_{1})\cdots(t-x_{n})(t-x_{n+1})\otimes 1
.
Similarly, for
eq.

10.22
, it suffices
to check that

\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)v_{n}(1)=(-1)^{n}v_{n}\big(\big((-1)^{n}t-x\big)^{-1}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n}t\big)

or, equivalently,

(-1)^{n}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{n}t\big)v_{n}(1)=v_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)
.
This follows because

(10.31)

\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}{\tilde{v}}_{n}(1)={\tilde{v}}_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)

in
\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell}[t]=OS\!ym_{(n,1,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}[t]

since both sides are equal to
(t-x_{n})\cdots(t-x_{1})\otimes 1
thanks to
eq.

4.55
.

The relations check made in the previous paragraph
implies that there is a well-defined
graded superbimodule homomorphism

V/T\rightarrow V_{n}^{\ell}
taking
v\big(f(x)\big)+T
to
v_{n}\big(f(x)\big)

for all
f(x)\in\mathbb{F}[x]
.
Moreover, this map is surjective by (1a)–(1b).
To show that it is an isomorphism,
it suffices to show that

V/T

is generated as a right
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
-module
by the vectors
v(1)+T,v(x)+T,\dots,v(x^{n})+T
.
It is generated as a superbimodule by all

v(x^{r})+T\>(r\geq 0)
.
The relation
eq.

10.22
implies that
any
\bar{a}v\big(f(x)\big)
for
a\in OS\!ym_{n},f(x)\in\mathbb{F}[x]
can
be expanded as a linear combination of vectors of the form
v(g(x))\bar{b}
for
b\in OS\!ym_{n+1},g(x)\in\mathbb{F}[x]
.
Hence,
V/T
is generated just as a right
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
-supermodule
by the vectors
v(x^{r})+T\>(r\geq 0)
.
Now we let
V^{\prime}
be the right
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
-submodule of
V

generated by
T
and
the vectors
v(1),v(x),\dots,v(x^{n})
,
and complete the argument by
showing
that
v(x^{n+r})\in V^{\prime}

by induction on
r=0,1,2,\dots
.
The base
r=0
is vacuous.
For the induction step, take
r>0
. Consider the relation arising from the
t^{-1}
-coefficients in
eq.

10.22
. The left hand side is a polynomial, so this coefficient is zero on the
left hand side.
Hence, the
t^{-1}
-coefficient of the right hand side belongs to

T\subseteq V^{\prime}
.
Working out this coefficient explicitly reveals that it equals

\pm v(x^{n+1+r})
plus a linear combination of terms of the form

v(x^{s})\bar{a}
for
0\leq s\leq n+r
and
a\in OS\!ym_{n+1}

of positive degree. All of these “lower terms” are in
V^{\prime}
by induction, hence,
v(x^{n+1+r})\in V^{\prime}
.

Finally, to establish (1c),
looking at the
t^{n}
-coefficients of
eq.

10.21

shows that
v(x^{r+1})+T
lies in the sub-bimodule generated
by
v(x^{r})+T
for any
r\geq 0
.
Hence, by another induction on
r
, the sub-bimodule
of
V/T
generated by
v(1)+T
contains all
v(x^{r})+T
.

(2)
This follows by a similar argument to the proof of (1). We just
explain how to see that the relations
eqs.

10.24
and
10.25
both hold
for the actions of
OH_{n}^{\ell}
and
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}

on
U_{n}^{\ell}
.
For
eq.

10.24
, it suffices to check it in the case that
r=0
, then one can act on the right with
x^{r}

using
lemma

10.5
to get the general result.
To prove it when
r=0
, it suffices to show that

\bar{e}^{(n+1)}(t)u_{n}(1)=(-1)^{n}u_{n}(t-x)\bar{e}^{(n)}(-t)
.
This follows because, in view of the signs in the definition
of the left and right actions in
lemma

10.1
(2) and also
eq.

10.6
,
both sides are equal to

\big(t-(-1)^{n^{\prime}}x_{n^{\prime}+1}\big)\big(t-(-1)^{n^{\prime}}x_{n^{\prime}+2}\big)\cdots\big(t-(-1)^{n^{\prime}}x_{\ell}\big)
.
Similarly, to prove
eq.

10.25
,
it suffices to check the case

r=0
, which amounts to showing that

u_{n}(1)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)=(-1)^{n+1}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(-t)u_{n}\big((t+x)^{-1}\big)
.
This follows because both sides are equal to

\big(t+(-1)^{n^{\prime}}x_{\ell}\big)\cdots\big(t+(-1)^{n^{\prime}}x_{n^{\prime}+2}\big)
.
∎

The final lemma in this section is an application of
the presentation for
V_{n}^{\ell}
derived in
lemma

10.8
(1).
Recall the graded
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra automorphism

\delta_{n}^{\ell}:OH_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow OH_{n}^{\ell}

from
eq.

8.7
.
In terms of generating functions, we have that

(10.32)

\displaystyle\delta_{n}^{\ell}\big(\bar{e}^{(n)}(t)\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell n}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big)-(-1)^{\ell n}t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big)+(-1)^{\ell n}t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+1}t\big).

This follows from
eq.

8.9
on
equating coefficients of
t
.

Lemma 10.9
.

There is a unique even degree 0 graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule automorphism

\phi_{n}^{\ell}:V_{n}^{\ell}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}V_{n}^{\ell}
such that

\phi_{n}^{\ell}\big(v_{n}(1)\big)=v_{n}(1)
and

\phi_{n}^{\ell}\big(avb\big)=\delta_{n}^{\ell}(a)\phi_{n}^{\ell}(v)\delta_{n+1}^{\ell}(b)

for all
a\in OH_{n}^{\ell},b\in OH_{n+1}^{\ell}

and
v\in V_{n}^{\ell}
.
Moreover, we have that

(10.33)

\displaystyle\phi_{n}^{\ell}\big(v_{n}(x^{r})\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell r}v_{n}(x^{r})+(-1)^{(\ell+1)(r-1)}\big(1-(-1)^{r}\big)\dot{o}^{(\ell)}v_{n}(x^{r-1})

for all
r\geq 0
.

Proof.

The uniqueness is clear since
V_{n}^{\ell}
is a cyclic
superbimodule thanks to
lemma

10.8
(1c).
To prove existence,
consider the
\big(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule
V
that is

V_{n}^{\ell}
with the left and right actions of
OH_{n}^{\ell}
and
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}

defined by twisting the usual actions with
the automorphisms
\delta_{n}^{\ell}
and
\delta_{n+1}^{\ell}
,
respectively. We are trying to show that there is a superbimodule homomorphism

\phi_{n}^{\ell}:V_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow V

satisfying
eq.

10.33
.
We can check this using the presentation for the superbimodule

V_{n}^{\ell}
from
lemma

10.8
(1) with the defining
relations
eq.

10.21
.
This reduces the proof to checking that the identity

(10.34)

\phi_{n}^{\ell}\big(v_{n}(x^{r})\big)\delta_{n+1}^{\ell}\big(\bar{e}^{(n+1)}(t)\big)=(-1)^{n(r+1)}\delta_{n}^{\ell}\Big(\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)\Big)\phi_{n}^{\ell}\big(v_{n}\big((t-x)x^{r}\big)\Big)

is satisfied in the superbimodule

V_{n}^{\ell}

for any
r\geq 0
.
Substituting the formulae from
eqs.

10.32
and
10.33
into
eq.

10.34
and
cancelling
(-1)^{\ell r+\ell(n+1)}
from both sides,
this expands to the equation

\Big(v_{n}(x^{r})-(-1)^{\ell+r}\big(1-(-1)^{r}\big)\dot{o}^{(\ell)}v_{n}(x^{r-1})\Big)\Big(\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big)-t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big)+t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{\ell+1}t\big)\Big)\\
\quad=\Big((-1)^{n(r+1)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r}t\big)-(-1)^{(n+1)r}t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r}t\big)+(-1)^{(n+1)r}t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r+1}t\big)\Big)\\
\times\>\Big(v_{n}\big(\big((-1)^{\ell}t-x)x^{r}\big)-(-1)^{r}\big(1-(-1)^{r}\big)t\dot{o}^{(\ell)}v_{n}(x^{r-1})-(-1)^{\ell+r}\big(1+(-1)^{r}\big)\dot{o}^{(\ell)}v_{n}(x^{r})\Big).

Now we expand both sides using that

\big(\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\big)^{2}=0
. Commuting
\dot{o}^{(\ell)}
then

\bar{e}^{(n+1)}(\pm t)
to the left with
eq.

10.21
,
the left hand side contributes the sum of the following four terms:

\displaystyle v_{n}(x^{r})\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n(r+1)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r}t\big)v_{n}\big(\big((-1)^{\ell}t-x)x^{r}\big),

\displaystyle-(-1)^{\ell+r}\big(1-(-1)^{r}\big)\dot{o}^{(\ell)}v_{n}(x^{r-1})\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big)

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{(n+1)r+\ell}\big(1-(-1)^{r}\big)\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r+1}t\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times v_{n}\big(\big((-1)^{\ell}t-x\big)x^{r-1}\big),

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n+r}t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}v_{n}(x^{r})\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{\ell}t\big)

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{(n+1)r}t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r}t\big)v_{n}\big(((-1)^{\ell}t-x)x^{r}\big),

\displaystyle(-1)^{n+r}t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}v_{n}(x^{r})\bar{e}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{\ell+1}t\big)

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{(n+1)r}t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r+1}t\big)v_{n}\big(((-1)^{\ell}t+x)x^{r}\big).

Commuting
\dot{o}^{(\ell)}
to the left, the right hand side
contributes the sum of the following five terms:

\displaystyle(-1)^{n(r+1)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r}t\big)v_{n}\big(\big((-1)^{\ell}t-x)x^{r}\big),

\displaystyle-(-1)^{(n+1)r}t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r}t\big)v_{n}\big(\big((-1)^{\ell}t-x)x^{r}\big),

\displaystyle(-1)^{(n+1)r}t^{-1}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r+1}t\big)v_{n}\big(\big((-1)^{\ell}t-x)x^{r}\big),

\displaystyle-(-1)^{(n+1)r}\big(1-(-1)^{r}\big)t\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r+1}t\big)v_{n}(x^{r-1}),

\displaystyle-(-1)^{(n+1)r+\ell}\big(1+(-1)^{r}\big)\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\bar{e}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{\ell+n+r+1}t\big)v_{n}(x^{r}).

From this, without making any further commutations, it follows that the two sides are equal.
∎

11.
Rigidity of
\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}

In this section, we prove that the
2
-supercategory
\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}

from
definition

10.6
is

rigid
in the sense that all of its 1-morphisms have
left and right duals.
Given a formal Laurent series
f(t)
, we use the notation
\big[f(t)\big]_{t^{r}}

to denote its
t^{r}
-coefficient.
Similarly, we use
\big[f(t)\big]_{t^{\leq r}}
,

\big[f(t)\big]_{t^{\geq r}}
, etc. for the
formal Laurent series obtained by keeping only the terms with the
specified powers of
t
.
Note also the following elementary identity:
we have that

(11.1)

\displaystyle f(x)

\displaystyle=\left[(t-x)^{-1}f(t)\right]_{t^{-1}}

for any polynomial
f(x)\in\mathbb{F}[x]
.
For the first lemma, recall the notation
eqs.

10.15
and
10.16
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Suppose that
\ell=n+1+n^{\prime}

and
0\leq r,s\leq n
.

(1)

\displaystyle v_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}x^{r}\big)=\sum_{p=r}^{n}v_{n}(x^{p})\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\right]_{>t^{p}}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(t)t^{r-p-1}-\sum_{p=0}^{r-1}v_{n}(x^{p})\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\right]_{\leq t^{p}}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(t)t^{r-p-1}
.

(2)

\displaystyle u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}x^{s}\big)=\sum_{q=s}^{n}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(t)\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\right]_{>t^{q}}u_{n}(x^{q})t^{s-q-1}-\sum_{q=0}^{s-1}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(t)\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\right]_{\leq t^{q}}u_{n}(x^{q})t^{s-q-1}
.

Proof.

(1)
Remembering
eq.

10.15
and the definition of the right action of
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
from
lemma

10.1
(1), the identity obtained by applying

\gamma_{n+1}
to
lemma

6.11
(2) implies for any
m\geq 0
that

v_{n}\big(x^{m+n+1}\big)=-\sum_{p=0}^{n}v_{n}\big(x^{p}\big)\sum_{s=0}^{m}(-1)^{m+n+1-p-s}\bar{\varepsilon}_{m+n+1-p-s}^{(n+1)}\bar{\eta}_{s}^{(n+1)}.

We multiply this by
t^{r-m-n-2}
and sum over
m\geq 0
to obtain

\sum_{m\geq 0}v_{n}\big(x^{m+n+1}\big)t^{r-m-n-2}=-\sum_{p=0}^{n}v_{n}\big(x^{p}\big)\left(\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{s=0}^{m}(-1)^{m+n+1-p-s}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{m+n+1-p-s}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{s}t^{p-m-n-1}\right)t^{r-p-1}.

The expression in brackets is equal to
\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\right]_{\leq t^{p}}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(t)
,
as may be checked by comparing
t^{p-m-n-1}
-coefficients for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Using this and
eq.

4.57
, we obtain

\displaystyle v_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}x^{r}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=r}^{n}v_{n}\big(x^{p}\big)t^{r-p-1}+\sum_{m\geq 0}v_{n}\big(x^{m+n+1}\big)t^{r-m-n-2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=r}^{n}v_{n}\big(x^{p}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(t)t^{r-p-1}-\sum_{p=0}^{n}v_{n}\big(x^{p}\big)\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\right]_{\leq t^{p}}\bar{\gamma}^{(n+1)}(t)t^{r-p-1}.

It remains to write the first

\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)

as

\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\right]_{>t^{p}}+\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\right]_{\leq t^{p}}

and make some obvious cancellations to obtain the desired formula.

(2)
Remembering the sign in
eq.

10.16

and the matching sign in the definition of the left action of
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
from
lemma

10.1
(2), the identity obtained by
applying
\operatorname{p}^{n^{\prime}}\circ\operatorname{sh}_{n^{\prime}}\circ{\gamma}_{n+1}
to

lemma

6.11
(1)
gives

u_{n}\big(x^{m+n+1}\big)=-\sum_{q=0}^{n}\sum_{r=0}^{m}(-1)^{m+n+1-q-r}\bar{\eta}_{r}^{(n+1)}\bar{\varepsilon}_{m+n+1-q-r}^{(n+1)}u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big)

for any
m\geq 0
.
We multiply this by
t^{s-m-n-2}
and sum over
m\geq 0
to obtain

\sum_{m\geq 0}u_{n}\big(x^{m+n+1}\big)t^{s-m-n-2}=-\sum_{q=0}^{n}\left(\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{r=0}^{m}(-1)^{m+n+1-q-r}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{m+n+1-q-r}t^{q-m-n-1}\right)u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big)t^{s-q-1}.

The expression in brackets is equal to
\bar{\gamma}^{(n+1)}(t)\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\right]_{\leq t^{q}}
.
Now the proof is completed as in (1).
∎

Lemma 11.2
.

Suppose that
\ell=n+1+n^{\prime}
.
The element

z:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,s\geq 0\\
r+s\leq n\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r+s}v_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-s}\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)\in V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}

is central in the sense that
az=za
for all
a\in OH_{n}^{\ell}
.

Proof.

By
lemma

10.8
(b),
any element of
V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}

can be written as
\sum_{p,q=0}^{n}v_{n}\big(x^{p})f_{p,q}\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big)
for unique
f_{p,q}\in OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
.
So there are unique

L_{p,q}(t)=\sum_{k=0}^{n}L_{p,q;k}t^{k},R_{p,q}(t)=\sum_{k=0}^{n}R_{p,q;k}t^{k}\in OH_{n+1}^{\ell}[t]

such that

\displaystyle\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)z

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q=0}^{n}(-1)^{p+q}v_{n}\big(x^{p})L_{p,q}(t)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big),

\displaystyle z\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q=0}^{n}(-1)^{p+q}v_{n}\big(x^{p})R_{p,q}(t)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big).

To prove the lemma,
it suffices to show that

\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)z=z\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)
,
which we do by computing
L_{p,q}(t)
and

R_{p,q}(t)
explicitly then checking that

L_{p,q;k}=R_{p,q;k}
for all
0\leq p,q,k\leq n
.
For brevity, we adopt the convention that

\varepsilon^{(n+1)}_{r}=0
for
r<0
; this allows the
restriction
r+s\leq n
on the summation in the definition
of
z
to be omitted.

To compute
L_{p,q}(t)
, we expand

\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)z
using
eq.

10.22
,
lemma

11.1
(1) and
eq.

4.57
:

\displaystyle\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)z

\displaystyle=\sum_{r,q\geq 0}(-1)^{r+q}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)v_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r,q\geq 0}(-1)^{r+q+n(r+1)}v_{n}\big(((-1)^{n+r}t-x)^{-1}x^{r}\big)\,\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r,q\geq 0}\sum_{p=r}^{n}v_{n}(x^{p})\left((-1)^{r+q+n(r+1)+(n+r)(r-p-1)}\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)\right]_{>t^{p}}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}t^{r-p-1}\right)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{r,q\geq 0}\sum_{p=0}^{r-1}v_{n}(x^{p})\left((-1)^{r+q+n(r+1)+(n+r)(r-p-1)}\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)\right]_{\leq t^{p}}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}t^{r-p-1}\right)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big).

Hence, using
r+q+n(r+1)+(n+r)(r-p-1)+p+q\equiv pr+pn+p+r\pmod{2}
to simplify the sign,
we have that

(11.2)

L_{p,q}(t)=\sum_{r=0}^{p}(-1)^{pr+pn+p+r}\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)\right]_{>t^{p}}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}t^{r-p-1}\\
-\sum_{r\geq p+1}(-1)^{pr+pn+p+r}\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)\right]_{\leq t^{p}}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}t^{r-p-1}

for
0\leq p,q\leq n
.
Similarly, we compute
R_{p,q}(t)
using
eq.

10.25
,
lemma

11.1
(2) and
eq.

4.57
:

\displaystyle z\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,s\geq 0}(-1)^{p+s}v_{n}\big(x^{p}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-p-s}\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,s\geq 0}(-1)^{p+s+n(s+1)}v_{n}\big(x^{p}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-p-s}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{s+1}t\big)\otimes u_{n}\big(((-1)^{s+1}t-x)^{-1}x^{s}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,s\geq 0}\sum_{q=s}^{n}v_{n}\big(x^{p}\big)\left((-1)^{p+s+n(s+1)+(s+1)(s-q-1)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-p-s}\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{s+1}t\big)\right]_{>t^{q}}t^{s-q-1}\right)\otimes u_{n}(x^{q})

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{p,s\geq 0}\sum_{q=0}^{s-1}v_{n}\big(x^{p}\big)\left((-1)^{p+s+n(s+1)+(s+1)(s-q-1)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-p-s}\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{s+1}t\big)\right]_{\leq t^{q}}t^{s-q-1}\right)\otimes u_{n}(x^{q}).

From this, we get that

(11.3)

R_{p,q}(t)=\sum_{s=0}^{q}(-1)^{ns+qs+n+1}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-p-s}\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{s+1}t\big)\right]_{>t^{q}}t^{s-q-1}\\
-\sum_{s\geq q+1}(-1)^{ns+qs+n+1}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-p-s}\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{s+1}t\big)\right]_{\leq t^{q}}t^{s-q-1}

for
0\leq p,q\leq n
.

Now we use
eqs.

11.2
and
11.3
to compute the
t^{k}
-coefficients

L_{p,q;k}

and
R_{p,q;k}
for
0\leq k\leq n
:

(11.4)

\displaystyle L_{p,q;k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{k}(-1)^{(n+k)(r+k)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+r-p-k}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}-\sum_{r\geq p+1}(-1)^{(n+k)(r+k)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+r-p-k}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q},

(11.5)

\displaystyle R_{p,q;k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{k}(-1)^{(n+k)s}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-p-s}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+s-q-k}-\sum_{s\geq q+1}(-1)^{(n+k)s}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-p-s}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+s-q-k}.

The details of these two computations are very similar, so we just elaborate
on the first one.
Note that

(-1)^{pr+pn+p+r}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}\big((-1)^{n+r}t\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}t^{r-p-1}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{pr+pn+p+r+(n+r)j+n+1+j}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+1-j}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}t^{j+r-p-1}.

To get a contribution of
t^{k}
from this,
we must have that
j=k+p-r+1
,
in which case

(-1)^{pr+pn+p+r+(n+r)j+n+1+j}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+1-j}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}=(-1)^{(n+k)(r+k)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+r-p-k}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}.

Using these observations, it follows
that
the
t^{k}
-coefficients from the
first summation in
eq.

11.2

contribute

\sum(-1)^{(n+k)(r+k)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+r-p-k}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}

summing over
r
with
0\leq r\leq p

such that
j:=k+p-r+1
satisfies
j>p
,
i.e.,
0\leq r\leq\min(k,p)
.
Similarly, the
t^{k}
-coefficients from the second
summation in
eq.

11.2
contribute

-\sum(-1)^{(n+k)(r+k)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+r-p-k}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}

summing over
r
with
r\geq p+1

such that
j:=k+p-r+1
satisfies
j\leq p
,
i.e.,
r\geq\max(k,p)+1
.
Thus, we have shown that

L_{p,q;k}=\sum_{r=0}^{\min(k,p)}(-1)^{(n+k)(r+k)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+r-p-k}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}-\sum_{r\geq\max(k,p)+1}(-1)^{(n+k)(r+k)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+r-p-k}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-q}.

This is exactly as in
eq.

11.4
when
k\leq p
.
To see that it is also
equal to
eq.

11.4
when
k>p
, one just has to cancel
the overlapping terms
when
p+1\leq r\leq k

in the first and second summations from
eq.

11.4
.

It remains to see that
L_{p,q;k}=R_{p,q;k}
for every
0\leq p,q,k\leq n
.
In fact, the first summation in
eq.

11.4
is equal to the first summation in

eq.

11.5
. This is easily seen on making the substitution
s=k-r
in one of them. To see that the second summation in
eq.

11.4
is equal to the second summation in
eq.

11.5
, we substitute
m=r-p
in
eq.

11.4
and
m=s-q
in
eq.

11.5
, and the problem reduces to showing that

\sum_{m\geq 1}(-1)^{(n+k)(m+p+k)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-k+m}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-p-q-m}=\sum_{m\geq 1}(-1)^{(n+k)(m+q)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-p-q-m}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-k+m}

for all
0\leq p,q,k\leq n
.
In fact this equality already holds in
OS\!ym_{n+1}
thanks to

lemma

4.9
or, rather, the identity obtained from that by applying the involution
{\gamma}_{n+1}
.
∎

Theorem 11.3
.

Suppose that
\ell=n+1+n^{\prime}
.
There are unique even degree 0 superbimodule homomorphisms

\displaystyle\operatorname{coev}_{n}

\displaystyle:OH_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}

(11.6)

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,s\geq 0\\
r+s\leq n\end{subarray}}(-1)^{n-r-s}v_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n-r-s}\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)

and

\displaystyle\operatorname{ev}_{n}

\displaystyle:U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow OH_{n+1}^{\ell}

\displaystyle u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+s-n}&\text{if $r+s\geq n$}\\
0&\text{otherwise,}\end{array}\right.

the latter being true for all
r,s\geq 0
.
Moreover, the following compositions are identities:

(11.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}OH_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\otimes_{EOH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{n}^{\ell},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\operatorname{coev}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{n}\otimes\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}

(11.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{coev}_{n}\otimes\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}

Hence,
\operatorname{coev}_{n}
and
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
are the unit and counit of an adjunction making
\left(U_{n}^{\ell},V_{n}^{\ell}\right)

into a dual pair
of 1-morphisms in
\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}
.

Before proving the theorem, we write down several equivalent formulations of the
definitions of
\operatorname{coev}_{n}
and
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
, assuming that such superbimodule homomorphisms do indeed exist.
For the unit of adjunction, the element
\operatorname{coev}_{n}(1)
in the statement of
theorem

11.3

is equal to
(-1)^{n}z
where
z
is the central tensor from
lemma

11.2
.
In terms of generating functions, we have that

(11.11)

\operatorname{coev}_{n}(1)=\left[v_{n}((t-x)^{-1})\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\otimes u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\right]_{t^{-1}}.

This is easily checked by computing coefficients of
t
.
Using
eqs.

10.25
and
10.22

with
r=0
, we have that

(11.12)

\displaystyle v_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)

\displaystyle=\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{n}t\big)v_{n}\big((-1)^{n}\big),

(11.13)

\displaystyle\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=u_{n}\big((-1)^{n}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(-t).

Hence, we can rewrite the right hand side of
eq.

11.11

to obtain

(11.14)

\displaystyle\operatorname{coev}_{n}(1)

\displaystyle=\left[v_{n}((t-x)^{-1})\otimes u_{n}\big((-1)^{n}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(-t)\right]_{t^{-1}}=\left[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{n}t\big)v_{n}\big((-1)^{n}\big)\otimes u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\right]_{t^{-1}}.

Equating coefficients in
eq.

11.14
gives two more formulae:

(11.15)

\displaystyle\operatorname{coev}_{n}(1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{n}v_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes u_{n}(1)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}_{n-r}=\sum_{s=0}^{n}(-1)^{(n+1)s}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}_{n-s}v_{n}(1)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{s}\big).

For the counit
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
, we have the following two equivalent formulations:

(11.16)

\displaystyle\operatorname{ev}_{n}\left(u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}f(x)\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(g(x)\big)\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(t)f(t)g(t)\right]_{<t^{0}}

(11.17)

\displaystyle\operatorname{ev}_{n}\left(u_{n}\big(f(x)\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(g(x)(t-x)^{-1}\big)\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(t)f(t)g(t)\right]_{<t^{0}}

for any
f(x),g(x)\in\mathbb{F}[x]
.
This can be checked by
assuming that
f(x)=x^{r},g(x)=x^{s}
then equating coefficients of
t
.

Proof of
theorem

11.3
.

We split the proof up into six steps.

Step one.

We first construct the maps
\operatorname{coev}_{n}
and
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
.
For
\operatorname{coev}_{n}
, we define

\operatorname{coev}_{n}(a):=(-1)^{n}az
for any
a\in OH_{n}^{\ell}
,
where
z
is as in
lemma

11.2
.
This is an even degree 0 homomorphism of graded left
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodules.
Since
(-1)^{n}az=(-1)^{n}za
according to
lemma

11.2
,
it is also a right
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule homomorphism, so it is a
superbimodule homomorphism.
Thus, we have defined the superbimodule homomorphism
\operatorname{coev}_{n}
, and

eq.

11.6
holds.
For
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
,

U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}
is a free graded right
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
-supermodule with basis

u_{n}(x^{r})\otimes v_{n}(x^{s})\>(0\leq r\leq n^{\prime},0\leq s\leq n)

by
lemma

10.8
(1b) and (2a).
So there is a unique even degree 0
graded right
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
-supermodule homomorphism
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
such that

theorem

11.3
holds for
0\leq r\leq n^{\prime}
and
0\leq s\leq n
.
It is not yet clear that
theorem

11.3
holds for other values of
r
and
s
,
or that
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
is a graded left
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
-supermodule homomorphism.

Step two.

Next we use induction to show that

theorem

11.3
also holds for
0\leq r\leq n^{\prime}
and all

s>n
.
Fix a choice of
r
with
0\leq r\leq n^{\prime}
.
We know by our definition that
theorem

11.3
holds for
0\leq s\leq n
.
For the induction step, we take some
s\geq n
, assume that
theorem

11.3

holds for this and all smaller values of
s
, and show that it also holds
when
s
is replaced by
s+1
.
The
m=0
case of
lemma

6.11
(2) shows that

x_{1}^{n+1}=\sum_{p=0}^{n}(-1)^{n-p}x_{1}^{p}e^{(n+1)}_{n+1-p}
.
Multliplying on the left
by
x_{1}^{s-n}
then applying
{\gamma}_{n+1}
, we deduce that

x_{n+1}^{s+1}=\sum_{p=0}^{n}(-1)^{n-p}x_{n+1}^{p+s-n}\varepsilon_{n+1-p}^{(n+1)}
.
So

u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(x^{s+1}\big)=\sum_{p=0}^{n}(-1)^{n-p}u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(x^{p+s-n}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+1-p}.

Now we apply the right supermodule homomorphism

\operatorname{ev}_{n}
using the induction hypothesis
to see that

\operatorname{ev}_{n}\Big(u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(x^{s+1}\big)\Big)=\sum_{p=\max(0,2n-r-s)}^{n}(-1)^{n-p}\bar{\eta}_{p+r+s-2n}^{(n+1)}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}_{n+1-p}.

This is equal to
\bar{\eta}_{r+s+1-n}^{(n+1)}
by
eq.

4.57
,
which is what we wanted.

Step three.

Since
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
is a right supermodule homomorphism by definition,
the composition of maps in
eq.

11.9
makes sense
and is a right
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule homomorphism
4
4

4

However,
eq.

11.10
does not make sense at this point
since
\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n}
is not defined until we can shown that
\operatorname{ev}_{n}

is a left supermodule homomorphism.
.
To show that the composition is equal to the identity map,
it suffices to show that it
takes
u_{n}(f(x))
to
u_{n}(f(x))
for any
f(x)\in\mathbb{F}[x]
of degree
\leq n^{\prime}
,
since these elements generate
U_{n}^{\ell}
as a right
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule
by
lemma

10.8
(2a).
Using
eq.

11.11
, we apply the even map

\id\otimes\operatorname{coev}_{n}
to
u_{n}(f(x))\otimes 1

to obtain

\left[u_{n}\big(f(x)\big)\otimes v_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\otimes\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\right]_{t^{-1}}.

By step two,
eq.

11.17
holds for
f(x)
of degree
\leq n^{\prime}
.
We use it to apply
\operatorname{ev}_{n}\otimes\id
, then multiply out the tensor,
to obtain

\left[\big[\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(t)f(t)\big]_{<t^{0}}\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\right]_{t^{-1}}.

Now,
eq.

11.13
shows that
\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)
is a polynomial in
t
, so we can omit the inside square brackets. Then
the
\eta
and
\varepsilon
cancel by
eq.

4.57
, leaving us with

\left[f(t)u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\right]_{t^{-1}}=u_{n}\big(f(x)\big),

where we applied
eq.

11.1
for the final equality. Hence, the composition
eq.

11.9

is the identity.

Step four.

We prove that
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
is a left supermodule homomorphism.
Since
v_{n}(1)
generates
V_{n}^{\ell}
as a superbimodule (
lemma

10.8
(1c))
and
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
is a right supermodule homomorphism, it suffices to show that

a\operatorname{ev}_{n}(u\otimes v_{n}(1))=\operatorname{ev}_{n}(au\otimes v_{n}(1))
for all

a\in OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
and
u\in U_{n}^{\ell}
.
By step three, we have that

a\left((\operatorname{ev}_{n}\otimes\id)\circ(\id\otimes\operatorname{coev}_{n})(u\otimes 1)\right)=a(1\otimes u)=(1\otimes au)=(\operatorname{ev}_{n}\otimes\id)\circ(\id\otimes\operatorname{coev}_{n})(au\otimes 1)

in
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}
.
Using the formula for
\operatorname{coev}_{n}(1)
from
eq.

11.15
, this shows that

\sum_{s=0}^{n}(-1)^{(n+1)s}a\operatorname{ev}_{n}\big(u\otimes\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}_{n-s}v_{n}(1)\big)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)=\sum_{s=0}^{n}(-1)^{(n+1)s}\operatorname{ev}_{n}\big(au\otimes\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}_{n-s}v_{n}(1)\big)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{s}\big).

By
lemma

10.8
(2b),
U_{n}^{\ell}
is a free left
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}
-supermodule
with basis
u_{n}(x^{s})\>(0\leq s\leq n)
, so we can project to

-\otimes u_{n}(x^{n})
-components in the identity just proved
to obtain the
desired equality
a\operatorname{ev}_{n}\big(u\otimes v_{n}(1)\big)=\operatorname{ev}_{n}\big(au\otimes v_{n}(1)\big)
.

Step five.

We know already that
theorem

11.3
holds for
0\leq r\leq n^{\prime}
and
s\geq 0
.
We now show that it holds for all remaining
r>n^{\prime}
and
s\geq 0
.
Take
r\geq n^{\prime}
and
assume by induction that
theorem

11.3
holds for this value of
r
and
all
s\geq 0
.
Equating
t^{n}
-coefficients in
eq.

10.24
gives
that
u_{n}\big(x^{r+1}\big)=\bar{o}^{(n+1)}u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)+(-1)^{r}u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\bar{o}^{(n)}
.
Equating
t^{n}
-coefficients in
eq.

10.21

(with
r
replaced by
s
) gives that
v_{n}\big(x^{s+1}\big)=v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)\bar{o}^{(n+1)}-(-1)^{n+s}\bar{o}^{(n)}v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)
.
Making these substitutions, it is then easy to check that

u_{n}\big(x^{r+1}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)=(-1)^{n+r+s+1}u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n}(x^{s+1})+(-1)^{n+r+s}u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)\bar{o}^{(n+1)}+\bar{o}^{(n+1)}u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big).

Now we compute
\operatorname{ev}_{n}\Big(u_{n}\big(x^{r+1}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)\Big)

by applying the superbimodule homomorphism
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
to the right hand side of the equation just derived and using the induction hypothesis. If
r+s+1<n
the right hand side evaluates to
0

so
\operatorname{ev}_{n}\Big(u_{n}\big(x^{r+1}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)\Big)=0
as required.
If
r+s+1=n
the right hand side evaluates to
\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+1+s-n}

as required. If
r+s+1>n
the right hand side evaluates to

(-1)^{n+r+s+1}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+1+s-n}+(-1)^{n+r+s}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+s-n}\bar{o}^{(n+1)}+\bar{o}^{(n+1)}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+s-n}.

If
n+r+s
is odd then
\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+s-n}
and
\bar{o}^{(n+1)}

commute by the image of the defining relation
eq.

4.1
under
{\gamma}
, so this simplifies to
the desired
\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+1+s-n}
.
If
n+r+s
is even then
\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+s-n}\bar{o}^{(n+1)}+\bar{o}^{(n+1)}\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+s-n}=2\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+1+s-n}
by the image of
the second relation from
eq.

4.60

under
{\gamma}
, so again the expression simplifies to

\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+1+s-n}
.

Step six.

It remains to check that the composition

eq.

11.10
, which makes sense as
\operatorname{ev}_{n}
is a left supermodule homomorphism, is the identity.
We do this by showing
that it takes
v_{n}(f(x))
to
v_{n}(f(x))
for any
f(x)\in\mathbb{F}[x]
.
The argument is similar to step three.
By
eq.

11.11
,
the map
\operatorname{coev}_{n}\otimes\id
takes
1\otimes v_{n}(f(x))
to

\left[v_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\otimes u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(f(x)\big)\right]_{t^{-1}}.

Then we apply the even map
\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n}
using
eq.

11.16
(whose validity relies on the conclusion of step five)
to get

\left[v_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\big[\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(t)f(t)\big]_{<t^{0}}\right]_{t^{-1}}.

Since
v_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)

is a polynomial in
t
by
eq.

11.12
,
we can omit the inside square brackets. After doing that,

\varepsilon
and
\eta
cancel using
eq.

4.57
, so we obtain

\left[v_{n}\left((t-x)^{-1}\right)f(t)\right]_{t^{-1}}=v_{n}\big(f(x)\big),

where we used
eq.

11.1
for the final equality.
∎

For the following corollary, recall from
corollary

9.6
that

ONH_{n}^{\ell}
is a subalgebra of
ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}
in a natural way
for
0\leq n<\ell
.

Corollary 11.4
.

For
0\leq n<\ell
, the following diagrams of graded superfunctors commute up to even degree 0 isomorphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ONH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n+1}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\!\!{(\Pi Q^{-2})^{n}\res_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}\!\!\!\!\!-}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ONH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Pi Q^{2})^{n}\ind_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n+1}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}-\!\!}

(The vertical arrows are the equivalences
of graded supercategories from
corollary

9.3
.)

Proof.

Note that

(\Pi Q^{2})^{n}\ind_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}

is left adjoint to
(\Pi Q^{-2})^{n}\res_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}
.
Also
U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}-
is left adjoint to

V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}-
by
theorem

11.3
.
Hence, using the uniqueness of left adjoints,
it suffices to prove that the first square commutes.
We show equivalently that the following commutes up to even degree 0 isomorphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ONH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\res_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n+1}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n+1}}OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}\!\!\!\!\!-}

Here, we have removed the degree and parity shifts on the horizontal arrows
and we have replaced the equivalence

(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n+1}-\simeq\Hom_{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}\big(ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n+1},-\big)

on the left hand vertical arrow with the
quasi-inverse equivalence

ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n+1}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}-\simeq O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n+1}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n+1}}OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}-
.
To prove this new diagram commutes, it suffices to show that

(\omega\xi)_{n}O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n+1}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n+1}}OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\simeq\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell}

as
(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n+1}^{\ell})
-superbimodules.
As the proof of
lemma

10.1
(1) in the case
d=0
shows, the isomorphism

OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}OS\!ym_{(n+1,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}

of
theorem

8.5
(1) is an isomorphism of
graded
(OH_{n+1}^{\ell},OH_{n+1}^{\ell})
-superbimodules.
So

(\omega\xi)_{n}O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n+1}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n+1}}OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\simeq(\omega\xi)_{n}O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n+1}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{n+1}}OS\!ym_{(n+1,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}
.
By
eq.

5.30
,
we have that
(\omega\xi)_{n}O\hskip-0.42677ptPol_{n+1}\simeq OS\!ym_{(n,1)}
, so
this is

\simeq OS\!ym_{(n,1,n^{\prime})}\otimes_{OS\!ym_{\ell}}R_{\ell}
,
which is exactly the definition of
\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell}
.
∎

The next theorem, which we prove by twisting
theorem

11.3
with
some automorphisms,
gives the second adjunction. The explicit formulae for
this are not as nice as for the first adjunction.

Theorem 11.5
.

Suppose that
\ell=n+1+n^{\prime}
.
There are unique even degree 0 superbimodule homomorphisms

\displaystyle\widetilde{\operatorname{coev}}_{n}

\displaystyle:OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\rightarrow\widetilde{U}_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell}

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{s=0}^{n^{\prime}}(-1)^{\ell s+\binom{s}{2}}\big(\psi_{n+1}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}\Big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{n^{\prime}-s}^{(n^{\prime})}\Big){\tilde{u}}_{n}(1)\otimes{\tilde{v}}_{n}(x^{s})

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{n}

\displaystyle:\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}\widetilde{U}_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow OH_{n}^{\ell}

\displaystyle\!\!\!{\tilde{v}}_{n}(x^{r})\otimes{\tilde{u}}_{n}(x^{s})

\displaystyle\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\!\!(-1)^{n(r+s)+\binom{r}{2}+\binom{s+1}{2}}\Big[\big(\psi_{n}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}\Big(\bar{\eta}_{r+s-n^{\prime}}^{(n^{\prime}+1)}+(-1)^{n+1}\big(1-(-1)^{s}\big)\bar{\eta}_{r+s-n^{\prime}-1}^{(n^{\prime}+1)}\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\Big)\Big]&\text{if $r+s>n^{\prime}$}\\
\!\!(-1)^{n(r+s)+\binom{r}{2}+\binom{s+1}{2}}&\text{if $r+s=n^{\prime}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

giving the unit and counit of an adjunction making

\left(\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell},\widetilde{U}_{n}^{\ell}\right)

into another dual pair of 1-morphisms in
\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}
.

Proof.

Let
\dagger:=\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}\circ*:\widetilde{U}_{n}^{\ell}\rightarrow V_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
, where
\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}
is the map
from
lemma

10.9
.
Since
\psi_{n+1}^{\ell}=\delta_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}\circ\big(\psi_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}

and
\psi_{n}^{\ell}=\delta_{n^{\prime}+1}^{\ell}\circ\big(\psi_{n^{\prime}+1}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}

according to the definition
eq.

8.7
,

lemma

10.9
and
lemma

10.2
(2) imply that

(11.18)

\left(\bar{b}_{1}u\bar{b}_{2}\right)^{\dagger}=\psi_{n+1}^{\ell}(\bar{b}_{1})u^{\dagger}\psi_{n}^{\ell}(\bar{b}_{2})

for
u\in\widetilde{U}_{n}^{\ell}
,
b_{1}\in OS\!ym_{n+1}
and
b_{2}\in OS\!ym_{n}
.
Now we define even degree 0 graded
R_{\ell}
-supermodule homomorphisms

\widetilde{\operatorname{coev}}_{n}
and
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{n}

so that the following diagrams commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{U}_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\ell}_{n^{\prime}}\otimes_{OH_{n^{\prime}+1}^{\ell}}U^{\ell}_{n^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{\ell}_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\operatorname{coev}}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dagger\otimes*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{coev}_{n^{\prime}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}\widetilde{U}_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}}V_{n^{\prime}}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n^{\prime}+1}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*\otimes\dagger}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{n}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{n^{\prime}}}

To see that the vertical maps
\dagger\otimes*
and
*\otimes\dagger
in these diagrams make sense, one needs to check that they are balanced,
which follows using

lemma

10.2
(1) and
eq.

11.18
.
In fact,
\widetilde{\operatorname{coev}}_{n}
and
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{n}
defined
in this way are superbimodule homomorphisms.
This again follows using

lemma

10.2
(1) and
eq.

11.18

since
\operatorname{coev}_{n^{\prime}}
and
\operatorname{ev}_{n^{\prime}}
are superbimodule homomorphisms.

The zig-zag identities
for
\widetilde{\operatorname{coev}}_{n}
and
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{n}

follow from their definitions using the zig-zag identities

eqs.

11.9
and
11.10

for
\operatorname{coev}_{n^{\prime}}
and
\operatorname{ev}_{n^{\prime}}
.
Hence they give the unit and counit of an adjunction.

It just remains to compute the explicit formulae for the maps given in
the statement of the corollary.
To see that
\widetilde{\operatorname{coev}}_{n}(1)=\sum_{s=0}^{n^{\prime}}(-1)^{\ell s+\binom{s}{2}}\Big[\big(\psi_{n+1}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}\Big]\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{n^{\prime}-s}^{(n^{\prime})}\big){\tilde{u}}_{n}(1)\otimes{\tilde{v}}_{n}(x^{s})
, the image of
1
under the
southwest pair of maps in the diagram defining
\widetilde{\operatorname{coev}}_{n}

is
\sum_{s=0}^{n^{\prime}}(-1)^{(n^{\prime}+1)s}\bar{\varepsilon}_{n^{\prime}-s}^{(n^{\prime})}v_{n^{\prime}}(1)\otimes u_{n^{\prime}}(x^{s})
thanks to
eq.

11.15
.
This is also the image of

\sum_{s=0}^{n^{\prime}}(-1)^{\ell s+\binom{s}{2}}\Big[\big(\psi_{n+1}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{n^{\prime}-s}^{(n^{\prime})}\big)\Big]{\tilde{u}}_{n}(1)\otimes{\tilde{v}}_{n}(x^{s})

under the right hand map
\dagger\otimes*
by

eqs.

10.7
,
10.8
,
11.18
and
10.33
.

To compute
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{n}\big({\tilde{v}}_{n}(x^{r})\otimes{\tilde{u}}_{n}(x^{s})\big)
, we use the diagram defining
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{n}
.
From
eqs.

10.7
,
10.8
and
10.33
,
one sees that

(*\otimes\dagger)\big({\tilde{v}}_{n}(x^{r})\otimes{\tilde{u}}_{n}(x^{s})\big)=(-1)^{\binom{r}{2}+\binom{s+1}{2}+n(r+s)}u_{n^{\prime}}(x^{r})\otimes\left[v_{n^{\prime}}(x^{s})+(-1)^{n+1}\big(1-(-1)^{s}\big)v_{n^{\prime}}(x^{s-1})\dot{o}^{(\ell)}\right].

Using
theorem

11.3
, the image of this under the homomorphism

\big(\psi_{n}^{\ell}\big)^{-1}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{n^{\prime}}
is easily seen to be equal
to the formula for
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{n}\big({\tilde{v}}_{n}(x^{r})\otimes{\tilde{u}}_{n}(x^{s})\big)
given in the statement of the lemma.
∎

Corollary 11.6
.

For
0\leq n<\ell
, the following diagrams of graded superfunctors commute up to even degree 0 isomorphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ONH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n+1}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\res_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{V}_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}\!\!\!\!\!-}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ONH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coind_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\omega\xi})_{n+1}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{U}_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}-\!\!}

(The vertical arrows are the equivalences
of graded supercategories from
corollary

9.3
.)

Proof.

The commutativity of the first diagram follows from
corollary

11.4
, then the
second follows using
theorem

11.5
and the uniqueness of right adjoints.
∎

The following generalizes
corollary

9.7
.

Corollary 11.7
.

For
0\leq n<\ell
, we have that

\ind_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}\simeq(\Pi Q^{2})^{\ell-2n-1}\coind_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}.

Hence,
ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}
is a graded Frobenius extension of

ONH_{n}^{\ell}

of degree
2(\ell-2n-1)
and parity
\ell-2n-1\pmod{2}
.

Proof.

The second statement follows from the first statement by the general
theory of Frobenius extensions explained after
eq.

2.11
.
To prove the first statement,

corollaries

11.4
and
11.6

show that under the Morita equivalence
\ind_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}

corresponds to
(\Pi Q^{2})^{-n}U_{n}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}-

and
\coind_{ONH_{n}^{\ell}}^{ONH_{n+1}^{\ell}}
corresponds
to
\widetilde{U}_{n}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}-
.
Now the result follows because

(\Pi Q^{2})^{-n}U_{n}\simeq(\Pi Q^{2})^{\ell-2n-1}\widetilde{U}_{n}
according to
eq.

10.2
.
∎

The final task in this section is to compute various mates of the
endomorphisms of
U_{(1^{d});n}^{\ell}
defined by the
action of
ONH_{d}
from
lemma

10.5
(2). Specifically,
we need to work out the endomorphisms in
\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}

that correspond to the diagrams
eqs.

13.4
and
13.8
in the graphical calculus to be introduced later in the article.
Suppose that
\ell=n+d+n^{\prime}
for
d\geq 0
.
We define graded superalgebra homomorphisms

(11.19)

\displaystyle\rho_{(1^{d});n}:ONH_{d}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\End_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}{\text{-}}OH_{n}^{\ell}}\big(U_{n+d-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}\cdots\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}\big)^{{\operatorname{sop}}}

(11.20)

\displaystyle\lambda_{n;(1^{d})}:ONH_{d}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\End_{OH_{n}^{\ell}{\text{-}}OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}\big(V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}\cdots\otimes_{OH_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}(V_{n+d-1}^{\ell})\big)

as follows.
For
a\in ONH_{d}
,
\rho_{(1^{d});n}(a)
is defined
to be the top map the following diagram commute:

(11.21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{n+d-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}\cdots\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{n+d-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}\cdots\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{(1^{d});n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{(1^{d});n}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(b_{(1)^{d}}\big)^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{(1^{d});n}(a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{(1^{d});n}(f)\mapsto(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(a)\operatorname{par}(f)}u_{(1^{d});n}(f)\cdot a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{(1)^{d}}}

Also
\lambda_{n;(1^{d})}(a)
is the top map making
the following commute:

(11.22)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}\cdots\otimes_{OH_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}V_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}\cdots\otimes_{OH_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}V_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n;(1^{d})}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n;(1^{d})}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(c_{(1)^{d}}\big)^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{n;(1^{d})}(a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{n;(1^{d})}(f)\mapsto a\cdot v_{n;(1^{d})}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{(1)^{d}}}

The vertical maps in
eqs.

11.21
and
11.22
come from
eqs.

10.18
and
10.17
.
We also remind the reader that

u_{(1^{d});n}(f)\cdot a=(-1)^{(d-1)\operatorname{par}(a)}u_{(1^{d});n}(f\cdot a)

and
a\cdot v_{n;(1^{d})}(f)=(-1)^{(n\#(d-1))\operatorname{par}(a)}v_{n;(1^{d})}(a\cdot f)
.

Lemma 11.8
.

For
0\leq n<\ell
, the mate
of the
\big(OH_{n+1}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule endomorphism

\rho_{(1);n}(x_{1})

under the adjunction from
theorem

11.3
,
that is, the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{coev}_{n}\otimes\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\rho_{(1);n}(x_{1})\otimes\id}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}

is equal to
\lambda_{n;(1)}(x_{1})
.

Proof.

Since
V_{n}^{\ell}
is generated as a superbimodule
by
v_{n}(1)
by
lemma

10.8
(1c), it suffices to show that

(\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n})\circ(\id\otimes\rho_{(1);n}(x_{1})\otimes\id)\circ(\operatorname{coev}_{n}\otimes\id)\big(1\otimes v_{n}(1)\big)=\lambda_{n;(1)}(x_{1})\big(v_{n}(1)\big)\otimes 1.

This is a calculation from the definitions.
The right hand side is

v_{n}(x)\otimes 1
by the definition
eq.

11.22
.
For the left hand side, we first apply

\operatorname{coev}_{n}\otimes\id
using
eq.

11.14

to get

\Big[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{n}t\big)v_{n}\big((-1)^{n}\big)\otimes u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\otimes v_{n}(1)\Big]_{t^{-1}}.

Then we apply the odd endomorphism

\id\otimes\rho_{(1);n}(x_{1})\otimes\id

defined by
eq.

11.21
to get

\Big[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{n+1}t\big)v_{n}\big((-1)^{n}\big)\otimes u_{n}\big((t+x)^{-1}x\big)\otimes v_{n}(1)\Big]_{t^{-1}}.

Finally we apply

\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n}
using
eq.

11.16
with
t
replaced by
-t
to get

\Big[\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{n+1}t\big)v_{n}\big((-1)^{n}\big)\big[\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}(-t)t\big]_{<t^{0}}\Big]_{t^{-1}}\otimes 1.

Computing the coefficients explicitly,
this is equal to

v_{n}(1)\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{1}-(-1)^{n}\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}^{(n)}v_{n}(1)
.
Since
\eta_{1}^{(n+1)}=x_{1}+\cdots+x_{n+1}

and
\varepsilon_{1}^{(n)}=x_{1}+\cdots+x_{n}
(and
(-1)^{n}
cancels
when
\varepsilon_{1}^{(n)}
acts on
v_{n}(1)
due to the parity shift) this is
v_{n}(x)\otimes 1
.
∎

Lemma 11.9
.

For
0<n<\ell
, the
\big(OH_{n}^{\ell},OH_{n}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule
endomorphism
\sigma_{n}
that is defined by the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n-1}^{\ell}}V_{n-1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}U_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n-1}^{\ell}}V_{n-1}^{\ell}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{coev}_{n}\otimes\id\otimes\id}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}U_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n-1}^{\ell}}V_{n-1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}U_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n-1}^{\ell}}V_{n-1}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\rho_{(1^{2});n-1}(\tau_{1})\otimes\id}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}OH_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}

maps

u_{n-1}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n-1}\big(x^{s}\big)

to

(11.23)

(-1)^{nr+rs+r+s+n+1}v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)+\sum_{p=0}^{n}\sum_{q=0}^{r+s-n}(-1)^{nq+pq+rq+r+q}v_{n}\big(x^{p}\big)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}_{n-p}\bar{\eta}^{(n)}_{r+s-n-q}

for any
r\geq 0
and
0\leq s\leq n-1
.

Proof.

First, we show that

\rho_{(1^{2});n-1}(\tau_{1})\Big(u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\otimes u_{n-1}\big(x^{r}\big)\Big)

equals

(11.24)

-u_{n}\big((t+x)^{-1}x^{r}\big)\otimes u_{n-1}\Big(\big(t-(-1)^{r}x\big)^{-1}\Big)+\sum_{q=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{q+r+rq}u_{n}\big((t+x)^{-1}x^{q}\big)\otimes u_{n-1}\big(x^{r-q-1}\big).

To do this, according to the definition
eq.

11.21
,
we first use the inverse of the map
b_{(1)^{2}}
from
eq.

10.18
to pass to

U_{(1^{2});n-1}^{\ell}
.
This maps

u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\otimes u_{n-1}\big(x^{r}\big)

to

(-1)^{r}u_{(1^{2});n-1}\big((t-x_{2})^{-1}x_{1}^{r}\big)
.
The application of
\rho_{(1^{2});n-1}(\tau_{1})

takes this to

-u_{(1^{2});n-1}\big((t+x_{2})^{-1}x_{1}^{r}\cdot\tau_{1}\big)
.
This we can compute with
lemma

5.11

to get

-u_{(1^{2});n-1}\Big((t+x_{2})^{-1}x_{2}^{r}\big(t+(-1)^{r}x_{1}\big)^{-1}\Big)-\sum_{q=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{rq}u_{(1)^{2};n-1}\Big((t+x_{2})^{-1}x_{2}^{q}x_{1}^{r-q-1}\Big).

After that we apply
b_{(1)^{2}}
to
obtain the vector
in
U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}U_{n-1}^{\ell}

displayed in
eq.

11.24
.

Now to prove the lemma, we again calculate
with generating functions.
Start with the vector
u_{n-1}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n-1}\big(x^{s}\big)
for
0\leq s\leq n-1
(this assumption on
s
will be crucial shortly).
By
eq.

11.11
, the map

\operatorname{coev}_{n}\otimes\id\otimes\id
takes it to

(11.25)

\Big[v_{n}((t-x)^{-1})\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(t)\otimes u_{n}\big((t-x)^{-1}\big)\otimes u_{n-1}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n-1}\big(x^{s}\big)\Big]_{t^{-1}}.

Then we apply the odd homomorphism

\id\otimes\rho_{(1^{2});n-1}(\tau_{1})\otimes\id

using
eq.

11.24
to obtain

(11.26)

\Big[v_{n}((t+x)^{-1})\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(-t)\otimes u_{n}\big((t+x)^{-1}x^{r}\big)\otimes u_{n-1}\Big(\big(t-(-1)^{r}x\big)^{-1}\Big)\otimes v_{n-1}\big(x^{s}\big)\Big]_{t^{-1}}\\
+\sum_{q=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{q+r+rq+1}\Big[v_{n}((t+x)^{-1})\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(-t)\otimes u_{n}\big((t+x)^{-1}x^{q}\big)\otimes u_{n-1}\big(x^{r-q-1}\big)\otimes v_{n-1}\big(x^{s}\big)\Big]_{t^{-1}}.

It just remains to apply
\id\otimes\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n-1}
.
We treat the two terms in
eq.

11.26

separately. For the first term, we have
that

\operatorname{ev}_{n-1}\Big(u_{n-1}\Big(\big(t-(-1)^{r}x\big)^{-1}\Big)\otimes v_{n-1}\big(x^{s}\big)\Big)=(-1)^{rs+r}\left[\bar{\eta}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{r}t\big)t^{s}\right]_{<t^{0}}

by
eq.

11.16
(with
t
replaced by
(-1)^{r}t
).
The assumption that
s\leq n-1

means that we can omit the truncation to
<t^{0}
here.
So the first term contributes

(-1)^{rs+r}\Big[v_{n}((t+x)^{-1})\otimes\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(-t)u_{n}\big((t+x)^{-1}x^{r}\big)\otimes\bar{\eta}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{r}t\big)t^{s}\Big]_{t^{-1}}.

Now we use
eq.

10.25
to rewrite this as

(-1)^{nr+rs+r+n+1}\Big[v_{n}((t+x)^{-1})\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{r}t\big)\bar{\eta}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{r}t\big)t^{s}\otimes 1\Big]_{t^{-1}}.

The
\varepsilon
and
\eta
cancel by the infinite Grassmannian relation
to leave

(-1)^{nr+rs+r+n+1}\Big[v_{n}((t+x)^{-1}t^{s})\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes 1\Big]_{t^{-1}}\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}(-1)^{nr+rs+r+s+n+1}v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes 1.

It remains to consider the term obtained by applying

\id\otimes\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n-1}

to the second term from
eq.

11.26
. Using
theorems

11.3
and
10.25
, this contributes

\sum_{q=0}^{r+s-n}(-1)^{q+r+rq+1}\Big[v_{n}((t+x)^{-1})\otimes\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n+1)}(-t)u_{n}\big((t+x)^{-1}x^{q}\big)\otimes\bar{\eta}^{(n)}_{r+s-q-n}\Big]_{t^{-1}}=\\
\sum_{q=0}^{r+s-n}(-1)^{q+r+rq+nq+n}\Big[v_{n}\big((t+x)^{-1}\big)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{q}\big)\bar{\varepsilon}^{(n)}\big((-1)^{q}t\big)\bar{\eta}^{(n)}_{r+s-q-n}\otimes 1\Big]_{t^{-1}}.

It remains to work out the
t^{-1}
-coefficient explicitly to complete the proof.
∎

Lemma 11.10
.

For
0<n<\ell
, the mate of
\rho_{(1^{2});n-1}(\tau_{1})

under the adjunction from
theorem

11.3
, that is, the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n-1}^{\ell}}V_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{coev}_{n-1}\otimes\id\otimes\id}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}U_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n-1}^{\ell}}V_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\sigma_{n}\otimes\id}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}

where
\sigma_{n}
is the superbimodule homomorphism
5
5

5

Diagrammatically, we have rotated through
180^{\circ}
by rotating by
90^{\circ}
twice, see
eqs.

13.4
and
13.8
.

defined in
lemma

11.9
,
is equal to
\lambda_{n-1;(1^{2})}(\tau_{1})
.

Proof.

By
lemma

10.8
(1b)–(1c),

V_{n-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}V_{n}^{\ell}
is generated as an

\big(OH_{n-1}^{\ell},OH_{n+1}^{\ell}\big)
-superbimodule
by the vectors
v_{n-1}(1)\otimes v_{n}(x^{s})\>\>(0\leq s\leq n)
.
Therefore it suffices to show that

(\id\otimes\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n})\circ(\id\otimes\sigma_{n}\otimes\id)\circ(\operatorname{coev}_{n-1}\otimes\id\otimes\id)\big(1\otimes v_{n-1}(1)\otimes v_{n}(x^{s})\big)=\lambda_{n-1;(1^{2})}(\tau_{1})\big(v_{n-1}(1)\otimes v_{n}(x^{s})\big)\otimes 1

for
0\leq s\leq n
.
The right hand side may be computed directly
from
eqs.

11.22
and
10.17
.
It equals

(-1)^{n}c_{(1)^{2}}\Big(v_{n-1;(1^{2})}\big(\tau_{1}\cdot x_{2}^{s}\big)\Big)\otimes 1\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}(-1)^{n+1}c_{(1)^{2}}\Big(v_{n-1;(1^{2})}\big(\eta^{(2)}_{s-1}\big)\Big)\otimes 1.

So to complete the proof we must show that

(11.27)

\!\!\left(c_{(1)^{2}}\otimes\id\right)^{\!-1}\!\!\Big((\id\otimes\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n})\ \!\circ(\id\otimes\sigma_{n}\otimes\id)\circ(\operatorname{coev}_{n-1}\otimes\id\otimes\id)\big(1\otimes v_{n-1}(1)\otimes v_{n}(x^{s})\big)\Big)\\
=(-1)^{n+1}v_{n-1;(1^{2})}\big(\eta^{(2)}_{s-1}\big)\otimes 1.

To compute the left hand side, we first use
eq.

11.15

to get

(\operatorname{coev}_{n-1}\otimes\id\otimes\id)\big(1\otimes v_{n-1}(1)\otimes v_{n}(x^{s})\big)=\sum_{r=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{nr}\bar{\varepsilon}_{n-1-r}^{(n-1)}v_{n-1}(1)\otimes u_{n-1}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n-1}(1)\otimes v_{n}(x^{s}).

Then we apply
\id\otimes\sigma_{n}\otimes\id

using
eq.

11.23
, noting also that
\sigma_{n}
is
odd
.
Since
r\leq n-1
in this expression,
the summation over
q
on the right hand side of
eq.

11.23
is actually
an empty sum, so zero, and we get simply

(-1)^{n+1}\sum_{r=0}^{n-1}\bar{\varepsilon}_{n-1-r}^{(n-1)}v_{n-1}(1)\otimes v_{n}(1)\otimes u_{n}\big(x^{r}\big)\otimes v_{n}\big(x^{s}\big).

Next we apply
\id\otimes\id\otimes\operatorname{ev}_{n}
.
We must have that
r+s\geq n
so
r\geq n-s
, and the final expression is

(-1)^{n+1}\sum_{r=n-s}^{n-1}\bar{\varepsilon}_{n-1-r}^{(n-1)}v_{n-1}(1)\otimes v_{n}(1)\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{r+s-n}=(-1)^{n+1}\sum_{r=0}^{s-1}\bar{\varepsilon}_{r}^{(n-1)}v_{n-1}(1)\otimes v_{n}(1)\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{s-1-r}.

Applying
\big(c_{(1)^{2}}\otimes\id\big)^{-1}
as is required for
eq.

11.27
, we get

(-1)^{n+1}\sum_{r=0}^{s-1}\bar{\varepsilon}_{r}^{(n-1)}v_{n-1;(1^{2})}(1)\bar{\eta}^{(n+1)}_{s-1-r}.

There is a sign change of
(-1)^{r}

due to the parity shift
(n-1)\#2\equiv 1\pmod{2}
. Also
we have that
\sum_{r=0}^{s-1}(-1)^{r}\varepsilon_{r}^{(n-1)}\eta^{(n+1)}_{s-1-r}=\operatorname{sh}_{n-1}\big(\eta^{(2)}_{s-1}\big)
by a similar argument to the proof of
lemma

6.10
.
So this is
(-1)^{n+1}v_{n-1;(1^{2})}\big(\eta^{(2)}_{s-1}\big)

exactly as in
eq.

11.27
.
∎

12.
Singular Rouquier complex

Throughout the section, we fix
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
The graded
(Q,\Pi)
-2-supercategory

\mathpzc{OGBim}_{\ell}

categorifies the locally unital
\mathbb{Z}[q,q^{-1}]^{\pi}
-algebra that is
the image of
\mathbf{U}_{q,\pi}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})

in its representation on

\mathbf{V}(-\ell)
, notation as at the end of
section

3
.
To make this statement precise, let

K_{0}(OH_{n}^{\ell})
be the Grothendieck group of

OH_{n}^{\ell}
, which we identify with

K_{0}(OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pg\underline{smod}}})
.
Since
OH_{n}^{\ell}
is positively graded with
degree 0 component that is the ground field
\mathbb{F}
,
this is nothing more than the free
\mathbb{Z}[q,q^{-1}]^{\pi}
-module
generated by the isomorphism class
[OH_{n}^{\ell}]

of the regular module, with the actions of
\pi
and
q

induced by the parity and degree shift functors
\Pi
and
Q
, respectively.
So we can identify

(12.1)

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}(-\ell)

\displaystyle\equiv\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\ell}K_{0}(OH_{n}^{\ell}),

\displaystyle b_{n}^{\ell}

\displaystyle\equiv[OH_{n}^{\ell}].

The following matches up the action of generators of

\mathbf{U}_{q,\pi}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})

on
\mathbf{V}(-\ell)
with
endomorphisms of the Grothendieck group
induced by tensoring with odd Grassmannian bimodules.
