This follows from the relations
eqs.

13.2
and
13.10
,
with the signs in
eqs.

13.32
and
13.33

accounting for the difference between these and our preferred relations for
ONH_{n}

from
eqs.

5.1
,
5.4
,
5.2
,
5.5
,
5.3
and
5.6
.
Another consequence of
theorem

13.5
is that
both
\rho_{d}^{(k)}
and
\lambda_{d}^{(k)}

are
injective
.

Remark 13.8
.

On comparing with
eqs.

13.22
and
13.23
, it follows that
the composition of
\rho_{d}^{(2n-\ell)}

with the homomorphism

\End_{\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}(E^{d}1_{k})^{{\operatorname{sop}}}\rightarrow\End_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}{\text{-}}OH_{n}^{\ell}}\big(Q^{-nd-\binom{d}{2}}U_{n+d-1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d-1}^{\ell}}\cdots\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}U_{n}^{\ell}\big)^{\operatorname{sop}}

induced by the 2-superfunctor

\Psi_{\ell}
is equal to
the anti-homomorphism
\rho_{(1^{d});n}
from
eq.

11.19
(up to a degree
shift).
One can check similarly starting from
lemma

11.8
that the composition
of
\lambda_{d}^{(2n-\ell)}

with the homomorphism induced by
\Psi_{\ell}

is equal to the homomorphism
\lambda_{n;(1^{d})}
from
eq.

11.20
(up
to degree shift).

To conclude the section, we explain how to define divided
powers.
In
\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))
,
there are 1-morphisms

(13.34)

\displaystyle E^{(d)}1_{k}:=Q^{\binom{d}{2}}\Big(E^{d}1_{k},\rho^{(k)}_{d}((\xi\omega)_{d})\Big)

\displaystyle:k\rightarrow k+2d,

(13.35)

\displaystyle 1_{k}F^{(d)}:=Q^{\binom{d}{2}}\Big(1_{k}F^{d},\lambda^{(k)}_{d}((\omega\xi)_{d})\Big)

\displaystyle:k+2d\rightarrow k.

By
lemma

5.8

plus
eq.

5.29
, we have that

(13.36)

\displaystyle E^{d}1_{k}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in S_{d}}Q^{2\ell(w)-\binom{d}{2}}\Pi^{\ell(w)}E^{(d)}1_{k},

\displaystyle 1_{k}F^{d}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in S_{d}}Q^{2\ell(w)-\binom{d}{2}}\Pi^{\ell(w)}1_{k}F^{(d)}.

In view of
eq.

3.3
, it follows that
E^{(d)}1_{k}

and
F^{(d)}1_{k}
categorify the divided powers

eq.

3.12
, i.e., the isomorphism classes of the former 1-morphisms
under the isomorphism from

corollary

13.6
give the latter elements of
\mathbf{U}_{q,\pi}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
.
Note
E^{(d)}1_{k}
and
1_{k}F^{(d)}
are
obtained by upshifting
the bottom degree summands of
E^{d}1_{k}
and
1_{k}F^{d}
.
It is also useful to have available the following, which are
downshifts of the top degree summands:

(13.37)

\displaystyle\overline{E}^{(d)}1_{k}:=Q^{-\binom{d}{2}}\Big(E^{d}1_{k},\rho^{(k)}_{d}((\omega\xi)_{d})\Big)

\displaystyle:k\rightarrow k+2d,

(13.38)

\displaystyle 1_{k}\overline{F}^{(d)}:=Q^{-\binom{d}{2}}\Big(1_{k}F^{d},\lambda^{(k)}_{d}((\xi\omega)_{d})\Big)

\displaystyle:k+2d\rightarrow k.

These categorify the elements
\overline{E}^{(d)}1_{k}

and
1_{k}\overline{F}^{(d)}

of
\mathbf{U}_{q,\pi}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
from
eq.

3.13
since,
by
lemma

5.8

plus
eq.

5.29
again, we have that

(13.39)

\displaystyle E^{d}1_{k}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in S_{d}}Q^{\binom{d}{2}-2\ell(w)}\Pi^{\ell(w)}\overline{E}^{(d)}1_{k},

\displaystyle 1_{k}F^{d}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in S_{d}}Q^{\binom{d}{2}-2\ell(w)}\Pi^{\ell(w)}1_{k}\overline{F}^{(d)}.

Mirroring
eq.

3.14
, we have that

(13.40)

\displaystyle\overline{E}^{(d)}1_{k}

\displaystyle\simeq\Pi^{\binom{d}{2}}E^{(d)}1_{k},

\displaystyle 1_{k}\overline{F}^{(d)}

\displaystyle\simeq\Pi^{\binom{d}{2}}1_{k}F^{(d)}.

This follows because the idempotents

(\omega\xi)_{d}
and
(\xi\omega)_{d}
are conjugate
as discussed after
eq.

5.30
10
10

10

It could also be deduced from
eqs.

13.36
and
13.39

using Krull-Schmidt, but we prefer the argument given since it constructs the isomorphism explicitly.
.

Lemma 13.9
.

In
\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))
,
the 1-morphism

Q^{-d(k+d)}\Pi^{\binom{d}{2}}1_{k}F^{(d)}
is right dual to
E^{(d)}1_{k}
,
and the 1-morphism

Q^{d(k+d)}\Pi^{d(k+d)+\binom{d}{2}}E^{(d)}1_{k-2d}
is
right dual to
F^{(d)}1_{k}
.

Proof.

We first show that

Q^{-d(k+d)}1_{k}F^{(d)}
is right dual to
E^{(d)}1_{k}

in the
(Q,\Pi)
-envelope
\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))
.
By
eq.

13.3
,

Q^{-k-1}1_{k}F
is right dual to
E1_{k}
.
Hence,
Q^{-d(k+d)}Q^{-\binom{d}{2}}1_{k}F^{d}
is right dual to
Q^{\binom{d}{2}}E^{d}1_{k}
.
By definition,
E^{(d)}1_{k}
is the summand of

Q^{\binom{d}{2}}E^{d}1_{k}
defined by the idempotent

Q^{\binom{d}{2}}\rho_{d}^{(k)}\big((\xi\omega)_{d}\big)

and
Q^{-d(k+d)}1_{k}\overline{F}^{(d)}
is the summand of

Q^{-d(k+d)}Q^{-\binom{d}{2}}1_{k}F^{d}
defined by the
the idempotent

Q^{-d(k+d)}Q^{-\binom{d}{2}}\lambda_{d}^{(k)}\big((\xi\omega)_{d}\big)
.
Now we observe using
lemma

2.1
(2),

eq.

13.8
and
eq.

5.29

that
Q^{-d(k+d)}Q^{-\binom{d}{2}}\lambda_{d}^{(k)}\big((\xi\omega)_{d}\big)

is the right mate of
Q^{\binom{d}{2}}\rho_{d}^{(k)}\big((\xi\omega)_{d}\big)
.
Hence, we get that

Q^{-d(k+d)}1_{k}\overline{F}^{(d)}
is right dual to

E^{(d)}1_{k}
. It remains to apply

eq.

13.40
to pass from

Q^{-d(k+d)}1_{k}\overline{F}^{(d)}

to

Q^{-d(k+d)}\Pi^{\binom{d}{2}}1_{k}F^{(d)}
.

The proof that

Q^{d(k+d)}\Pi^{d(k+d)+\binom{d}{2}}E^{(d)}1_{k}
is
right dual to
1_{k}F^{(d)}
is similar using
eq.

13.11
instead of
eq.

13.3
.
By
eq.

13.11
and
lemma

2.1
(1),

Q^{k+1}\Pi^{k+1}E1_{k}
is right dual to
1_{k}F
.
Hence,

Q^{d(k+dx)-\binom{d}{2}}\Pi^{d(k+d)}E^{d}1_{k}
is right dual to

Q^{\binom{d}{2}}1_{k}F^{d}
.
Since
eq.

13.18
is more complicated than

eq.

13.8
, it is no longer true that
the idempotent

Q^{d(k+d)-\binom{d}{2}}\Pi^{d(k+d)}\rho_{d}^{(k)}\big((\xi\omega)_{d}\big)

is
equal
to the right mate of the idempotent

Q^{\binom{d}{2}}\lambda_{d}^{(k)}\big((\xi\omega)_{d}\big)
,
but these two idempotents are conjugate via even degree 0 units.
This follows by the Krull-Schmidt theorem applied to the finite-dimensional algebra that is the even degree 0 component of
ONH_{d}\otimes R
.
Hence,
Q^{d(k+d)}\Pi^{d(k+d)}\overline{E}^{(d)}1_{k}
is right dual to

1_{k}F^{(d)}
.
It remains to appeal to
eq.

13.40
one more time.
∎

14.
Some graded 2-representation theory

In this section, we develop some 2-representation theory of
the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
2-category
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
from

definition

13.1
. We work throughout in the graded setting, but all the
definitions and results here have analogs with the
\mathbb{Z}
-grading forgotten.
The following is modelled on
[
R1
, Def. 5.1.1]
.

Definition 14.1
.

By a
graded 2-representation

\mathpzc{V}
of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
, we mean
a strict graded 2-superfunctor

\mathpzc{V}:\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\rightarrow\mathpzc{gsCat}
.
Decoding the definition,
\mathpzc{V}
consists of the following data:

•

a graded supercategory
\mathpzc{V}
with a
given decomposition
into
weight subcategories

\mathpzc{V}=\coprod_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathpzc{V}_{k}
(or

\mathpzc{V}=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathpzc{V}_{k}
when
\mathpzc{V}
is additive);

•

graded superfunctors
E:\mathpzc{V}\rightarrow\mathpzc{V}

and
F:\mathpzc{V}\rightarrow\mathpzc{V}

such that
E|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}:\mathpzc{V}_{k}\rightarrow\mathpzc{V}_{k+2}

and
F|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}:\mathpzc{V}_{k}\rightarrow\mathpzc{V}_{k-2}
for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
;

•

graded supernatural transformations

x:E\Rightarrow E
and
\tau:E^{2}\Rightarrow E^{2}

which are odd of degrees 2
and
-2
, respectively;

•

(inhomogeneous) graded supernatural transformations

\eta:\Id\Rightarrow FE
and
\varepsilon:EF\Rightarrow\Id

whose
restrictions

\eta:\Id_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}\Rightarrow FE|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}

and

\varepsilon:EF|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k+2}}\Rightarrow\Id_{\mathpzc{V}_{k+2}}

are even of degrees
k+1

and
-k-1
, respectively.

Then there are the axioms:

•

the relations from
eq.

13.2
hold:

\tau\circ\tau=0
,
(\tau E)\circ(E\tau)\circ(\tau E)=(E\tau)\circ(\tau E)\circ(E\tau)
and

(Ex)\circ\tau+(xE)\circ\tau=(xE)\circ\tau+\tau\circ(Ex)=E^{2}
;

•

\eta
and
\varepsilon
satisfy the zig-zag relations:

(F\varepsilon)\circ(\eta F)=F

and
(\varepsilon E)\circ(E\eta)=E
(equivalently,
they define units and counits of adjunctions
making
Q^{-k-1}F|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k+2}}
into a
right adjoint to
E|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}
for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
);

•

letting
\sigma:=(FE\varepsilon)\circ(F\tau F)\circ(\eta EF):EF\Rightarrow FE

be the image of the rightward crossing under
\mathpzc{V}
,
the following inhomogeneous matrices of
supernatural transformations are isomorphisms:

\displaystyle\left(\>\sigma\qquad\varepsilon\qquad\varepsilon\circ(xF)\qquad\cdots\qquad\varepsilon\circ(xF)^{k-1}\right)^{T}

\displaystyle:EF|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}\Rightarrow FE|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}\oplus\Id_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}^{\oplus k}

for
k\geq 0

\displaystyle\left(\>\sigma\qquad\eta\qquad(Fx)\circ\eta\qquad\cdots\qquad(Fx)^{-k-1}\circ\eta\right)

\displaystyle:EF|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}\oplus\Id_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}^{\oplus(-k)}\Rightarrow FE|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}

for
k\leq 0

There are natural notations of (full)
sub-2-representations
(which are called “invariant ideals” in
[
BD
,
S\!
4.2]
),

quotient 2-representations
, and

morphisms
of graded 2-representations.
The latter definition, which is the super analog of
[
R1
, Def. 2.3]
,
is equivalent to the following, which is similar to the formulation adopted in

[
CR
, Sec. 5.2.1]
; the terminology being used is the same as in

[
BD
, Def. 4.6]
(and actually goes back to Ben Webster).

Definition 14.2
.

Let
\mathpzc{V}
and
\mathpzc{W}
be two graded 2-representations of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
.
A
strongly equivariant graded superfunctor

\Omega:\mathpzc{V}\rightarrow\mathpzc{W}

is a graded superfunctor such that
\Omega|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}:\mathpzc{V}_{k}\rightarrow\mathpzc{W}_{k}
for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
plus a degree 0 even graded
supernatural isomorphism

\zeta:E\Omega\mathrel{{\mathop{\Rightarrow}\limits}}\Omega E
,
such that the following holds

•

the supernatural transformation
(F\Omega\varepsilon)\circ(F\zeta F)\circ(\eta\Omega F):\Omega F\Rightarrow F\Omega
is invertible;

•

we have that
(\Omega x)\circ\zeta=\zeta\circ(x\Omega)
;

•

we have that
(\Omega\tau)\circ(\zeta E)\circ(E\zeta)=(\zeta E)\circ(E\zeta)\circ(\tau\Omega)
.

A
strongly equivariant graded superequivalence
is
a strongly equivariant graded superfunctor which is also a superequivalence of
supercategories.

Remark 14.3
.

For strongly equivariant graded superequivalences, the first axiom in
definition

14.2
actually
holds automatically; see
[
BD
, Rem. 4.8]
where this is explained (in the purely even setting).
Also in
[
BD
]
, the diagrammatic interpretation of these definitions
is discussed, which we still find helpful.

Remark 14.4
.

There is an obvious way to make the composition
of two strongly
equivariant graded superfunctors into a strongly equivariant graded superfunctor in its own
right.
Also the identity functor
\Id
is strongly equivariant with

\zeta:=1_{E}
.
So there is a category
\mathpzc{Rep}(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))

consisting of graded
2-representations and strongly equivariant graded superfunctors.

Usually, the graded supercategories
\mathpzc{V}_{k}
in
a graded 2-representation
\mathpzc{V}
will have some extra structure,
such as being additive or
\Pi
-complete.
We are mainly interested here in what we call

graded Karoubian 2-representations
.
By definition, this means a graded 2-representation
\mathpzc{V}
such that,
for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, the weight subcategory

\mathpzc{V}_{k}
is additive and
\Pi
-complete,
and the underlying ordinary category

\underline{\mathpzc{V}}_{k}
is idempotent complete.
Any graded 2-representation
\mathpzc{V}
can be upgraded to a Karoubian graded
2-representation by passing to its graded super Karoubi envelope
\GSKar(\mathpzc{V})
.

Given a graded Karoubian 2-representation

\mathpzc{V}
, the underlying graded 2-superfunctor
from
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
to
\mathpzc{V}

extends canonically to a graded
2-superfunctor from the
graded super Karoubi envelope
\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))
to
\mathpzc{V}
.
The direct sum over all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
of the images
under this graded 2-superfunctor
of the 1-morphisms

E^{(d)}1_{k}
and
F^{(d)}1_{k}

from
eqs.

13.34
and
13.35

give graded superfunctors

(14.1)

E^{(d)},F^{(d)}:\mathpzc{V}\rightarrow\mathpzc{V}.

By
eq.

13.36
, we have that

(14.2)

\displaystyle E^{d}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in S_{d}}\Pi^{\ell(w)}Q^{2\ell(w)-\binom{d}{2}}E^{(d)},

\displaystyle F^{d}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{w\in S_{d}}\Pi^{\ell(w)}Q^{2\ell(w)-\binom{d}{2}}F^{(d)}.

lemma

13.9
implies that

Q^{-d(k+d)}\Pi^{\binom{d}{2}}F^{(d)}|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k+2d}}
is right adjoint to

E^{(d)}|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}

and
Q^{d(k+d)}\Pi^{d(k+d)+\binom{d}{2}}E^{(d)}|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k-2d}}

is right adjoint to
F^{(d)}|_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}
,
with
units and counits of adjunction that are defined by images of 2-morphisms in
\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))
.

A graded 2-representation
\mathpzc{V}

is said to be
integrable
if
E
and
F
are locally nilpotent, i.e.,
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and any
M\in\mathpzc{V}_{k}

there is some
n\geq 0
such that
E^{n}M=F^{n}M=0
.
Also, for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, a
lowest weight object of weight
-\ell

means an object
M\in\mathpzc{V}_{-\ell}
such that
FM=0
.

Example 14.5
.

Suppose that
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. By

theorem

13.2
, there is an integrable
graded Karoubian 2-representation

(14.3)

OH^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}:=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\ell}OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}

with the weight
k
subcategory

(OH^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}})_{k}
equal to

OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}
if
k=2n-\ell

for
0\leq n\leq\ell
,
or the trivial (zero) graded supercategory otherwise.
Other data is as follows.

•

The graded superfunctors
E
and
F

are
Q^{-n}U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}-

on the weight subcategory
OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}

and
Q^{3n+1-\ell}V_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}-

on the weight subcategory
OH_{n+1}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}
,
respectively,
assuming
0\leq n<\ell
. On all other weight subcategories,
E
and
F
are zero.

•

The graded supernatural transformations
x
and
\tau
are defined
by the supernatural transformations
\rho_{(1);n}(x_{1})\otimes\id

viewed as elements

\operatorname{gsEnd}\big(Q^{-n}U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}-\big)_{2,{\bar{1}}}
and
the supernatural transformations

-\rho_{(1^{2});n}(\tau_{1})\otimes\id
viewed as elements of

\operatorname{gsEnd}\big(Q^{-2n-1}U_{n+1}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+1}^{\ell}}Q^{-n}U_{n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}-\big)_{-2,{\bar{1}}}
, respectively,
for all admissible
n
.

•

The graded supernatural transformations
\eta
and
\varepsilon
are given by the appropriate
counit and unit from

theorem

11.3
.

•

The homomorphisms induced by
eqs.

13.32
and
13.33

are equal to
eqs.

11.19
and
11.20
thanks to
remark

13.8
.

•

For
0\leq n\leq n+d\leq\ell
,
we have that

E^{(d)}|_{OH_{n}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}}\simeq Q^{-dn}U_{(d);n}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n}^{\ell}}-

and
F^{(d)}|_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}}\simeq Q^{-d(\ell-3n-2d+1)}V_{n;(d)}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{n+d}^{\ell}}-
;
cf.
theorem

12.1
.

We point out also by
theorem

12.1
that

K_{0}(OH^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pg\underline{smod}}}\big)

is naturally identified with the
\mathbf{U}_{q,\pi}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
-module

\mathbf{V}(-\ell)
, and
OH_{0}^{\ell}
is a lowest weight object of weight
-\ell
.

Now we come to one of the key constructions introduced by
Rouquier in
[
R1
]
in the purely even case, the construction of

cyclotomic quotients
.
For any
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
,
there is a graded 2-representation
\mathpzc{R}(\ell)

with

(14.4)

\mathpzc{R}(\ell)_{k}:=\mathpzc{Hom}_{\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}(\ell,k)

for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
viewed as a graded 2-representation of the graded 2-supercategory

\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
in an obvious way.
For example, the graded superfunctor

E|_{\mathpzc{R}(\ell)_{k}}:\mathpzc{R}(\ell)_{k}\rightarrow\mathpzc{R}(\ell)_{k+2}

is defined by horizontally composing on the left
with the 1-morphism
E1_{k}
, and the
supernatural transformation
x:E|_{\mathpzc{R}(\ell)_{k}}\Rightarrow E|_{\mathpzc{R}(\ell)_{k}}

is induced by the 2-endomorphism

\hbox to19.74pt{\vbox to16.33pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.23424pt\lower-7.02873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}:E1_{k}\Rightarrow E1_{k}
.
The graded 2-representation
\mathpzc{R}(\ell)

has the following universal property.

Lemma 14.6
.

Given any graded 2-representation
\mathpzc{V}
and any
M\in\mathpzc{V}_{\ell}

there is a canonical strongly equivariant graded superfunctor

\omega_{M}:\mathpzc{R}(\ell)\rightarrow\mathpzc{V}
taking the
object
1_{\ell}
of
\mathpzc{R}(\ell)_{\ell}
to
M
.

We define the
universal
graded 2-representation of lowest weight
-\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
,
denoted
\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)
, to be the quotient 2-representation

\mathpzc{R}(-\ell)/\mathpzc{I}
,
where
\mathpzc{I}
here is the sub-2-representation of
\mathpzc{R}(-\ell)

generated by
\Big\downarrow\>{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\scriptstyle-\ell}
(the identity endomorphism of the object
F1_{-\ell}
).
We denote the lowest weight object of
\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)_{-\ell}

arising from the object
1_{-\ell}\in\mathpzc{R}(-\ell)_{-\ell}

by
\overline{1}_{-\ell}
, and call this the
canonical
lowest weight object
. It is a generating object for
\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)
.
The identity endomorphism of

\overline{1}_{-\ell}
is equal to the image of
the bottom bubble

\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\scriptstyle{-\ell}
{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\bullet}\!\!\!\circ
\scriptstyle{-\ell-1}
, i.e., the image of
1\in R

under the homomorphism
eq.

13.12
.
If
\ell<0
, this bubble is not a fake bubble, so it
belongs to
\mathpzc{I}
.
This shows that the graded supercategory

\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)
is trivial if
\ell<0
.
Thus,
\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)
is only interesting if
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
i.e., it is a dominant weight for
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
.
The following, the universal property of
\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)
,
follows immediately from
lemma

14.6
and
the universal property of quotients.

Lemma 14.7
.

Let
\mathpzc{V}
be any graded 2-representation
of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and

M\in\mathpzc{V}_{-\ell}
be a lowest weight object.
The superfunctor
\omega_{M}:\mathpzc{R}(-\ell)\rightarrow\mathpzc{V}

from
lemma

14.6
induces a strongly equivariant graded superfunctor

\Omega_{M}:\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)\rightarrow\mathpzc{V}

taking
\overline{1}_{-\ell}
to
M
.

There is a more sophisticated version of
lemma

14.7
, which is analogous to

[
R1
, Prop. 5.6]
. To formulate this, we need one more preliminary lemma.

Lemma 14.8
.

The homomorphism
11
11

11

In fact,
\beta_{-\ell}
is
itself an isomorphism thanks
to
theorem

13.5
, but this result is not needed here.

\beta_{-\ell}:R\rightarrow\End_{\mathpzc{R}(-\ell)}(1_{-\ell})

from
eq.

13.12
induces an isomorphism

\bar{\beta}_{-\ell}:R_{\ell}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\End_{\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)}(\overline{1}_{-\ell})
.

Proof.

The bubble

\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\scriptstyle{-\ell}
{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\bullet}\!\!\!\circ
\scriptstyle{r-\ell-1}
belongs to
\mathpzc{I}
for
r>\ell
.
Up to a sign, the composition of
\beta_{-\ell}
with the canonical
map
\End_{\mathpzc{R}(-\ell)}(1_{-\ell})\twoheadrightarrow\End_{\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)}(\overline{1}_{-\ell})

takes
\dot{\varepsilon}_{r}\in R
to the
the image of this bubble, which is zero.
We deduce
that this homomorphism factors through the quotient
R_{\ell}
of
R

to induce

\bar{\beta}_{-\ell}
.
Moreover,
\bar{\beta}_{-\ell}
is surjective
since
\beta_{-\ell}
is surjective by the “easy” part of
theorem

13.5
.

To show that
\bar{\beta}_{-\ell}
is also injective, we use the following
diagram of graded supercategories and superfunctors:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\END_{\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})}(-\ell)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{0}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod-}}OH_{0}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)_{-\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{0}^{\ell}{\operatorname{-gsMod}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ev}_{\overline{1}_{-\ell}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes_{OH_{0}^{\ell}}OH_{0}^{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{OH_{0}^{\ell}}}

Here, the top map comes from
theorem

13.2
,
the left hand vertical superfunctor
is given by evaluating
on the object
\overline{1}_{-\ell}
,
and the right hand vertical superfunctor is given by
tensoring with the lowest weight object
OH_{0}^{\ell}
.
The way the bottom superfunctor

\Omega_{OH_{0}^{\ell}}
is defined in
lemma

14.7

ensures that this diagram commutes strictly.
It follows that the middle square
in the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathpzc{R}(-\ell)_{-\ell}}(1_{-\ell})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{OH_{0}^{\ell}{\text{-}}OH_{0}^{\ell}}(OH_{0}^{\ell})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)_{-\ell}}(\overline{1}_{-\ell})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{OH_{0}^{\ell}{\text{-}}}(OH_{0}^{\ell})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces OH_{0}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{-\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta\mapsto\theta\otimes\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\beta}_{-\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{OH_{0}^{\ell}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi\mapsto\phi(1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{0}^{\ell}}

corollary

13.4

shows that
the composition
R\rightarrow R_{\ell}

around the northeast boundary of this diagram
is equal to the canonical quotient map.
Hence, the composition
R_{\ell}\rightarrow R_{\ell}

of the three maps at the bottom of the diagram is the identity.
This implies that
\bar{\beta}_{-\ell}
is injective.
∎

Any morphism space

\Hom_{\mathpzc{R}(-\ell)}(X,Y)
in
\mathpzc{R}(-\ell)

can be viewed as a right
R
-supermodule
so that
{\mathrel{{\mathop{c}\limits}}}\in R
acts by horizontally composing on the right
with
\beta_{-\ell}({\mathrel{{\mathop{c}\limits}}})
.
This induces a structure of right
R_{\ell}
-supermodule
on any morphism space
\Hom_{\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)}(X,Y)
; cf.
the first paragraph of the proof of
lemma

14.8
.
Given a graded
R_{\ell}
-superalgebra
A
,
we let
\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)\otimes_{R_{\ell}}A

be the graded supercategory with the same
objects as
\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)
and
morphism spaces
\Hom_{\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)\otimes_{R_{\ell}}A}(X,Y):=\Hom_{\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)}(X,Y)\otimes_{R_{\ell}}A
.
This is naturally a graded 2-representation
of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
in its own right.

Theorem 14.9
.

Let
\mathpzc{V}
be any graded 2-representation
of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and

M\in\mathpzc{V}_{-\ell}
be any lowest weight object.
The strongly equivariant graded
superfunctor
\Omega_{M}:\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)\rightarrow\mathpzc{V}
from
lemma

14.7
extends to a fully faithful
strongly equivariant graded superfunctor

\Omega_{M}\otimes\id:\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)\otimes_{R_{\ell}}\End_{\mathpzc{V}}(M)\rightarrow\mathpzc{V}
.

Proof.

Let
A:=\End_{\mathpzc{V}}(M)
for short.
The graded superfunctor
\Omega_{M}
extends to

\Omega_{M}\otimes\id
by the universal property of tensor product.
To see that the resulting graded superfunctor is fully faithful,
we must show that it defines an isomorphism

\Hom_{\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)\otimes_{R_{\ell}}A}(X,Y)\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}\Hom_{\mathpzc{V}}(X,Y)
for objects of any weight subcategory of

\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)\otimes_{R_{\ell}}A
.
This is clear if
X=Y=\overline{1}_{-\ell}
.
The result in general then follows by the (now standard) technique
explained in the proof of
[
R1
, Lem. 5.4, Prop. 5.6]
.
∎

Corollary 14.10
.

For
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, let
OH^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}
be the graded Karoubian 2-representation from
example

14.5
,
and let
\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell))
be the graded super Karoubi envelope of

\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)
, which is another graded
Karoubian 2-representation.
The strongly equivariant graded superfunctor

\Omega_{OH_{0}^{\ell}}:\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)\rightarrow OH^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}

associated to the lowest weight object

OH_{0}^{\ell}

induces a strongly equivariant graded superequivalence

\Xi_{\ell}:\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell))\rightarrow OH^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}
.

Proof.

In view of
lemmas

14.8
and
14.9
,

\Omega_{OH_{0}^{\ell}}
is fully faithful.
This extends by the universal property of the graded super Karoubi envelope
to give
a fully faithful strongly equivariant graded superfunctor

\Xi_{\ell}:\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell))\rightarrow OH^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}
.
To see that
\Xi_{\ell}
is a graded superequivalence, it remains to check that it is dense.
This follows because

E^{(n)}OH_{0}^{\ell}\simeq U_{(n);0}^{\ell}\otimes_{OH_{0}^{\ell}}OH_{0}^{\ell}\simeq OH_{n}^{\ell},

the last isomorphism following
since

U_{(n);0}^{\ell}
is free of rank 1 as a graded left
OH_{n}^{\ell}
-supermodule
by
lemma

10.4
(2).
∎

We record one more basic lemma, which is analogous to the first part of
[
R1
, Lem. 5.2]
.

Lemma 14.11
.

Let
\mathpzc{V}
be an integrable Karoubian graded 2-representation of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
.
Let
N
be an object of
\mathpzc{V}_{k}
for some
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.
If

\Hom_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}(E^{n}M,N)=0
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,

n\geq 0
such that
k=2n-\ell

and all lowest weight objects
M\in\mathpzc{V}_{-\ell}
, then
N=0
.

Proof.

Suppose that
N\neq 0
.
By integrability, there exists
n\geq 0
such that

F^{n}N\neq 0
and
F^{n+1}N=0
.
This means that
M:=F^{n}N
is a non-zero lowest weight object
of
\mathpzc{V}_{-\ell}
for
\ell=k-2n\in\mathbb{N}
.
By assumption, we have that
\Hom_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}(E^{n}M,N)=0
.
Hence, by adjunction,

\End_{\mathpzc{V}_{-\ell}}(M)=\Hom_{\mathpzc{V}_{-\ell}}(M,F^{n}N)\simeq\Hom_{\mathpzc{V}_{k}}(E^{n}M,N)=0.

It follows that
1_{M}=0
, so
M=0
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Remark 14.12
.

There is more still to be done here. For example, Rouquier continues in
[
R1
, Sec. 5.1.4]
to construct
a Jordan-Hölder series in an arbitrary integrable
Karoubian 2-representation, and this result assuredly
carries over to our setting.
There is also a good theory of

locally finite Abelian
2-representations of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
,
including an analog of
[
CR
, Prop. 5.20]
which implies that
the irreducible objects of such a 2-representation
can be given the structure of a crystal in the sense of Kashiwara.
It would be worthwhile to extend
[
CR
, Th. 5.27]
(which is
a special case of Rouquier’s “control by
K_{0}
” from
[
R1
, Th. 5.22]
)
to this setting. This would pave the way to more applications involving representations of the supergroup
Q(n)
and
the Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{q}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.
In the ordinary case, an alternative approach by-passing control by
K_{0}
was developed in
[
BSW
]
, which we expect
should also have an interesting and non-trivial super analog.
Another direction we would like to investigate further is
to extend
theorems

13.2
and
13.5
from odd
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
to
the super Kac-Moody 2-category
associated to
“odd
\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1}
”,
thereby giving an odd analog of the 2-representation
of
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
constructed
in
[
KL
]
.

15.
The odd analog of the Rickard complex

Let
\mathpzc{V}
be a graded 2-supercategory.
The notation
\operatorname{Ch}^{b}(\mathpzc{V})
denotes the graded supercategory of bounded cochain complexes
and chain maps in
\mathpzc{V}
;
differentials in a cochain complex are assumed to be even of degree 0
but we allow
chain maps whose components are inhomogeneous.
Also
K^{b}(\mathpzc{V})
is the homotopy category, which
is a graded supercategory
with the same objects as

\operatorname{Ch}^{b}(\mathpzc{V})
and morphisms that are chain homotopy equivalence classes of chain maps;
chain homotopies are again required
to be even of degree 0.
If
\mathpzc{V}
is an
integrable graded Karoubian 2-representation of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})

as in the previous section,
both
\operatorname{Ch}^{b}(\mathpzc{V})
and
K^{b}(\mathpzc{V})
are themselves integrable graded Karoubian 2-representations
of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
in a natural way.

Fix
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The
odd Rickard complex

\Theta_{k}
,
so-called because it is the odd analog of the complex in
[
CR
, Sec. 6.2]

which was
introduced originally by Rickard in the context of symmetric groups,
is the following
cochain complex in

\operatorname{Ch}\big(\mathpzc{Hom}_{\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))}(-k,k)\big)
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}\cdots\rightarrow Q^{d}E^{(k+d)}F^{(d)}1_{-k}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}Q^{d-1}E^{(k+d-1)}F^{(d-1)}1_{-k}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow E^{(k)}1_{-k}\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots&\text{if $k\geq 0$}\\
\cdots\rightarrow Q^{d}E^{(k+d)}F^{(d)}1_{-k}\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}Q^{d-1}E^{(k+d-1)}F^{(d-1)}1_{-k}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow Q^{-k}F^{(-k)}1_{-k}\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots&\text{if $k\leq 0$},\end{array}\right.

where in both cases
E^{(k+d)}F^{(d)}1_{-k}

is in cohomological degree
-d
.
The differential

\partial^{-d}:Q^{d}E^{(k+d)}F^{(d)}1_{-k}\rightarrow Q^{d-1}E^{(k+d-1)}F^{(d-1)}1_{-k}

is
the composition first of the “inclusion”
of
Q^{d}E^{(k+d)}F^{(d)}1_{-k}\rightarrow Q^{k+3d-2}E^{(k+d-1)}EFF^{(d-1)}1_{-k}

as a summand
12
12

12

The idempotent endomorphism defining

Q^{k+3d-2}E^{(k+d-1)}EFF^{(d-1)}1_{-k}
as a summand of
Q^{d}E^{k+d}F^{d}1_{-k}

decomposes as the sum of two mutually orthogonal idempotents, one of which
is the idempotent defining
Q^{d-1}E^{(k+d)}F^{(d)}1_{-k}
.

of
E^{(k+d-1)}EFF^{(d-1)}1_{-k}
,
then

Q^{k+3d-2}E^{(k+d-1)}\varepsilon F^{(d-1)}:Q^{k+3d-2}E^{(k+d-1)}EFF^{(d-1)}1_{-k}\rightarrow Q^{d-1}E^{(k+d-1)}F^{(d-1)}1_{-k}
.
Note this is even of degree 0 as required.
The following checks that it is a cochain complex.

Lemma 15.1
.

We have that
\partial^{-d+1}\circ\partial^{-d}=0
for all
d
.

Proof.

Ignoring gradings for brevity,
it suffices to show that
the composition

E^{(2)}F^{(2)}1_{-k-2d+4}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}E^{2}F^{2}1_{-k-2d+4}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}E1_{-k-2d+4}F\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}1_{-k-2d+4}

is zero.
The identity endomorphism of

E^{(2)}F^{(2)}1_{-k-2d+4}

is

\rho_{2}^{(-k-2d)}(x_{1}\tau_{1})\lambda_{2}^{(-k-2d)}(\tau_{1}x_{1})=\big(\rho_{2}^{(-k-2d)}(\tau_{1})\lambda_{2}^{(-k-2d)}(\tau_{1})\big)\circ\big(\rho_{2}^{(-k-2d)}(x_{1})\lambda_{2}^{(-k-2d)}(x_{1})\big).

The composition of this with

\varepsilon\circ(E\varepsilon F)
is zero:

\hbox to63.1pt{\vbox to51.62pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.04544pt\lower-14.42638pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

∎

Remark 15.2
.

Note
lemma

15.1
plus
theorem

13.2
implies
lemma

12.5
.
So the proof of that lemma was actually unnecessary
(as, by association,
was
lemma

11.10
) but we included it to make
section

12

independent of the subsequent material.

Suppose now that
\mathpzc{V}
is an integrable graded Karoubian 2-representation
of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
.
Given any cochain complex
C\in\operatorname{Ch}^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{-k})
,
we can apply the complex of graded superfunctors that is the image under

\mathpzc{V}
of the odd Rickard complex
\Theta_{k}
to obtain a double complex.
The associated total complex is again bounded thanks to the integrability assumption.
This construction defines a graded superfunctor

\operatorname{Ch}^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{-k})\rightarrow\operatorname{Ch}^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})
. Passing to the quotient

K^{b}(\mathpzc{V})
of
\operatorname{Ch}^{b}(\mathpzc{V})
,
we obtain from this a graded superfunctor

(15.1)

\mathpzc{V}(\Theta_{k}):K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{-k})\rightarrow K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k}).

Lemma 15.3
.

Let
\mathpzc{V}
be the graded 2-representation
OH^{\ell}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}
from

example

14.5
. The image of the odd Rickard complex

\Theta_{k}
under
\mathpzc{V}
recovers the singular Rouqiuer complex
from
eq.

12.5
shifted globally in degree by an application of
Q^{-nk}
.

Proof.

This follows using the explicit identification of the
divided powers
E^{(d)}
and
F^{(d)}
as
endofunctors of
\mathpzc{V}
explained in
example

14.5
.
We just check that the degree shifts match correctly.
Let
n=\frac{\ell-k}{2}
and
d
be as in
definition

12.2
, so
k=\ell-2n
.
In the
-d
th cohomological degree in the odd Rickard complex,
we have

Q^{d}E^{(k+d)}F^{(d)}1_{-k}
.
In the 2-representation
\mathpzc{V}
, this acts by tensoring with the graded
superbimodule

Q^{d}\big(Q^{-(\ell-2n+d)(n-d)}U_{(k+d);n-d}^{\ell}\big)\otimes_{OH_{n-d}^{\ell}}\big(Q^{-d(\ell-3(n-d)-2d+1)}V_{n-d;(d)}^{\ell}\big)
, where
the degree shifts are as described in
example

14.5
.
The total grading shift here simplifies to

Q^{-nk}
,
so this is equal to the graded superbimodule

U_{(k+d);n-d}\otimes_{OH_{n-d}^{\ell}}V_{n-d;(d)}

in the
d
th homological degree of the singular Rouquier complex
shifted by
Q^{-nk}
.
∎

Corollary 15.4
.

For
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,

\big(\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)\big))(\Theta_{k}):K^{b}\big(\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell))_{-k}\big)\rightarrow K^{b}\big(\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell))_{k}\big)
is a graded superequivalence
inducing
T:1_{-k}\mathbf{V}(-\ell)\mathrel{{\mathop{\rightarrow}\limits}}1_{k}\mathbf{V}(-\ell)
at the level of the Grothendieck groups.

Proof.

This follows from
lemma

15.3
together with
corollary

12.4
and
corollary

14.10
.
∎

The proof of the following theorem is based on the argument in
[
R1
, Th. 5.18]
,
the main step really being
[
R1
, Lem. 5.5]
.
This was itself a generalization of
[
CR
, Th. 6.4]

which
constructed equivalences between
bounded derived categories of locally finite Abelian 2-representations.

Theorem 15.5
.

Let
\mathpzc{V}
be an integrable graded Karoubian 2-representation
of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
.
For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, the graded
superfunctor
\mathpzc{V}(\Theta_{k}):K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{-k})\rightarrow K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})
induced by the odd Rickard complex
is a graded superequivalence.

Proof.

By
lemma

13.9
, the 1-morphism
Q^{d}E^{(k+d)}F^{(d)}1_{-k}
has a right dual
in
\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))
.
Hence, we can form the right dual

\Theta^{k}
to
\Theta_{k}
, which is a cochain complex in

\operatorname{Ch}\big(\mathpzc{Hom}_{\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))}(k,-k)\big)
.
The 1-morphism in
the
d
th cohomological degree of
\Theta^{k}

is the right dual
of the 1-morphism
in the
(-d)
th cohomological degree of
\Theta_{k}
,
and the differentials in
\Theta^{k}
are the right mates
of the corresponding differentials in
\Theta_{k}
.
Let

\Theta^{k}\circ\Theta_{k}
and
\Theta_{k}\circ\Theta^{k}

be the total complexes associated to the double complexes obtained by
composing these cochain complexes.
The complex
\Theta_{k}
is bounded above, and
\Theta^{k}
is bounded below,
but neither is bounded. Consequently,
in each cohomological degree,
the total complexes

\Theta^{k}\circ\Theta_{k}
and
\Theta_{k}\circ\Theta^{k}

involve
infinite
direct sums of 1-morphisms in

\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))
,
so in fact, one needs to pass to a completion of this graded
(Q,\Pi)
-supercategory
for it to make sense. This does not cause issues since, on a given object in an
integrable graded Karoubian
2-representation, the superfunctors arising from
all but finitely many of the summands of these infinite
direct sums are zero.

Like
\Theta_{k}
, the complex
\Theta^{k}
defines a graded superfunctor
denoted
\mathpzc{V}(\Theta^{k}):K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})\rightarrow K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{-k})
. Moreover,
\mathpzc{V}(\Theta^{k})
is
right adjoint to
\mathpzc{V}(\Theta_{k})
, with
counit and unit of adjunction denoted

\displaystyle\mathpzc{V}(\varepsilon):

\displaystyle\mathpzc{V}(\Theta_{k})\circ\mathpzc{V}(\Theta^{k})\Rightarrow\Id_{K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})},

\displaystyle\mathpzc{V}(\eta):

\displaystyle\Id_{K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{-k})}\Rightarrow\mathpzc{V}(\Theta^{k})\circ\mathpzc{V}(\Theta_{k}).

This is explained in more detail in
[
CR
, Sec. 4.1.4]
.
As the notation
\mathpzc{V}(\varepsilon)
and

\mathpzc{V}(\eta)
suggests, if we identify

\mathpzc{V}(\Theta_{k})\circ\mathpzc{V}(\Theta^{k})
with
\mathpzc{V}(\Theta_{k}\circ\Theta^{k})

and

\mathpzc{V}(\Theta^{k})\circ\mathpzc{V}(\Theta_{k})
with

\mathpzc{V}(\Theta^{k}\circ\Theta_{k})
then
these even degree 0 supernatural transformations are induced by
corresponding chain maps denoted simply by

\varepsilon:\Theta_{k}\circ\Theta^{k}\Rightarrow 1_{k}

and
\eta:1_{-k}\rightarrow\Theta^{k}\circ\Theta_{k}

between cochain complexes in the completion of
\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))
.
Although not needed here, these chain maps can be seen quite explicitly; the
matrix coefficients of their components
are 2-morphisms in
\GSKar(\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2}))
that arise from the counits
and units defining the duality between
the 1-morphisms
Q^{d}E^{(k+d)}F^{(d)}1_{-k}
and their right duals.

To prove the theorem, it suffices to show that

\mathpzc{V}(\varepsilon)
and
\mathpzc{V}(\eta)
are isomorphisms.
We just explain the argument to see this in the case of

\mathpzc{V}(\varepsilon)
, since the case of
\mathpzc{V}(\eta)
is similar.
The even degree 0 graded supernatural transformation
\mathpzc{V}(\varepsilon)
is an isomorphism if and only if

\operatorname{Cone}(\mathpzc{V}(\varepsilon)_{C})=0
for all
C\in K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})
.
Now we observe that

\operatorname{Cone}(\mathpzc{V}(\varepsilon)_{C})=\mathpzc{V}(Z)(C)

where
Z:=\operatorname{Cone}(\varepsilon)
is the cone of

\varepsilon:\Theta_{k}\circ\Theta^{k}\Rightarrow 1_{k}
.
Thus, it suffices to show that
the graded superfunctor
\mathpzc{V}(Z):K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})\rightarrow K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})

is zero.

Consider
K^{b}(\mathpzc{V})
as an integrable Karoubian graded 2-representation in its own right.
In this paragraph, we show that

\mathpzc{V}(Z)(E^{n}C)=0

in
K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})

for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
n\geq 0

such that
k=2n-\ell
,
and all lowest weight objects
C\in K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{-\ell})
.
To see this, we apply
lemma

14.7
(with
\mathpzc{V}
replaced by

K^{b}(\mathpzc{V})
)
to get a
strongly equivariant graded superfunctor
\Omega_{C}:\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)\rightarrow K^{b}(\mathpzc{V})

taking
\bar{1}_{-\ell}
to
C
.
This extends to a strongly equivariant graded superfunctor

\widehat{\Omega}_{C}:\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell))\rightarrow K^{b}(\mathpzc{V})

by the universal property of
graded super Karoubi envelope.
Let
K^{b}(\widehat{\Omega}_{C}):K^{b}(\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)_{k}))\rightarrow K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})

be the graded superfunctor defined by applying

\widehat{\Omega}_{C}
to a complex in

K^{b}(\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)_{k}))
to obtain a double complex then taking the associated total complex.
Since
\widehat{\Omega}_{C}
is strongly equivariant,
we have that

(15.2)

K^{b}(\widehat{\Omega}_{C})\circ(\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)))(Z)\circ\operatorname{inc}\simeq\mathpzc{V}(Z)\circ\widehat{\Omega}_{C},

where
\operatorname{inc}:\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell))\rightarrow K^{b}(\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell))
is the canonical graded superfunctor
sending objects to complexes concentrated in cohomological degree 0.
By
corollary

15.4
,

(\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)))(Z)=0

in
K^{b}(\GSKar(\mathpzc{V}(-\ell)_{k}))
,
hence, the graded superfunctor on the left hand side of
eq.

15.2

takes
E^{n}\bar{1}_{-\ell}
to 0.
So the graded superfunctor on the right hand side takes

E^{n}\bar{1}_{-\ell}
to 0 too.
Since we have that

\widehat{\Omega}_{C}(E^{n}\bar{1}_{-\ell})\simeq E^{n}C
,
it follows that

\mathpzc{V}(Z)(E^{n}C)=0
as required.

To complete the proof,
we let
\mathpzc{V}(Z)^{\vee}
be a right adjoint to

\mathpzc{V}(Z):K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})\rightarrow K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})
, which exists by the general
discussion in
[
CR
, Sec. 4.1.4]
again.
We must show that
\mathpzc{V}(Z)(D)=0
for any

D\in K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})
, which we do by showing that

\mathpzc{V}(Z)^{\vee}\big(\mathpzc{V}(Z)(D)\big)=0
;
this is sufficient since
it implies that
\End_{K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})}\big(\mathpzc{V}(Z)(D)\big)=0
.
Using
lemma

14.11
, we just need to show that

\Hom_{K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})}\Big(E^{n}C,\mathpzc{V}(Z)^{\vee}\big(\mathpzc{V}(Z)(D)\big)\Big)=0

for
C
and
n
as in the previous paragraph.
This follows because by adjunction we have that

\Hom_{K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})}\Big(E^{n}C,\mathpzc{V}(Z)^{\vee}\big(\mathpzc{V}(Z)(D)\big)\Big)\simeq\Hom_{K^{b}(\mathpzc{V}_{k})}\big(\mathpzc{V}(Z)(E^{n}C),\mathpzc{V}(Z)(D)\big)

which is zero by the previous paragraph.
∎

16.
Application to representations of spin symmetric groups

theorem

15.5
can be applied to obtain graded superequivalences
between homotopy/derived categories of
supermodules over the
cyclotomic quiver Hecke superalgebras from
[
KKT
,
KKO1
,
KKO2
]
.
In explaining this, we will mainly cite
[
KKO2
, Sec. 8]
which presents the results needed to do this
rather concisely. However,
we need to
reverse the roles of
E
and
F

compared to
[
KKO2
]
to be consistent with our convention for
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})

in
section

13
, in which we preferred lowest weight modules
to highest weight modules.

Fix a Cartan superdatum
(A,P,\Pi,\Pi^{\vee})
as in
[
KKO2
, Sec. 4.1]
. So:

•

I
is an index set
with given decomposition
I=I_{\operatorname{even}}\sqcup I_{\operatorname{odd}}
;

•

A=(a_{i,j})_{i,j\in I}
is a symmetrizable
Cartan matrix such that
a_{i,j}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
for all
i\in I_{\operatorname{odd}},j\in I
;

•

P
is the weight lattice;

•

\Pi=\{\alpha_{i}\>|\>i\in I\}
is the set of simple roots;

•

\Pi^{\vee}=\{h_{i}\>|\>i\in I\}
is the set of simple coroots.

Let
d_{i}\>(i\in I)
be positive integers chosen
so that
d_{i}a_{i,j}=d_{j}a_{j,i}
for all
i,j\in I
.
Let
P^{+}
be the corresponding set of dominant weights
and
Q^{+}:=\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathbb{N}\alpha_{i}
be the non-negative
part of the root lattice.
Finally, let
W<\Aut(P)
be the Weyl group.

Let
\Bbbk=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}\Bbbk_{d}
be a positively graded commutative ground ring with
\Bbbk_{0}=\mathbb{F}
(our usual algebraically closed ground field) and
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\Bbbk_{d}<\infty
for all
d
. We view
\Bbbk
as a purely even
graded
\mathbb{F}
-superalgebra.
Given any
\alpha\in Q^{+}
, there is a corresponding
quiver Hecke superalgebra

R_{\alpha}
which is defined by generators and relations
as in
[
KKO2
, Sec. 8.1]
; the definition depends on an
additional choice of parameters as explained in
[
KKO2
]
.
Let
R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}
be the deformed cyclotomic quotient
from
[
KKO2
, Def. 8.10]
associated to a dominant weight

\lambda\in P^{+}
and a choice of
monic polynomials
a_{i}^{\lambda}\>(i\in I)
as in
[
KKO2
, (8.12)]
.
We are interested in the graded
(Q,\Pi)
-supercategory

(16.1)

R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}:=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in Q^{+}}R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}.

The constructions in
[
KKO2
, Sec. 8.3]
make
R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}

into a “supercategorification” of the
integrable lowest weight module
V(-\lambda)
for
the covering quantum group
U_{q,\pi}(\mathfrak{g})

with the given Cartan superdatum.
From this, it can be seen that

R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}
has the structure of a graded
2-representation of the corresponding graded Kac-Moody 2-supercategory
as defined in
[
BE2
]
, with the Grothendieck group

K_{0}(R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-pg\underline{smod}}})

being identified with the Kostant

\mathbb{Z}[q,q^{-1}]^{\pi}
-form for

V(-\lambda)
.

To be more precise, we focus now on some fixed
i\in I

and consider the corresponding
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-subalgebra
of
U_{q,\pi}(\mathfrak{g})
. In this generality,
we actually need to work now with
q_{i}:=q^{d_{i}}
and the grading shift functor

Q_{i}:=Q^{d_{i}}
rather than
q
and
Q
used in previous sections.
This means that when
d_{i}>1

definitions such as
definition

14.1

earlier in the paper
should be modified by replacing
Q
with
Q_{i}

and scaling all degrees by
d_{i}
too, e.g.,
x
and
\tau

are now of degrees
2d_{i}
and
-2d_{i}
rather than of degrees 2 and
-2
.
Since the
\mathbb{Z}
- and
\mathbb{Z}/2
-gradings are independent this does not cause any problems.
There are graded superfunctors

\displaystyle E_{i}

\displaystyle:R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}\rightarrow R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}},

\displaystyle F_{i}

\displaystyle:R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}\rightarrow R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}.

In terms of the induction and restriction functors denoted

F_{i}^{\lambda}
and
E_{i}^{\lambda}
in
[
KKO2
, Sec. 8.3]
,
our
E_{i}
is

F_{i}^{\lambda}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in Q_{+}}F_{i}^{\lambda}|_{R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}}

and our
F_{i}
is

\bigoplus_{\alpha\in Q^{+}}Q_{i}^{\langle h_{i},\alpha-\lambda\rangle-1}E_{i}^{\lambda}|_{R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}}
.
As well as switching the roles of
E
and
F

we have incorporated an additional grading shift
into the restriction functors compared to
[
KKO2
]
.
This is needed because
[
KKO2
]
does not follow the standard
conventions for covering quantum groups.
It ensures that the graded supernatural transformations

\varepsilon:E_{i}F_{i}|_{R^{\lambda}_{\alpha+\alpha_{i}}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}}\Rightarrow\Id_{R^{\lambda}_{\alpha+\alpha_{i}}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}}
,

\eta:\Id_{R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}}\Rightarrow F_{i}E_{i}|_{R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}}

defined on a graded supermodule by exactly the same underlying functions as for the natural adjunction between restriction and induction
are of the correct degree to match the degrees of the rightward cups and caps
in
eq.

13.9
(also now scaled by
d_{i}
).
Also in
[
KKO2
, Sec. 8.3]
, one finds the definition of graded supernatural transformations

x:E_{i}\Rightarrow E_{i}
of degree
2d_{i}
and

\tau:E_{i}^{2}\Rightarrow E_{i}^{2}

of degree
-2d_{i}
, both of which are even if
i\in I_{\operatorname{even}}

and odd if
i\in I_{\operatorname{odd}}
.
(A further complication is that the language of supercategory, superfunctor and supernatural transformation is used differently in
[
KKO2
]
compared to
here, but the appropriate translation is easy to make; see the table at the end of the introduction in
[
BE1
]
.)

This construction makes
R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}
into
a graded integrable Karoubian 2-representation of the ordinary
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}

2-category from
[
L1
,
R1
]

if
i
is even, or of our reduced odd
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
2-category

\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
as in
definition

14.1

if
i
is odd (with the modified convention for degrees when
d_{i}>1
).
The last statement is not stated explicitly in
[
KKO2
]
—the relevant place
is
[
KKO2
, Th. 8.13]
but one has to work through the proof which
goes back to
[
KK
, Th. 5.2]
to see that the isomorphisms are given by the
appropriate matrices of supernatural transformations needed to check
the difficult relations
eqs.

13.5
and
13.6
.
In the odd case,
the fact that the odd bubbles act as zero (as required by the final axiom in

definition

14.1
) follows
because they are zero on the generating lowest weight subcategory

R^{\lambda}_{0}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}
as that is purely even.

The following theorem now follows from
[
R1
, Th. 5.18]

if
i
is even, with the graded superequivalence being induced by the
even analog of the Rickard complex,
or our
theorem

15.5
if
i
is odd.

Theorem 16.1
.

In the above setup, for
\alpha\in Q^{+}
such that
V(-\lambda)_{\alpha-\lambda}\neq 0
, the even or odd Rickard complex
\Theta_{\langle h_{i},\lambda-\alpha\rangle}
induces a graded superequivalence

K^{b}\big(R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}\big)\rightarrow K^{b}\big(R^{\lambda}_{\alpha-\langle h_{i},\alpha-\lambda\rangle\alpha_{i}}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}\big)

categorifying the action of the
i
-th generator of the braid group
of
W
on
V(-\lambda)
.

There is also a dual version of this theorem
with
R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-pgsmod}}
replaced with

(16.2)

R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}:=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in Q^{+}}R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-gsmod}},

where for a graded superalgebra
A
we write

A{\operatorname{-gsmod}}
for the graded supercategory of
graded left
A
-supermodules that are finite-dimensional over the
ground field
\mathbb{F}
.
The underlying ordinary category
is a locally finite Abelian
(Q,\Pi)
-category.
The results from
[
KKO2
, Sec. 8.3]

show that this categorifies
the dual Kostant
\mathbb{Z}[q,q^{-1}]^{\pi}
-form
for
V(-\lambda)
.
For fixed
i\in I
again,

R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}

can be made into a graded
2-representation of the even or reduced odd
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
2-category
exactly as above.
Since we are now in an Abelian setting, it makes sense
to consider
D^{b}(R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-gsmod}})
,
the
graded super analog of the usual bounded derived category
of an Abelian category. Formally, this is defined to be the localization of
K^{b}(R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-gsmod}})
at even degree 0 quasi-isomorphisms.
The graded superequivalence of homotopy categories
obtained as in
[
R1
, Th. 5.18]
, or our
theorem

15.5
in the case that
i
is odd, immediately implies the
following.

Theorem 16.2
.

In the above setup, for
\alpha\in Q^{+}
such that
V(-\lambda)_{\alpha-\lambda}\neq 0
,
the even or
odd Rickard complex
\Theta_{\langle h_{i},\lambda-\alpha\rangle}

induces a graded superequivalence

D^{b}\big(R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}\big)\rightarrow D^{b}\big(R^{\lambda}_{\alpha-\langle h_{i},\alpha-\lambda\rangle\alpha_{i}}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}\big)

with quasi-inverse induced by the right adjoint

\Theta^{\langle h_{i},\lambda-\alpha\rangle}
of this complex.

For a graded superalgebra
A
, we write
A\otimes C_{1}
for the graded
superalgebra obtained by tensoring with the rank one Clifford superalgebra
generated by an odd degree 0 involution.
There is also a variation of
theorem

16.2
with

R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}
replaced by

(16.3)

R^{\lambda}\otimes C_{1}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}:=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in Q^{+}}R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}\otimes C_{1}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}.

This can be made into a graded 2-representation which also categorifies
the dual Kostant
\mathbb{Z}[q,q^{-1}]^{\pi}
-form
for
V(-\lambda)
, just as
R^{\lambda}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}
did earlier.
In particular, for each
i\in I
,
we can make

R^{\lambda}\otimes C_{1}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}
into a graded
2-representation of the even or reduced odd
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
2-category
exactly as above. This follows by the construction explained in the next paragraph.

There is a general notion of the

Clifford twist

\mathpzc{A}^{\mathtt{CT}}
of a graded
supercategory
\mathpzc{A}
, which goes back to
[
KKT
, Lem. 2.3]
.
By definition, this is the graded supercategory
whose objects are pairs
(X,\phi)
for
X\in\mathpzc{A}
and an
odd degree 0 involution
\phi\in\End_{\mathpzc{A}}(X)
.
A morphism
f:(X,\phi)\rightarrow(Y,\theta)

is a morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathpzc{A}
such that

\theta\circ f=(-1)^{\operatorname{par}(f)}\phi\circ f
.
Degree and parity of morphisms in
\mathpzc{A}^{\mathtt{CT}}
are induced by the ones for
\mathpzc{A}
.
There are obvious ways to define the
Clifford twist
F^{\mathtt{CT}}:\mathpzc{A}^{\mathtt{CT}}\rightarrow\mathpzc{B}^{\mathtt{CT}}

of a graded superfunctor
F:\mathpzc{A}\rightarrow\mathpzc{B}
,
and also the Clifford twist
\alpha^{\mathtt{CT}}:F^{\mathtt{CT}}\Rightarrow G^{\mathtt{CT}}

of a graded supernatural transformation
\alpha:F\Rightarrow G

between two graded superfunctors.
This makes
{\mathtt{CT}}
into a strict graded 2-superfunctor

{\mathtt{CT}}:\mathpzc{gsCat}\rightarrow\mathpzc{gsCat}
.
Now if
\mathpzc{V}
is any graded 2-representation of the even or the reduced odd
2-supercategory
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
, its Clifford twist
\mathpzc{V}^{\mathtt{CT}}
can be made into a graded 2-representation
in its own right, with the required
graded superfunctors
E
and
F
on
\mathpzc{V}^{\mathtt{CT}}
being the Clifford twists of the ones
for
\mathpzc{V}
, and all of the required graded supernatural transformations being the Clifford twists of the one for
\mathpzc{V}
too. If
\mathpzc{V}
is integrable and Karoubian then so is
\mathpzc{V}^{\mathtt{CT}}
.

Now the same general machinery used to prove
theorem

16.2
gives the following variation.

Theorem 16.3
.

In the above setup, for
\alpha\in Q^{+}
such that
V(-\lambda)_{\alpha-\lambda}\neq 0
,
the even or
odd Rickard complex
\Theta_{\langle h_{i},\lambda-\alpha\rangle}

induces a graded superequivalence

D^{b}\big(R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}\otimes C_{1}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}\big)\rightarrow D^{b}\big(R^{\lambda}_{\alpha-\langle h_{i},\alpha-\lambda\rangle\alpha_{i}}\otimes C_{1}{\operatorname{-gsmod}}\big)

with quasi-inverse induced by the right adjoint

\Theta^{\langle h_{i},\lambda-\alpha\rangle}
of this complex.

Assume henceforth that
the ground field
\mathbb{F}
is algebraically closed of odd characteristic
p=2l+1
,
and that the Cartan superdatum fixed above is of type

A_{2l}^{(2)}
, with the shortest simple root
\alpha_{0}
being odd and all other simple roots being even.
We consider the cyclotomic quiver Hecke superalgebras
R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}

for
\alpha\in Q_{+}
and
\lambda:=\Lambda_{0}
,
taking the ring
\Bbbk

to be the ground field
\mathbb{F}
, and all other choices made as
explained in
[
KLi
, Sec. 3.1]
.
Let

R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}\otimes C_{1}

be the superalgebra tensor product of
R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}
with the
rank one Clifford superalgebra generated by an odd involution.
Now we forget both the
\mathbb{Z}
- and
\mathbb{Z}/2
-gradings
on
R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}
and
R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}\otimes C_{1}

to view them as ordinary finite-dimensional algebras.
For such an algebra
A
, we write
A{\operatorname{-mod}}
for the Abelian category
of finite-dimensional left
A
-modules and
D^{b}(A{\operatorname{-mod}})
for its ordinary
bounded derived category.

In view of
[
KLi
, Lem. 3.1.39]
,
the following proves
[
KLi
, Conj. 2]
.

Theorem 16.4
.

Suppose that
\alpha,\beta\in Q^{+}
are
such that
\alpha-\lambda
and
\beta-\lambda
are weights of

V(-\lambda)
in the same
W
-orbit.
The categories

D^{b}\big(R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{-mod}}\big)

and

D^{b}\big(R^{\lambda}_{\beta}{\operatorname{-mod}}\big)
are equivalent
as are
D^{b}\big(R^{\lambda}_{\alpha}\otimes C_{1}{\operatorname{-mod}}\big)

and

D^{b}\big(R^{\lambda}_{\beta}\otimes C_{1}{\operatorname{-mod}}\big)
.

Proof.

Since the simple reflections generate
W
,
it suffices to prove the theorem in the special case that

\alpha-\lambda
is a weight of
V(-\lambda)

and
\beta=\alpha-\langle h_{i},\alpha-\lambda\rangle\alpha_{i}
for some
i\in I
.
The graded superequivalences in
theorems

16.2
and
16.3
are obtained by taking the derived tensor product
with the complex of graded superbimodules
arising from the appropriate Rickard complex.
Similarly, the quasi-inverse graded superequivalences are obtained from the
right adjoint of this complex. Now we are forgetting both the
\mathbb{Z}
- and
\mathbb{Z}/2
-gradings,
viewing these complexes of graded superbimodules as complexes of ordinary bimodules.
The resulting complexes define functors
between the ordinary derived categories.
Since they are quasi-inverse with all gradings present, they are obviously quasi-inverse without these gradings.
∎

Corollary 16.5
.

Broué’s Abelian Defect Group Conjecture holds for double covers of symmetric and alternating groups over any algebraically closed field of positive characteristic.

Proof.

See
[
KLi
, Th. 5.4.12]
where this is deduced from
[
KLi
, Conj. 2]
.
∎
